# Server

`server/Server` is the game server: the local server a player runs on their own PC, and the code of the community server. Tests: `server/Server.Tests`. One SQLite database per server ([ADR 0001](adr/0001-sqlite-for-profiles.md)).

At this stage it answers the hunt itself (connect, heartbeat, kills, harvest, score, purchases, expeditions, multiplayer sessions, chat notices, photo upload) from a player state, and answers everything else from what the operator supplies: the game's install archives, recorded replies and a mirror folder. It needs no recordings to start: the player state is built from the operator's own install and the facts file ([Starting data](#starting-data)). The current game build (one web host, signed tokens) is described in [The current build](#the-current-build).

## Run it

```
dotnet run --project server/Server -c Release -- [--settings <file>] serve | admin ... | import backup|pictures ... | review ... | claim ... | coverage [--rules] | backup <folder> [keep]
```

`serve` is the default. The settings file is `--settings <file>`, else the environment variable `SERVER_SETTINGS`, else `settings.json` in the current folder. It is never committed (`.gitignore`); start from `server/Server/settings.example.json`. Relative paths are relative to the settings file.

| Key | Meaning |
|---|---|
| `web_host` | Required. **Our website's public domain**: the sign-in pages and the account pages are served under it (the website forwards the browser's `Host`, and the Steam return address is built from it) |
| `nat_host` | Optional. The name players reach the punch-through server by (UDP 10000); default `web_host`. Set it when `web_host` is proxied by Cloudflare, which does not carry UDP: use a DNS-only name that points at the VPS |
| `game_web_host` | Required. **The game's own web host**: the one name the current build sends everything to ([The current build](#the-current-build)). It is not our domain: a player's hosts file points it to their PC, and the request reaches the server in the `host_header` header |
| `crash_host` | The host the game's crash reports go to (answered 204). Recommended |
| `gameserver_host`, `api_host`, `apiv2_host`, `signup_host`, `static_host`, `static_legacy_host`, `map_host`, `photo_host`, `avatar_host`, `news_host`, `telemetry_host` | The names the old build asked for; only needed for it, and to find the recordings made of them. Left out, each is a placeholder (`<role>.invalid`) that no client asks for; `map_host` is also needed by the current build, to fill map tiles from the map host's folder. The organ and lodge picture fallback reads the mirror under `static_host`, so a server without `static_host` cannot fill those pictures from the mirror and serves only what the archive, a recorded reply or the game web host's own mirror folder holds. The server routes by the `Host` of a request |
| `install_dir` | Required. The game's install folder: its archives answer image and map requests (`game/archives`) and hold the item and species definitions, and its sample files (`game/json_connect_results.json`, `game/json_inventory.json`) seed the connect reply and the item definitions ([Starting data](#starting-data)). Read only |
| `data_dir` | Default `data`: `state.db`, `spawn.json`, `named_animals.json` (with `spawn_named_animals`: the population ids given to named animals; `backup` copies only the `.db` files, so keep this file: ids given again could make a stored harvest count for another animal, [Named animals](#missions)), `photos/<account id>/`, `reports/<account id>/` ([Problem reports](#problem-reports)), the miss log `misses.jsonl` and `telemetry.jsonl` ([Crash reports](#crash-reports)) |
| `recordings_dir` | Default `recordings`: Optional: HAR files the operator recorded of their own traffic. The newest 200 reply per request wins. Beside them, `mission_rules.json` states the rules of single mission objectives ([Overridden rules](#missions)) |
| `mirror_dir` | Default `mirror`: saved static pages, `<mirror_dir>/<host>/<path>` |
| `images_dir` | Default `images`: the operator's pictures, `<images_dir>/<file>`. The game's avatars are read from it as `avatar-<id>.png`, else the stock copy `avatars/256/<id>.png` ([Avatars](#avatars)); the website reads the same folder for its slots ([images.md](images.md)) |
| `variants_file` | Optional: a JSON list of `{id, min_score, max_score, css_multiplier}` per trophy variant for the score sheets; its rows win over the facts file's `variants`, which hold the bounds of every scored species ([Scoring and paying a harvest](#scoring-and-paying-a-harvest)), so the file is only needed to change them |
| `offline_mode` | Default `true`: a request that no token resolves is the local account's (a player's own PC). `false` on a community server: it gets the reply of an expired token, see [Accounts](#accounts) |
| `all_licenses` | Default `true`: every player hunts with an unlimited license, see [Licenses](#licenses). `false`: licenses are items like any other |
| `unlimited_ammo` | Default `true`: every player has unlimited ammunition, see [Unlimited ammunition](#unlimited-ammunition). `false`: ammunition is an item like any other. Independent of `all_licenses` |
| `unlimited_fuel` | Default `true`: every player has unlimited vehicle fuel and none is sold, see [Unlimited fuel](#unlimited-fuel). `false`: fuel is an item like any other. Independent of `unlimited_ammo` |
| `spawn_named_animals` | Default `false` (the live server runs it on): the hunt's reply places the animals that named mission objectives ask for ([Named animals](#missions)). Tested in the game with single animals; a placed group of several animals (an operator's `N animals`) is newer and not yet seen in the game |
| `harvest_gm_bonus` | Optional: a list of periods, `[{"multiplier": 2, "from": "2026-11-27T15:00:00Z", "until": "2026-11-30T07:00:00Z", "name": "Harvest gm$ x2 weekend"}]` (`multiplier` a number above 0, `from` and `until` ISO times, no zone means UTC, `from` before `until`; `name` optional; `from` optional: the period starts when the setting is read). From `from` until `until` every harvest pays `multiplier` times its gm$, see [Harvest gm$ bonus](#harvest-gm-bonus). Periods must not overlap (one may start where another ends; one with no `from` counts from the time it is read, and one already over is not checked). Today's single `{"multiplier": 2, "until": "..."}` still works. Unset, empty or past: no bonus. Read at start and again whenever the file changes, with no restart |
| `store_sales` | Optional: a list of store sales, `[{"name": "Black Friday", "from": "2026-11-27T15:00:00Z", "until": "2026-11-30T07:00:00Z", "percent": 25, "currencies": ["em", "gm"], "items": [123, 456]}]`, fixed (`items`, `bundles`) or random (`random: {count, percent, every, categories, min_price_em, max_price_em, min_price_gm, max_price_gm, exclude}`), alone or tied to an `event`, see [Store sales](#store-sales). Unset or empty: nothing is on sale (the recorded `Application/discounts` of the original is not read). Read at start and again whenever the file changes, with no restart |
| `referral` | Optional: the rules and rewards of the referral codes, `{"qualify_hours": 2, "qualify_score": 10, "weekly_cap": 5, "enter_hours": 72, "existing_days": 31, "recruit_reward": null, "tiers": [{"recruits": 1, "reward": null}], "discord_milestones": [{"recruits": 1, "reward": null}]}`; a reward is `{"gm": 0, "em": 0, "items": [{"id": 384, "count": 1}]}`. Every key is optional; absent or all rewards `null`: the system works and pays nothing. See [Referrals](#referrals). Read at start and again whenever the file changes, with no restart |
| `default_inventory_capacity` | Default `20000` (grams, a whole number, at least 1): the backpack capacity of every account, 20 units. The original gave 10000; this is a community-server change. The owned Increased Capacity upgrade (item 92) adds its 10000 on top: 30 units. See [Backpack capacity](#backpack-capacity) |
| `link_discord` | Optional: an `https` invite URL of the operator's Discord; the `LINK_DISCORD` environment variable (set by the deploy from the repository secret, [deploy.md](deploy.md#deploys)) wins over it. When set, the launcher's start page when signed in shows "Join the Discord: news, help and the operator" as a link to it (the website's confirm and "Done" pages show the same line from its own `LINK_DISCORD`). Unset, empty or not `https`: nothing is shown |
| `review_webhook` | Optional: an `https` URL of a Discord-style webhook. When a zip is kept for review ([Upload a backup zip](#upload-a-backup-zip)) or an archive claim waits ([Archive claims](#archive-claims)) the server posts `{"content": "..."}` there. The same webhook gets the alerts for a refused upload and a problem report. Every alert has the layout and fields of [Discord alerts](#discord-alerts), nothing else. Unset or empty: no alert. An `http` or malformed URL is logged once and turns it off. A failed post is logged and never delays the upload |
| `upload_account_files` | Default `3`, at least `1`: how many zips one account may have stored for the operator at once: the ones waiting for review plus the refused ones kept under `refused/` ([Upload a backup zip](#upload-a-backup-zip)). Restored zips do not count. A new upload over it is refused at `restore/start` (status `quota`) |
| `upload_account_bytes` | Default `1073741824` (1 GB), at least `1`: the most bytes of stored zips (with their picture files) one account may have for the operator. An upload that would pass it is refused at the start, or as soon as the bytes received pass what is left (status `quota`) |
| `upload_total_bytes` | Default `5368709120` (5 GB), at least `1`: the most bytes of stored zips on the whole server. Over it, uploads are refused at the start and during the upload (status `paused`) |
| `upload_min_free_bytes` | Default `10737418240` (10 GB), `0` or more: uploads are refused (status `paused`) when the data disk (`data_dir`) has less than this free, counting the zip being received |
| `news_webhook` | Optional: an `https` URL of the webhook of the announcements channel. `admin news add --discord` posts the new post there ([News](#news)). Unset: `--discord` is refused. |
| `discord_client_id`, `discord_client_secret` | Optional, both or neither: the Discord application's id (digits only; also its application id) and its client secret, for [Discord Linked Roles](#discord-linked-roles). The secret is the operator's, never committed ([deploy.md](deploy.md#discord-linked-roles)). Unset: `/discord/link` and `/discord/callback` are not served. |
| `discord_role_bot_token`, `discord_guild_id`, `discord_role_verified`, `discord_role_veteran` | Optional: the role bot's token (a second Discord application's bot with Manage Roles only), the Discord server's id and the ids of its "Verified hunter" and "Veteran" roles (ids: digits only), for [the roles](#discord-linked-roles). The token is the operator's, never committed ([deploy.md](deploy.md#discord-linked-roles)). The server gives the roles only when all four are set (and `discord_client_id` and `discord_client_secret`, online); with any one unset or empty it never calls the Discord server, and when some are set it says so once at start (`discord roles: off until ...`). |
| `discord_veteran_at` | Default `20000`, `0` to `10000000`: the HunterScore from which the role bot gives "Veteran". The one source of the threshold: `GET /auth/me` says it (`discordVeteranAt`), and the website's result page reads it from there ([website.md](website.md#discord-linked-roles)). |
| `steam_web_api_key`, `steam_app_id` | Optional, both or neither: a Steam Web API key (the operator's own, [steamcommunity.com/dev/apikey](https://steamcommunity.com/dev/apikey); never committed) and the game's Steam app id (digits only), for the [Steam licence](#steam-licence) check. Unset: `GET /auth/me` says `steamLicence: "unknown"` and Steam is never called. |
| `signup_webhook` | Optional: an `https` URL of a Discord-style webhook, like `review_webhook`. The server posts `{"content": "..."}` there when a new account signs up (its handle, account id, Steam link, and `fresh start` or what its "I played before" claim came to) and when a claim made on the website is restored at once (account id, handles, hunterscore, Steam link), in the same [alert layout](#discord-alerts). Claims that wait for review use `review_webhook`; the operator's `claim` and approve send nothing here. Unset or empty: no alert. An `http` or malformed URL is logged once and turns it off |
| `picture_link_hosts` | Optional: a list of host names (no scheme, port, path or IP address) a backup's gallery links may be fetched from, such as the original photo host ([Pictures from links](restore.md#pictures)). Exact name match; default none, so no link is fetched. When an upload's zip lists a gallery picture it does not hold, the server fetches it from such a host at upload time and keeps it with the zip; only https links on the default port are fetched, at most 16 MiB each (1 GiB a save) and JPEG only, a redirect only to a listed host, never an address that is not public |
| `avatar_uploads_dir` | Default `avatar-uploads`: the operator's saved copies of the players' uploaded avatars, `<avatar_uploads_dir>/242/<uid>.jpg` (the 242 pixel thumbnail of a user id). A restore reads the thumbnail of the link's uid here first, and asks `avatar_uploads` only when there is no file (or it is not a JPEG); with a copy, `picture_link_hosts` is not needed for it. See [Uploaded avatars](restore.md#pictures) |
| `avatar_uploads` | Optional: an `https` base URL (no credentials, query or fragment) the uploaded avatars are fetched from by user id: `<avatar_uploads>/uploads/thumb/242x242/<uid>.jpg` ([Uploaded avatars](restore.md#pictures)). Default: the base of the game's avatar list, which on a community server is often the dead website host, so set it to the host that still serves the thumbnails. Its host must also be in `picture_link_hosts`, or nothing is fetched |
| `archive_db` | Default `<data_dir>/archive.db`: the operator's archive of public profiles that players can claim ([Archive claims](#archive-claims)). Opened read only. No file there: claims are off. A file at the top of `data_dir` is copied by `backup` every day; on the community server it lives in `data_dir/archive/` ([deploy.md](deploy.md#backups)) |
| `mission_texts_dir` | Default `<data_dir>/mission-texts`: the missions' texts per language ([Mission texts](#mission-texts)), game text that is not in the repository. Read at the start. A missing folder or file, or one that is not a flat JSON object of texts, means English for it (broken JSON, a duplicate key and a text that is no string included): `serve` logs one line for each and one for what it loaded, the admin commands none, and nothing stops the start. `mission-texts check` reports such a file as a problem |
| `listeners` | Required: a list of `{address, port}`; add `cert` and `key` (PEM files) for HTTPS. Plain HTTP is a listener without them. `"admin": true` (loopback addresses only) makes it serve the admin API, see below. Every listener speaks HTTP/1.1 only |
| `geoip_file` | Optional: the path of an offline city-level IP database in MMDB format (DB-IP "IP to City Lite", free, CC BY 4.0), read for the multiplayer list's distance dots ([How far a host is](#how-far-a-host-is)). Not in the repository and never downloaded by the server. Unset, missing or not an MMDB file: no dots, and the start says so once (`distance dots: off (...)`) |
| `trusted_proxies` | Addresses or `address/prefix` of the proxies in front of the server (default none) |
| `host_header` | Default `X-Game-Host`, see below |
| `event` | Optional: the seasonal event the connect reply announces while its window runs, `{name, define, reserves, start, end}` ([Seasonal events](#seasonal-events)); none by default |
| `event_community_divisor` | Default 20, a whole number of at least 1: the event's community missions need `ceil(number / divisor)` ([Seasonal events](#seasonal-events)) |
| `game_build` | Optional, a whole number of at least 1: the Steam build id of the game version the server supports. `GET /stats/game` answers `{"build": n}` (`null` when unset, and the launcher then skips its version check). `settings.example.json` names the supported build, and the launcher's table of clean DLLs (`CleanDlls`, docs/launcher.md#the-install-check) is of that build, which `InstallChecksTests` checks: a new build is a new table. The launcher does not judge the DLLs of a game of another build |
| `playtime_since` | Default `2026-11-12T00:00:00Z`, an ISO time (no zone means UTC): the day the server opened. A hunt that started before it is no server playtime and is left out of the hunting statistics (an operator's test), see [Server playtime and hunting statistics](#server-playtime-and-hunting-statistics) |
| `max_body_bytes` | Default 16 MiB: the request body limit. The admin API's is 64 KiB |

`serve` needs no change to the PC: it does not read or require the Windows hosts file. How the client is pointed at the server is not part of the server.

`admin rescore [--account <id or handle>] [--kill <id>] [--apply]` (below), `admin item <item_id> [count]`, `admin em|gm <amount>` and `admin spawn <reserve> <animal> <males> [females] [x z radius]` change the player in `data_dir` from the command line (add `--account <user id or handle>` to `admin item|em|gm` for another account, e.g. `docker compose exec server dotnet server.dll admin item <item_id> <count> --account <handle>`; the admin API's account routes take the same as the `account` query parameter; without it the local account); `admin ethics [--account <id or handle>] [--apply]` judges the stored kills again by the ammunition rule and `admin ethics list` lists the kills it flagged ([The ammunition rule on stored kills](#the-ammunition-rule-on-stored-kills)); `admin mp-harvests [--apply]` moves the harvests of past multiplayer games to the player who shot the animal ([Multiplayer](#multiplayer)), `admin missions-rollback [--apply] [--account <user id or handle>]` reopens mission objectives the earlier rules finished without their conditions, and `admin missions-audit [--hours N] [--account <user id or handle>] [--discord]` lists the recent mission completions that look suspicious, and `admin missions-stuck [--days N] [--account <user id or handle>] [--discord]` the open objectives players attempt and nobody completes ([Missions](#missions)); `coverage` replays every request of the recordings through the server and lists what it cannot answer, naming hosts by their settings key and masking ids and tokens in paths, then reports how many mission objectives the server understands ([Missions](#missions)). `coverage --no-recordings` replays them against the server as it starts without recordings and without the mirror (player state from the install and the facts file, on a temporary database): what it lists as unanswered still needs a recording.

`admin ban <user id, handle or Steam id> [reason]` bans a Steam id for good (no sign-in with it, whether or not the account was deleted since; the account it has now gets `banned`) and `admin unban <Steam id>` lifts it; the admin API has the same (`POST /admin/ban`, `POST /admin/unban`), see [Archive claims](#archive-claims).

`admin message --account <user id or handle> "<text>"` and `admin message --all [--yes] "<text>"` write an inbox message from the server to one player or to everyone, `admin messages [--account <user id or handle>] [--sent]` lists the players' replies or what was sent; the admin API has `POST /admin/accounts/<account>/message` and `POST /admin/message`, see [Messages](#messages).

`admin backfill-lodges [--apply] [--account <user id or handle>]` fills the lodges of the accounts that a section save restored before `lodge_contents` was read as lodges ([Restore](restore.md)): that save's contents sit whole in the account's `extras` (`json_lodge_contents.json`) and the lodge list names the lodges, but no `/game/user/lodge` row exists, so `GET /game/user/<uid>/lodges/<id>` has no answer of its own and the game does not open the lodge. For each listed lodge without contents it writes the saved contents as that row (the animals it mounts are shown as trophies when read, as for any restored lodge). It prints one line per account and lodge, `account <id> lodge <id>: would add|added, N slots`; a lodge that has contents is never touched, so a second run adds nothing, and without `--apply` nothing is written (take a `backup` before `--apply`).

`admin ammo-fit [--apply] [--account <user id or handle>]` (#1009; once, with the server stopped, after `backup`) finds the ammunition stacks that sit in an equipped ammunition slot (8 to 10) beside a weapon (the slot 6 below) that does not fit them, as a restore could leave them ([Restore](restore.md), [Equipped ammunition](#equipped-ammunition-must-fit-its-weapon)), and moves each, whole, to the lodge (slot 0): nothing is lost, the player can equip it under a weapon it fits. A stack beside two weapons in one slot (a restore's merge could leave two) is listed once, when one of them refuses it. It prints one line per stack, `account <id> slot <n>: weapon <id> (<name>), ammunition <id> (<name>) stack <guid>: would move|moved to the lodge`, then a total line. Without `--apply` nothing is written; a second `--apply` finds none. Only stacks move: two weapons in one slot stay as they are.

`admin event add <name> <start> <end> [--kind seasonal]`, `admin event list` and `admin backfill-rewards --events <file> [--apply]` write and fill the events log and the base of every payment ([Reward bases and events](#reward-bases-and-events), with the exact file and commands for the ×2 weekend and the ×1.25 bonus).

A reserve takes at most 20 new animal kinds from `admin spawn`; further entries of that reserve are not applied.

`backup <folder> [keep]` copies every `*.db` of `data_dir` to `<folder>/<name>-<yyyymmdd>.db` with SQLite's `VACUUM INTO` (consistent while the server runs) and keeps the newest `keep` (default 7) copies of each; the community server's daily backup is this command ([deploy.md](deploy.md#backups)).

## How a request is answered

1. A request for `telemetry_host` or `crash_host` gets 204. With player state, the crash host's fatal and error reports are kept per account ([Crash reports](#crash-reports)) and everything else it sends is dropped; the telemetry host's requests (and the crash host's, without player state) are kept in `telemetry.jsonl`.
2. The player state answers what it owns: `heartbeat`, `heartbeatScenario`, `disconnect`, `connect`, the photo upload and photo files on the game host; `Inventory/move_multiple2`, `Inventory/buy`, `Launch/start`, `Launch/start_mp`, `Launch/join`, `Network/list` ([Multiplayer](#multiplayer)), `session/start_tutorial`, `User/jwt`, `Oauth/token`, `Chat/conversations`, `Chat/conversation`, `Skill/list`, `Statistics/total`, `Achievement/stats` (the last three for a restored account's own user id, see [Import a backup zip](#import-a-backup-zip); on a community server `Achievement/stats` and `Statistics/total` also for the user id of a player the caller may see, see [Public reads](#public-reads)), the lists that wrap a base reply (`Splash/list_active`, `Public_user/expedition`, `Expedition/list`, `Me/poll`, `Me/me`: the recorded reply on a local server, the empty shape on a community server; `Public_user/expedition` and `Expedition/list` also for the user id of a player the caller may see, see [Public reads](#public-reads)) and, on a community server, `Inventory/list`, `Inventory/dict`, `Trophy/list`, `Gallery/list`, `Gallery/delete` ([Gallery](#gallery)), `Wallet/transactions`, `Wallet/silver_transactions` and `Wallet/spent` ([Wallet history](#wallet-history)), `Mission/missions` and the page's mission writes ([Missions](#missions)) and the friends calls ([Friends](#friends)) on the API host; the harvest pages, the trophies, the lodges and the edits of lodges and trophies on the API v2 host ([Lodges](#lodges-and-trophy-visibility)); the sign-in and start page on the website and signup hosts, and the old launcher's own calls on the game host ([Sign-in and tokens](#sign-in-and-tokens)); the kill details page on the website host. `Inventory/buy` accepts amounts of 1 to 1000; any other amount gets `success: false` and costs nothing.
3. Everything else, in this order: an archive file in the URL's own folder, the recorded reply (a JSONP callback is swapped; on a community server only for a global endpoint, [below](#recordings-on-a-community-server)), the mirror, an archive file found by its name, an empty 200 for a request that was only recorded as unchanged, else a miss: `null` under `/v1/`, `{"err": 0}` on the game host, 404 on the API v2 host. Every miss carries `Cache-Control: no-store`, so the client's disk cache never keeps one and a later fix reaches players without a cache clear. A miss is written to the miss log, keys only.

The state answer wins; a handler that has nothing to say returns control to step 3. On a local server a reply is never made up where a recorded one exists.

## Recordings on a community server

A recording holds one player's replies, so with `offline_mode` false step 3 replays a recorded reply only for a **global** endpoint, one that is the same for everyone. Every other request on the API, API v2, game and website hosts is **per player**: the state answers it from the database, or it gets the empty reply of its shape, and a path the table does not list gets the miss reply (default deny). The table is `ReplayScope.Rules`; a test checks that no per-player rule ever returns recorded bytes. The asset hosts (static, map, photo, avatar, news, signup) and the mirror are not affected. With `offline_mode` true nothing changes: the recordings are the operator's own.

| Host | Global (recorded reply) | Per player, from the database | Per player, empty reply |
|---|---|---|---|
| API (`/v1/`) | `Application/application`, `Application/client_application`, `Store/products2`, `Splash/list_active`, `Splash/feed_data`, the two `get_active_stylesheet`, `Membership/get_type`, `Membership_Bundle/get_active`, `Avatar/getAvatars`, `Challenge/get_challenges`, `Sidebar_Counter/getTemplate` | `Application/discounts` (the same for everyone, but the operator's [store sales](#store-sales), never the recording), `Me/poll`, `Me/me` (every account: the restored profile, else a new account's; its `rank` is the account's place on the hunterscore board, see [The hunterscore](#the-hunterscore)), `Expedition/list` and `Public_user/expedition` (the user id of a player the caller may see, [Public reads](#public-reads)), `Inventory/list`, `Inventory/dict` (own user id only), `Trophy/list` (the restored trophies; the user id of a player the caller may see, without its hidden ones), `Gallery/list` (the user id of a player the caller may see, [Gallery](#gallery)), `Gallery/delete` (an own photo), `Wallet/transactions`, `Wallet/silver_transactions`, `Wallet/spent` (own user id only, [Wallet history](#wallet-history)), `Mission/missions`, `Mission/activate` and the page's other mission writes ([Missions](#missions)), `Challenge/get_user_challenges` (the user id of a player the caller may see, with a token, [Challenges](#challenges)), `Skill/list`, `Statistics/total` (the user id of a player the caller may see, [Public reads](#public-reads)), `Achievement/stats` (the same, [Public reads](#public-reads)), `Leaderboard/personal` (the same, [Public reads](#public-reads)), `Leaderboard/current_personal`, `Page_content/leaderboards_all`, `Page_content/leaderboard_details`, `Page_content/leaderboards_hall_of_fame` ([Leaderboards and seasons](#leaderboards-and-seasons)), `Chat/conversations`, `Chat/conversation`, `Inventory/buy`, `Inventory/move_multiple2`, `Launch/start`, `Launch/start_mp`, `Launch/join`, `Network/list` (the open multiplayer sessions, kept in memory, not the database: [Multiplayer](#multiplayer)), `User/jwt`, `Oauth/token`, `Public_user/getByHostname` (any account by handle, with a token, [Public reads](#public-reads)), `Me/friends`, `User/friendslist` (own user id only), `Friend/add`, `Friend/remove` ([Friends](#friends)) | another player's user id the caller may not see on the reads above; the bank history, missions, challenges, competitions, message settings, and the writes of these pages (answered with a failure) |
| API v2 | | the harvest pages (a server kill, else a restored harvest) and the purchase of a server kill's taxidermy ([Taxidermy](#taxidermy)), the account's trophies (`/game/user/trophies`: the restored ones, the animals mounted in the restored lodges, then every harvest made here that was taxidermized, in the restored row shape; a new kill id is above every restored id), the lodges of a player the caller may see (`/game/user/<id>/lodges[/<lodge>]`, [Lodges](#lodges-and-trophy-visibility)) and the player's own edits of a lodge and of a trophy's visibility (`PUT /game/user/lodges/<id>[/settings]`, `PUT /game/user/trophies/<id>`; also at `/v1/game/user/...`, the current build), the account's own loadouts (`GET /game/user/loadout`, `PUT /game/user/loadout/<id>`, [Loadouts](#loadouts)), the profile page header and the ranks of a player the caller may see, with a token (`/game/user/<id>/profile`, `/game/user/<id>/ranks`, [Public reads](#public-reads)) | any of the above the account has none of (empty list, or 404 for one record); `/game/user/backgrounds` and the antler boards (`/game/user/leaderboards/antlers/single/all` and `double`): an empty list |
| Game | the client's ping, `POST /` | heartbeat, heartbeatScenario, connect, disconnect | |
| Website | `static`, `content`, `dist`, `feed`, `media`, `/client`, `/launcher` (a community server answers the launcher page itself), the launcher ping | `/user/<handle>/score/` | every other path (the miss reply; a GET still finds a page of the operator's mirror folder) |

The website's `/v1/` paths are the API's, in any case. Empty replies are the shapes in the table (`[]`, `{}`, `null`, or an object with empty members). A new account's connect reply and `Me/me` carry its own ids, handle and statistics (its server hunts only, [The statistics](#the-statistics)), never the recorded player's. Another player's data is served only by the public reads ([Public reads](#public-reads)); the other reads of another player's user id are empty. Competitions are the operator's to decide.

## The current build

The current client build sends all its traffic to `game_web_host`, with lower-case paths and JSON bodies. `NewBuild.Rewrite` (before routing) turns a request for that host into the one the handlers know, so every handler, recording and rule below is the old one:

| Request on `game_web_host` | Becomes |
|---|---|
| `/v1/json/<service>/<method>` (the game's RPC: `connect`, `heartbeat`, `disconnect`) | the game server's path and host; the body stays JSON. Other `/v1/json/...` paths stay on the web host (the launcher's ping) |
| any other `/v1/<module>/<method>`, any case | the API host, the path in the case of the handlers (or the recordings'); a JSON body becomes a form (`a=1&b=2`, nested values as their JSON text), except the body of a PUT, which stays as sent (the lodge edits, [Lodges](#lodges-and-trophy-visibility)) |
| the photo upload path and the photo links | the game host, the same path; the body stays as sent |
| everything else (`/client`, `/static/...`, `/content/...`, `/media/...`) | unchanged: the archive, the recordings, the mirror (`<mirror_dir>/<game_web_host>/...`; map tiles also the map host's folder, below) answer it |

The server's own answers for this build:

| Path | Answer | Replay scope |
|---|---|---|
| `POST /auth/jwt/refresh` | A new access JWT (`exp` = now + 900 s) and refresh JWT, in the body (`expires_in`, always 63072000, not the JWT's 900 s; `access_token`, `refresh_token`) and the access and refresh cookies (the refresh one for the path `/auth/jwt/refresh` only). The credential is a refresh JWT this server signed (`typ` refresh: body `refresh_token` or the cookie); an access JWT, a session id or any other token gets 401. One exception, the game's photo uploader (it has no JWT of its own and sends no upload without one): `X-App-Id: client`, the refresh cookie text `refresh_token` and an `X-Hun-Session` that is a session (40 hex characters) that resolves; the refresh JWT it gets lasts 7 days, the session's lifetime (a later refresh with that JWT is an ordinary one, 30 days). Nothing valid: 401. A local server: the local account. `scp` is 4 for `X-App-Id: client`, else 1 | - |
| `POST /auth/emotelauncher/token` | Always 401 `auth.invalid_steam_ticket` (a Steam ticket cannot be checked, [above](#sign-in-and-tokens)): the launcher falls back to the page | - |
| `GET /launcher` | The start page ([above](#sign-in-and-tokens)): sign in, then `ewlauncher.startGame(<launch URL>)`. Signed in or not it also has "Open the new launcher" (`ewlauncher.openURL('hunter-resurgence://open')`, then `ewlauncher.exitLauncher()`; outside the old launcher it says to open the page there) and a "Download it" link to the latest release. A local server: the recorded page | global |
| `POST /v1/session/start_single_player` | `{session_id, network_id}` (the session is the first segment of the launch URL a launcher builds from it, and the game's `X-Hun-Session`) and the session and network id as cookies; opens the expedition at `lodge_id` in `reserve` | - |
| `POST /v1/session/start_tutorial` | `{oauth_access_token, tutorial_id, loadout_id}`: the reply and cookies of `start_single_player`, for the catalogue's tutorial `tutorial_id` (`Application/application` `tutorials`; 0 is 1; any other id: 400). It opens no expedition. The session's `connect` is a single-player connect in the tutorial's reserve whose `expedition` is `mode` 1, `tutorial` the id, `tutorial_data` the catalogue's tutorials, `expedition_id` 0, `lodge` `(reserve_id - 1) * 100`, `x`/`y`/`z` of the entry, `dir_x` 1000, `start_time` 720, `weather` 0.1. Until the account's next `start_single_player` or `Launch/start`, `Inventory/move_multiple2` is ignored (reply `null`: the tutorial's item numbers are not the account's). The account's latest tutorial session is kept in RAM: a restart makes its connect an ordinary hunt | - |
| `POST .../heartbeatScenario` (the game's RPC, like `heartbeat`; a tutorial's only event, 26) | `{"err": 0}`; nothing is stored. A tutorial also sends `GET .../ping`, which the game host's fallback answers `{"err": 0}` (a tutorial sends no `heartbeat` or `disconnect`) | - |
| `connect`, `heartbeat` | As before (they read `X-Hun-Session`, else `Authorization: Bearer`). The first heartbeat's `event 6` rows with quantity 0 use nothing up | - |
| `POST /v1/application/client_application` | The recorded reply, else the catalogue of the player state (species with their permitted ammo, items, reserves, locations; missions only if the recording has them) | global |
| `GET /v1/splash/feed_data` | `{posts, splashes}`: the recording with the server's notice first in `splashes`, else only the notice | global |
| `GET /v1/application/application` | The recorded reply (recordings are matched by method, so one of the current build's GET; none without a recording) | global |
| `GET /v1/game/user/loadout`, `PUT /v1/game/user/loadout/<id>` | The account's loadouts and their save ([Loadouts](#loadouts)); a local server answers the `GET` with `[]` | per player, empty reply |
| `POST /v1/harvest/<id>/taxidermize` (the taxidermy and the skull mount of a harvest; body `{currency: "gold"\|"silver", is_skull?}`, the JWT as `Authorization: Bearer`) | The API host's handler, the same as the old build's `POST /game/user/harvest/<id>/taxidermize` ([Taxidermy](#taxidermy)); a JSON body reaches it as a form, and both are read | per player, answered by the player state |
| `GET /v1/harvest/screen` (the confirm-kill page's question, with the JWT as `Authorization: Bearer`; added by the served `client.js`, below) | `{"taxidermy": false}` while the player's latest harvest counts for the session member who shot the animal, else `{"taxidermy": true}` ([Multiplayer › Harvests](#multiplayer)); the expired-token reply (HTTP 200 `{"errorCode": 5, "message": "token expired"}`) without a valid token | per player, answered by the player state |
| `POST /v1/mission/missions`, `mission/activate` and the page's other `mission/` writes | `{active, completed, available, states}`, all empty; `false` for the writes; a community server with recorded missions answers from the account's rows ([Missions](#missions)) | per player, empty reply |
| `POST /v1/Daily_Mission/get_daily_missions_calendar`, `Daily_Mission/activate_user_progress` | `{is_frozen: 1, broken_streak_info, calendar: []}`; `{"success": false}`; a community server with daily mission templates in the facts file answers from the account's streak ([Daily missions](#daily-missions)) | per player, empty reply |
| `POST` or `GET /v1/challenge/get_user_challenges` | `{}`; a community server answers from the account's rows, with a player's token, for a player the caller may see ([Challenges](#challenges)) | per player, empty reply |
| `POST /v1/network/list` | The open multiplayer sessions on a community server ([Multiplayer](#multiplayer)); a local server has none, so the recorded reply or the miss reply answers | per player, answered by the player state |
| `POST /v1/inventory/move_multiple2`, `inventory/buy`, `me/me`, `me/poll` (adds `active_campaign`, `campaign_stylesheet`, `community_campaign_stylesheet`, `last_hunt_update_ts`), `expedition/list` (`has_more` next to `total`) | The old handlers | per player, from the database |
| `/client`, `/static/...`, `/content/...`, `/media/...` | The mirror; the Confirm kill screen's `/content/animal_organs/<species id>/<male, female or organ id>.png` from the install's archive (`static/img/animal_organs/<species id>/`, that species' folder only), else the game host's own mirror folder, else the static host's copy in the mirror (outlines only), else a 404 (the screen skips that overlay); a map tile `/content/map/v7/tiles/<z>/<x>/<y>.jpg` (zoom 1 to 5, x and y below 2^z) that the game host's folder lacks is read from the map host's folder (`<mirror_dir>/<map_host>/v7/tiles/...`) | global |
| `/static/app/<version>/webgui/index.html`, `/dist/main.js` | The trophy lodge editor's frame (`client#lodge_edit`, opened with I inside a lodge) loads that page, and the page loads the script: the install's archive copies of the app's page and script (`webguiv2/index.html`, `webguiv2/dist/main.js`; the script from the mirror first, when it has one), for the game web host only. Without the page the editor shows its error and Resume game. The editor's server addresses are explained below | global |

**The lodge editor's addresses.** The editor builds its server addresses from the domain the game hands it (`api.`, `apiv2.` and `static.` names the player's PC does not send to this server), so its requests never arrived. Whatever answers `/dist/main.js`, the server replaces the three address builders with this host's own origin (`/v1` for the two API ones, the origin for the pictures), an exact text replacement on the served bytes (each text must be found exactly once, else the script is served unchanged and one warning is logged). The script and its frame page are served with `Cache-Control: no-cache`, and the page asks for the script as `main.js?t=urls2`. The page's reads of the lodge lists carry no token while the routes need the player's, so the frame page wraps `fetch` before the script runs, adding the game's own token to each request to this origin that has none. The editor asks for its pictures under `/static/img/`, which the game answers from its own archives alone (a request for a picture they lack never reaches this server), so the same replacement turns the script's lodge and item picture addresses into `/content/` ones (the gun racks' icons, which the script takes from a fixed host, too; its other `/static/img/` addresses stay): `/content/lodge/<animals, skulls, weapons, slots, styles, layouts or lodges>/<name>` is read from the archive (a file of that exact path), else from the static host's folder of the mirror, else, for a layout only (its name is in no other folder), from the archive's one file of that name.

**The lodge editor's game functions.** The editor calls the game's own functions (`window.hunter`: the language, the player's and the lodge's ids, `isStaff`, the inventory, label texts) as if each answered at once, but in the current build each answers with a promise, so the editor failed on its first label and stayed empty. The frame page (`/static/app/<version>/webgui/index.html`, whatever answers it) therefore no longer names the script in its own tag. When the language call answers with a promise, the page first waits for the answers the editor reads at once (`getServer`, `getActiveLodgeId`, `getHostPlayerGuid`, `getLocalPlayerGuid`, `isStaff`, `isSinglePlayer`, `getInventory`, `getLocale`) and for the locale script of that language (`/static/app/<version>/locale/<language>.js`, found from the page's own address), replaces `window.hunter` by an object that returns those answers, reads the label texts from the locale script (the key itself when it is not there, as the game does) and passes every other call on to the game, returning its promise (`editLodge`, `loadLodge`, `resumeGame` and the token calls, which the editor wraps in a promise itself); only then (or after 8 seconds, whatever has not answered by then) does it add the script, as `/dist/main.js?t=urls2`; a call that fails is not kept and is passed on like the others. The replacement is a proxy of the game's object, so every member stays reachable. The game's page asks the frame to go to the editor's route (`setURL`) as soon as the frame has loaded, which can be before the script is added, so the frame page defines `setURL` at once as a stand-in that keeps the route; when the script has loaded, its own `setURL` is called with it. The editor's own style zooms its container to 0.9 (`zoom`), which in this build puts each tooltip (a fixed element placed from the icon's position) on top of its own icon, so the tooltip closes at once; the frame page therefore adds a style that sets that zoom back to 1 on the three containers that have it (the editor's and the two menus'), under the names they have in the mirror's script and in the install's. The style also gives the page the dark background the other in-game pages have instead of plain black (`#050505` under a dark gradient, on the body; min-height 100% on the html and body). The editor's page layout draws its own full-window container in plain black on top of the body, so the style also clears that container's background (under its two names, like the zoom), and the body's shows around the panel. When the language call answers at once, the script is added as before. The token the page adds to the requests that carry none is asked for at each request, since it is a promise too; a failing token call only leaves the request as it was. The inventory is read once, when the page loads. Not on the game's object, and not made up here: `getSelectedPlatformId`, `getMountedTrophiesDelta`, `getUnmountedTrophiesDelta`, `getWeaponPlatformTypeID`, `selectTrophyMount` and `selectWeaponMount` (the trophy and weapon menus' own routes, not the editor) and `getLoggedInUserID` and `confirmBuy` (only when the page sits in the website).

**The in-game pages fit the window.** Two pages of the in-game browser were fixed boxes: the missions page (940 px wide, its list 800 px high with its own scrollbar, and an empty rest of the window under it) and the lodge editor (a panel 762 px wide, 962 px for one layout, whose map overflowed the panel's right edge, and a frame as high as the whole body, so its lower end was cut off under the tab bar). Both now follow the window, with no fixed size in the added styles. Whatever answers `/client` (when its `<head>` is found exactly once, else it is served as it is and one warning is logged), the server adds a style to the head: for the lodge editor's and the missions pages (the body's id the page sets, with a selector as specific as the page's own, so no other page is touched) the body is a column of the tab bar and `#main`, which takes the height that is left; on the missions page the box takes the window's width up to 75rem and all that height, and its list scrolls inside the box, so nothing is left empty under it. The filters' labels, half the width of their row in the page's own style, would have the Search field beside its label in the wider box, so each is shown above its control (the checkbox's stays as it is). The page is served with `Cache-Control: no-cache`. The editor's frame page (above) adds to its style: the layout's container keeps a margin of 2% of the window's smaller side; the panel takes the window's width up to 80rem and at most its height (a very small window scrolls it); the editor's container takes the height of its content; the left column floats and the right one takes the width that is left; the list's and the style picture's heights follow the window (`--l` and `--h`, set by the script below); the style picture is no larger than its column. The map is a picture with the slots placed on it in pixels, so it cannot be resized by a style alone: a second script in the frame page sets `zoom` on the map's container (the picture's parent) so it fits the width of the right column and the height that is left under it (no more than 1.6 times and no less than 0.3), at load, on resize, whenever the editor draws something and whenever the panel or the column changes size. The tooltips and the drag picture, which are `position: fixed` inside that container, get the inverse zoom, so they stay where the icon's measured position puts them. The selectors are the names of both scripts the server may send (the mirror's and the install's), as for the zoom above. The picture shown under the mouse while a trophy or weapon is dragged (a `position: fixed` element) is placed by the script at the mouse's position divided by 0.9, the zoom of the editor's own style that the frame page cancels; left as it is, it showed 11% further right and down than the mouse (the drop was not affected: it reads the mouse and the targets' rectangles). Whatever answers `/dist/main.js`, the server drops that division from the four places that have it (`offset.x / 0.9` and `offset.y / 0.9`), so the picture is at the mouse. A script without those texts is served as it is. The panel is shared by the other in-game pages (lodge selection, trophy and weapon menus, bait sites), whose own boxes keep their sizes, so the window-sized panel rules apply only to a panel with the class `fit`, which the second script sets on the panel that holds the editor's container; the other pages are unchanged. The arrow before a mission pack or a mission ([#1000](https://github.com/yinyin99/hunter-resurgence/issues/1000)) did not turn when it opened, because the page shows the hidden list and changes nothing on the row: the served `client.js` (when `toggleSet`, `toggleDetails` and the pack the route opens by its id are each found exactly once, else it is served as it is and one warning is logged) also toggles the class `hr-open` on the row, and an added rule in the missions page's style turns the row's arrow 90° while it has it. The Packs, Active and Available headlines already swap their own caret.

**Key bindings can be changed.** After the player presses a new key on the in-game options page, the game reports it with `game.updateKeybinding(...)` (or `game.overrideKeybinding(...)` when the key is already bound to another action), but the page only defines these two on `window.ewcallback`, so the field stayed blank, the page never saw a change and never sent Save. Whatever answers `/client` (when its `<head>` is found exactly once), the server adds a small script to the head, before the style, that defines both on `window.game` (an existing `game` is kept, and a member that is already set is not replaced) and forwards every argument to the `window.ewcallback` member of the same name, looked up when the call is made because the page sets `ewcallback` later. The game keeps the bindings in the settings file of its environment, `%APPDATA%\<game name>Settings_local.bin` for a community server (the same file as its other options; only the keys you changed are written; Save writes it). Checked by a test of the served page; the check in the game itself (rebind, Save, restart, the key is kept) is the maintainer's, [#494](https://github.com/yinyin99/hunter-resurgence/issues/494).

**The mission buttons send the player's token.** The in-game missions panel posts Activate, Activate All, the favourite toggles and the automatic-activation and filter switches to `/v1/mission/...` without a token, so the server (rightly) answered token expired and the button did nothing. The server rewrites these six calls in `client.js` to pass the page helper's own `authMode:"required"` option: the helper then waits for the game's token and sends it as `Authorization: Bearer`, as the page's other authenticated calls do. Server auth is unchanged. Checked by a test of the served page; the check in the game itself (O panel: Activate All with a mission waiting, a single Activate, favouriting a mission pack and reopening the panel) is the maintainer's, [#774](https://github.com/yinyin99/hunter-resurgence/issues/774).

**The loadout saves send the player's token.** The inventory page's two loadout saves (the save icon, and the rename and colour dialog) call `PUT /v1/game/user/loadout/<id>` with no options, so the page's helper sent them without a token and the server answered token expired: the save was lost while the dialog closed as if it had worked ([#814](https://github.com/yinyin99/hunter-resurgence/issues/814)). The server rewrites both calls in `client.js` to pass `authMode:"required"`, as for the mission buttons; a call that already has options is left alone, so a script without these calls, or one already rewritten, is served as it is. Checked by a test of the served page; the check in the game (save a loadout, reopen the inventory, start a hunt with it) is the maintainer's.

**The in-game page's round timer.** The inventory page's Teams tab expects the round timer as a number, but the game answers a record `{time, running}`; for the host the page then recursed until it failed and the tab never opened. Whatever answers `/static/app/<version>/client.js` (recording, archive or mirror), the server replaces the page's two reads of the timer with one that takes a plain number or the record's `time`. The change is an exact text replacement on the served bytes (a page copy is never kept in the repository); if either expected text is not found exactly once that rewrite is skipped and one warning is logged. The script is served with `Cache-Control: no-cache`, and the page `/client` asks for it as `client.js?t=timer6` (the `?t=` token of the page's script tag is replaced), so a copy the game cached earlier is not used. Nothing of the page is rewritten for ammunition: its copying of a lodge stack is the one [Unlimited ammunition](#unlimited-ammunition) answers.

**The in-game page loads no trackers or preload.** The old site's page sent its page views and errors to third-party services and preloaded 61 pictures it does not need ([#829](https://github.com/yinyin99/hunter-resurgence/issues/829)). On `/client` the values of the keys `google_tag_manager_id` and `google_analytics_id` are emptied (matched by key; a page without them is served unchanged, with no warning). In `client.js` the 2 s picture preload is removed and the error-reporting init is made never to run; these are exact text replacements, and if one of the two texts is not found exactly once that rewrite is skipped and one warning is logged. This list is applied independently of the round timer's, so a script lacking one still gets the other.

**The Taxidermize button waits for the server.** The confirm-kill page enables its Taxidermize button for any ethical score, but a harvest that counts for the session member who shot the animal is not the harvester's to taxidermize, and nothing the game hands the page tells the two apart. Whatever answers `/static/app/<version>/client.js`, the server replaces the page's enable step (matched by pattern, as the two script versions name it differently): the button stays disabled until `GET /v1/harvest/screen` answers, and is enabled unless the answer is `{"taxidermy": false}`; a failed call (no token, a network error) enables it, as before. If the pattern is not found exactly once that rewrite is skipped and one warning is logged. The answer is kept in memory: after a server restart (or a skipped rewrite) the harvester's button is enabled, and his purchase is refused (404: his screen's harvest is kill 0), which the page shows as its "insufficient em$" (or gm$) message.

**The in-game script carries unused profile templates.** The served `client.js` (both versions) defines the `profile/gallery`, `profile/galleryDownload` and `profile/profileLayout` templates, but nothing renders them: the script has two routers (the missions router, and the one for the in-game screens: pause menu, options, confirm kill, inventory, lodge select and edit, loading, credits and the like), and neither has a profile or gallery route, and it never calls `Gallery/list`. The game has no gallery screen, and the server leaves the script as it is. Trophy shots and the gallery are shown on the website and the launcher (the profile tab, `GET /hunters/<handle>/gallery`, [Gallery](#gallery)), and a kill's photo on its kill details page (`/user/<handle>/score/`).

**The in-game page paints less.** On a weak PC the page is drawn on the CPU and stuttered on hover, click and scroll ([#829](https://github.com/yinyin99/hunter-resurgence/issues/829)): the lodge shows about 200 items with a large picture each. On `/client` the head gets a style that gives the lodge's items (`#lodge-main-view .inventory-item`) `content-visibility:auto`, so the ones scrolled out of view are not painted (the rule is on the item, not its tile, which the page moves and which is smaller than its content; the hover info and the drag picture are added to `body` and not clipped). A page without a `</head>` is served as before.

**The lodge inventory's carry grids scroll.** In the in-game Lodge Inventory the grids under "Unequipped carry items" (backpack, dog backpack, vehicle) grow with their rows, and the container of the panel's bottom strip clips them (`overflow:hidden` on `.inventory-bottom .backpack-view-container` in the page's `client.css`), so the rows of a full backpack below the third could not be reached ([#1039](https://github.com/yinyin99/hunter-resurgence/issues/1039)). The `/client` head gets `.inventory-bottom .backpack-view-container>.backpack-view{overflow-y:auto}`, and the drop target inside the grid (`.backpack-slot`) is made as tall as its rows (`align-self:flex-start;min-height:100%`), so a drop on the lower rows still lands. The panel keeps its height and the other panels are unchanged.

**Recordings of the current build** are found too: `NewBuild.Alias` adds, for every recorded request to `game_web_host` that the rewrite would move, the key of the moved request (the original stays).

**The old build** is no longer served by the server's own start page and connect reply: the launch URL, `config.domains`, `network` and the photo links are the current build's. Its other handlers keep their paths.

**Tokens.** The launch URL is `<scheme>://<game_web_host>/<session>/lang/<code>/access_token/<JWT>/refresh_token/<JWT>`, `<code>` the game's language (`en_US` unless `POST /auth/launch` was given another, see [Sign-in and tokens](#sign-in-and-tokens)). Both JWTs are HS256-signed with a key the server makes in a file beside the database (`state.db.jwtkey`) and carry `sub`, `user_id` (the account), `iss`, `scp`, `typ` (`access`/`refresh`), `iat`, `exp`, `jti`. Each is also stored in `tokens` like every other token, so a ban or a delete ends it, and every endpoint that takes a token takes them. The client does not check the signature; the server does when a refresh token comes back. An expired token is never sent: the game crashes on one.

**Sign-in on two hosts.** On our website (`web_host`) it is as above. In the launcher (`game_web_host`, through the player's forwarder) the page asks `POST /auth/link` for a code; the reply's `url` points the player's own browser to `https://<web_host>/auth/steam/login?link=<code>`, because only our domain has a certificate the browser trusts. The browser signs in and confirms there, and the launcher, polling `GET /auth/link/<code>` on the game host, receives the session cookie for that host (the launcher app and the backup tool do not use this: they sign in by [the loopback sign-in](#the-loopback-sign-in)). A poll that is still pending is not logged to the console (about one a second); the poll that completes and any error are.

**Settings the current build needs**: `web_host`, `game_web_host` and `crash_host`. The player's kit redirects `game_web_host` and `crash_host` only; `web_host` is ours and is never redirected.

## Backpack capacity

The game's carrying capacity is the connect reply's `boost.default_inventory_capacity` plus the capacity of the owned Increased Capacity item (item 92, `BACKPACK`, 10000 g), which it reads from the account's inventory row (the `boost` entry's own capacity is not read). The server sets the first from the `default_inventory_capacity` setting ([settings](#run-it), default 20000: everyone carries 20 units, 30 with the upgrade; the original gave 10 and 20, so this is a community-server change). The game adds the capacity of every owned copy of the item, so the connect sends only one row of it even if the account holds several. The value is computed at every connect, so it is the same after a restart and for every player in a hunt together.

## Starting data

`serve` builds the player state from three sources and needs none of the recordings. For each input the first source that has it wins (a recording never loses to the install, and the install never loses to the facts file), except where noted:

| Input | 1. Recording | 2. Install (`install_dir`) | 3. Facts file |
|---|---|---|---|
| Connect reply (the game data, and the local account's start) | the newest recorded `connect` | `game/json_connect_results.json`, with the sample account replaced (below) | - |
| Application catalogue (`species`, `items`, `reserves`, `locations`) | the recorded `Application/application` | `species.json` and `item.json` of the archives | reserves, lodge positions and the enum tables (`types`, `groups`, `categories`, `client_types`) |
| `populations` and `species_ammo` of a reserve | the newest connect after a `Launch/start` of that reserve | - | `reserves[].populations` and `species`; the ammo of a species is its entry of the connect's `ethical`, also over a recorded connect (a recorded one may hold a seasonal event's ammo); a werewolf's and a wendigo's entry is the facts file's `species_rules` when the connect lists none |
| Kill page labels and texture names | the recorded locale and web scripts | - | `labels`, `textures` |
| Item definitions of update 10 | the recorded connect's | `game/json_inventory.json`, then the catalogue above, then the install's item definitions | - |
| The player's skill list | recorded only (without it: fresh skills) | - | - |
| Discounts | never recorded: the operator's [store sales](#store-sales) (without one: none) | - | - |
| The avatar list (`Avatar/getAvatars`) | recorded only: a restore picks the profile's avatar from it ([docs/restore.md](restore.md#storing-the-model)); without it every restored account has the default avatar, unreported, and the website offers no avatar to choose ([Avatars](#avatars)) | - | `store_avatars`: the avatars the store sells, added after the recorded ones |
| Animal ids, score families | - | - | `animals`, `families` (always) |

With recordings that cover every input, the replies are the ones the recordings gave, except that the connect reply has `ingame_event` null unless a seasonal event runs for the player ([Seasonal events](#seasonal-events)), and `species_ammo` from the `ethical` list (the event's ammo on the event's reserves). Every connect reply also sets `boost.default_inventory_capacity` from the setting ([Backpack capacity](#backpack-capacity)). It also sets each `boost` entry's `own` per account: true when the account owns the entry's item (in any slot, placed or stacked) or the item has a `hunterscoreGiveLevel` and the account's hunterscore is at least that level (a purchase or a reached level unlocks it at the next connect), else false. Without a recorded connect, `connect` is the install's sample made neutral: the account, `account_info`, `network`, `user_stats` and `boost` of the sample's test player are replaced (handle `hunter`, user id 1, no e-mail, Steam id, address or counters; `config.domains` names the settings' hosts), `spawn` and `competitions` are empty and everything that is game data (species, items text, locations, store, ethical ammo, achievements, ...) stays. `serve` says `player state: off` only when there is neither a recorded connect nor a connect sample in the install.

**The welcome message.** Every `connect` queues one `update 3` toast for the first heartbeat (`State.TOAST_TEXT`, shown once however many times the game connects): "Hunter Resurgence community server" and "Progress is saved on the community server", two short lines that fit the in-game toast.

**The starting profile of a new account** is neutral and the same for everyone, recordings or not: the items of `start.items` of the facts file, one each in the first free slot of its type above the backpack and the pockets, or in the backpack when those are taken (a full stack for items with a stack size), the items of `start.stored` in the inventory (slot 0, never equipped), the wallet of `start.wallet` (1,000 em$ and 15,000 gm$) and every skill at level 1 (no weapon skill rows until a first hit), and an `account` object made from the install's sample (no avatar, name or other field of the operator's recorded account). Today that is the free start of the original game: a 12 GA single-shot shotgun and a .243 bolt-action rifle, a box each of .243 and of 12 GA birdshot, buckshot and slug shells, binoculars, the blue basic Arctic set (jacket, pants, boots, hat, gloves, scarf), a T-shirt, basic pants and basic boots, and a second T-shirt in the backpack (the inventory page fills an empty body, legs or feet slot with the slot's default clothing, looked up among the account's own items, and fails without it); and, stored since kit version 2, a 4x32mm rifle scope, a deer bleat call, a roe deer caller, a compact digital camera and a water bottle, and the items of the original's free Steam extra equipment: scent eliminator, camping supplies, first aid kit, wind indicator, face paint, moose scent, hog scent spray and whitetail deer urine spray; and, stored since kit version 3 for the Halloween event's creatures, the Maisto 12 GA sawed-off shotgun "Wolfsbane" and a box of Silver 12 GA slug shells (the ammunition `species_rules` allows for the werewolf and the wendigo); and, stored since kit version 4, the Bust Through minigame (item 65). Change the facts file to change it; a restored backup keeps what it brings and gets each start item it lacks, the way the next paragraph describes. Only the local account of a local server starts from a recorded connect. A community server (`offline_mode` false) with a recorded connect and no connect sample in the install does not start.

**The start kit of an older account.** `start.version` of the facts file (1 when absent) is the kit's version, and `accounts.start_kit` the version an account has (0: it existed before schema 22). At each server start, every account with a lower version gets each start item it does not own, once and with no action from the player, and then has the current version: in the item's slot unless that is taken, else in the backpack, the way a restore adds the start items its zip lacks (`PlayerRows.AddMissingStart` is the one function of both). An item the account owns, in any slot, is never added again. A banned account is skipped until the first start after the ban is lifted. A new account has the current version from the start: it is seeded with the kit. Raise the version when items are added to the kit.

**Equipped ammunition must fit its weapon.** The game fires whatever ammunition sits in the ammunition slot (8 to 10, the weapon slot 2 to 4 plus 6), with no check of its own that the two fit. So the server never leaves an ammunition there under a weapon it does not fit: it fits when the weapon's catalogue `fit` list of the ammunition category has it, or, for a weapon without one (a server with no recorded catalogue), when the ammunition is among the attachments of the weapon's install definition; a weapon with neither list is not judged. The start items a restore adds, and the start-kit update's at start-up (only for an account the kit added something to; the server prints how many equipped items it moved), are added first and the equipped slots are judged after them (`State.SettleEquipped`: one weapon to a weapon slot, the one with the highest guid; every fitting stack stays in an ammunition slot, several stacks of one ammunition too, and only the stacks that do not fit the weapon beside them go to the lodge, slot 0), so an ammunition already in a slot the kit then fills is judged too; [Restore](restore.md) has the restore's part. A loadout skips an ammunition entry for an ammunition slot whose weapon the same loadout puts in the slot 6 below when it does not fit ([Loadouts](#loadouts)). `admin ammo-fit` (below) repairs the accounts restored before.

**The free ammunition.** `start.free_ammo` of the facts file lists the ammunition every player gets for free (`items`: the .243 ammunition and the three 12 GA shells) and `boxes`, how many full boxes (3). Every launch tops each of them up in the launch's transaction, before the connect, to that many full boxes (`Launch/start`, `Launch/start_mp`, `Launch/join`, `session/start_single_player`, and `POST /auth/launch` when it opens a hunt), a box being the item's stack size in the catalogue (so 30 .243 rounds, 90 birdshot, 30 buckshot, 30 slugs): the stacks the player has used are filled first, then new full stacks are added. A larger stock, bought in the store, is never lowered. The refilled stacks are in the connect that follows, like any change of the inventory.

**Unlimited ammunition.** With `unlimited_ammo` on (the default) the same launches also show the game only the oldest of the lodge's stacks of one ammunition (the others are kept, hidden), fill every other ammunition the player owns to a full box, and top the free ammunition up to one box instead of `boxes` (the lodge keeps one stack of each, which the page copies, and it copies a stack only when it is the only one in the lodge), [Unlimited ammunition](#unlimited-ammunition).

**The `infos` of the connect reply** are the definitions of the account's own items, one per distinct `item_id`, in order of first appearance, as the game's server sends them. A list longer than `items` stops the game right after `connect`, so the recorded connect's `infos` are only a source of definitions, never sent whole.

A definition not in the samples is built from the catalogue, with its `arm_type` (`extra.arm_type`: 1 for a top without sleeves, to which the game then adds the arms, else 0; without it a character wears a bare-armed top with hands only); an ammo's is completed with the `extra.ammo` ballistics of the install's item definitions, which the game needs to get past `connect` for an account that owns such an ammo. An item the catalogue does not list (a restored inventory may keep one the store no longer sells) has its definition built from the install's item definitions the same way; the store still shows only the catalogue's items. An owned item without any definition is left out of `infos`, and the game closes after `connect`.

**The `items_locale` of the connect reply** lists the name of every item. The game looks an item's name up there when it creates one that stands in the world (a placed tent), and it closes on an item the list lacks, as soon as a reserve with such an item placed loads. The sample's list stops at the last item before the events, so the server adds, with the catalogue's `define`, `name` and `variationname` (as `variationName`), every item of the catalogue and of the install's definitions that the list does not have (the event tents among them); the entries the list has stay as they are.

Still answered only from recordings (without them: a miss, `null` under `/v1/`): the launcher and website pages and their assets, the sign-up host, the avatar and photo hosts, news, `Launch/start` (its tokens), `User/jwt`, the lists that wrap a recorded base reply (`Me/poll`, `Expedition/list`, `Public_user/expedition`, `Splash/list_active`) and the profile, statistics, mission, challenge, leaderboard and friend pages. `coverage --no-recordings` prints the exact list.

## Public reads

What a profile page shows of a player, in the game and on the website (community server only; a local server replays its recordings). Every public read finds its account with `State.PublicAccount(<user id or handle>)`: an account of this server, not banned, never the recorded player's (no player here owns it, so a backup that nobody restored onto their own account is never shown). It then reads that account's rows with `Store.Open(account)` like every other read ([Accounts](#accounts)). The owner-only areas (inventory, loadouts, map setups, wallet, messages, missions) check the token's own user id instead (`State.IsOwn`).

**Nobody can list the players.** A game read by `user_id` needs a player's token and answers for the caller himself and for another player only when the caller may see him (`State.SeenAccount`: `State.CanSee`, his friends and whoever a board shows him, [Who sees whom](#who-sees-whom)), within the caller's [read limit](#read-limits); for anyone else it is the reply of a user id that is none here, so walking user ids finds nothing. By handle (`Public_user/getByHostname`, `/hunters/<handle>`) any player here is found, by a signed-in player. A player sees only his own friends, and `online` only for himself and his friends.

**Signed-in players only.** On the website's host the reads of another player are for a signed-in player: every `/hunters/` route (`HuntersApi`: one filter on the group) and the kill details page `/user/<handle>/score/` answer 401 `{"status": "signed_out", "message": "Sign in first."}` without a session cookie (`SignIn.Session`); the score page also takes the game's token (`State.Authenticate`, so a local server answers it as the local account). The website and the launcher send the player's cookie ([docs/website.md](website.md#signed-in-players-only)).

| Read | Answer |
|---|---|
| `POST /v1/Public_user/getByHostname`, form `hostname=<handle>` (any case), with a player's token (without: token expired) | `{id, handle, hostname, avatar, hunterscore, rank, membership, membershipType, membershipTitle, online, stats: {distance, duration}}`: `membership` as the connect `account` has it, the type and title as `Me/me` has them; `online` while a hunt of the account is open and had a heartbeat in the last `HEARTBEAT_TIMEOUT`, shown to the account itself and its friends only (false for anyone else); `stats` the distance and duration of [the statistics](#the-statistics). `null` for a handle that is unknown or banned, and past the caller's read limit |
| `GET /game/user/<id>/profile` (API v2; also `/v1/game/user/<id>/profile`, the current build), with a player's token, for a player he may see | `{background, ribbon, hunterscore, earned_achievements}`: `background` of the account's restored reply (null without one), `ribbon` by the share of the connect's achievement thresholds the account has earned (the restored reply's only when the connect has no achievements), the thresholds of its achievements that have an earn time ([Achievements](#achievements)). 404 (`application/vnd.goa.error`) for a user id that is unknown or banned, one the caller may not see, and without a token |
| `GET /hunters/<handle>` on `web_host`, for the website's server | `{handle, hunterscore, rank, membership, avatar, background, ribbon}` ([The board](#the-hunterscore)); `avatar` is the URL the website draws ([Avatars](#avatars)); `background` is the header picture the player chose, the `background` of the account's restored `/game/user/<id>/profile` reply (a file name such as `exploration_3.png`), null without one or when it is not text; `ribbon` is the collector ribbon the profile shows (a file name such as `moose.png`): by the share of the connect's achievement thresholds the account has earned, the original's rule (90 % and more `turkey.png`, 75 `fox.png`, 50 `bear.png`, 25 `elk.png`, 5 `moose.png`, below 5 % null), the restored `ribbon` when the connect has no achievements; the reply of `GET /game/user/<id>/profile` has the same `ribbon` |
| `POST /v1/Skill/list`, form `user_id`, with a player's token | The numbers of the account of that user id (the player's own, or another player he may see: `{weapon: {item id: n}, tracking: [n], spotting: [n]}`, n = level plus the fraction of its span; a weapon type's number is on every item of its family, [One shooting skill per weapon type](#one-shooting-skill-per-weapon-type)); the empty skills for anyone else and an unknown or banned one |
| `GET /hunters/<handle>/skills` on `web_host`, for the website's server | `{species: [{name, image, spotting, tracking}], weapons: [{id, name, variation, group, value, finishes}]}`, the same numbers named by the catalogue (`image` is the species' define in lower case, null without one; the dog species `DOG`, `DOG_02`, `DOG_03` and species 56 are left out, as the original website did; a weapon row is a weapon type, one per [weapon family](#weapon-families) the player has a skill in: `id`, `name`, `variation` (null without one) and `group` (the sub-category, null without one) are those of the family's representative, or of its first member shown to the account when the representative is not (below), `value` the type's shooting skill, `finishes` the family's other items as one text each: the finish alone when the item has the representative's name (`Camo`), else its name and its finish (`Falcon (Oriental)`), `[]` for a weapon alone; an item is a finish or a row only if the catalogue lists it or the account owns it, [One shooting skill per weapon type](#one-shooting-skill-per-weapon-type)); 404 for an unknown or banned handle |
| `POST /v1/Statistics/total`, form `user_id=<id>`, with a player's token | `{duration, distance, data}` of the account `<id>` names: its restored statistics plus its server hunts, the open one's counts so far included ([The statistics](#the-statistics)), the player's own as before, another player's when the caller may see him. A user id the caller may not see, unknown, banned or the recorded player's is the empty reply |
| `POST /v1/Achievement/stats`, form `user_id=<id>`, with a player's token | The achievements of the account `<id>` names (the caller's, or a player he may see), as [the player's own](#achievements): one row per achievement with its counter and, once earned, its earn time for each threshold (`triggers`); `[]` for anyone else and a user id that is none here, banned or the recorded player's. A local server answers the player's own only |
| `GET /hunters/<handle>/statistics` on `web_host`, for the website's server | `{weapons: [{weapon, weaponId, ammo, ammoId, shots, accuracy, kills, killsPerHit, longest}], animals: [{name, spotted, tracked, harvested}], collectables: [], distanceKm, distanceKmHere, huntSeconds, huntSecondsHere}`: the same statistics, summed per weapon and ammo and per species, named from the catalogue (a weapon or ammo without a name by its id, a species by `#<id>`; a typical row sits before the non-typical one of the same species); `weaponId` and `ammoId` the item ids (null for a key that is not a number); `accuracy` and `killsPerHit` in percent, `longest` the longest hit in metres (null without one), `distanceKm` the walked distance (the statistics' metres) in kilometres, `distanceKmHere` the same for the server hunts alone ([The statistics](#the-statistics)), `huntSeconds` the hunt time (the restored total plus the server hunts), `huntSecondsHere` the part of it played on this server ([Server playtime](#server-playtime-and-hunting-statistics)), whole seconds. `collectables` is always empty (the server has no names for them). 404 for a handle that is not an account of a player here |
| `POST /v1/User/friendslist`, form `user_id`, `offset`, `limit`, with the player's token | `{total, friends}` of the token's own account only: `total` all its friends, `friends` one page of them ([Friends](#friends)); another player's `user_id` is `{total: 0, friends: []}` |
| `GET /hunters/<handle>/trophies?page=<n>` on `web_host`, for the website's server | `{page, pages, total, rows: [{name, date, competitionId, competition, image}]}`, 24 rows a page: the account's restored `Trophy/list` rows (competition trophies), the hidden ones left out, newest first by `ts` (rows without a readable one last; the order inside one page is the order of the whole list); the kills of the lodges (`/game/user/trophies`) and the harvests made here are not listed. `name` is the row's `name`, `date` the day of its `ts` (UTC, yyyy-MM-dd; "" without one), `competitionId` and `competition` its `competition_id` and `competition_name` (null without them), `image` the file name of its `image` address without the extension (null without one); a page past the end is the last; 404 for an unknown or banned handle |
| `GET /hunters/<handle>/dogs` on `web_host`, for the website's server | `{dogs: [{id, name, type, variation, level, percent, skills: [{skill, title, type, level}], ribbons}]}`: one per owned dog (an inventory row whose catalogue item is of the DOGS category and the DOG type); `id` is the item's id, `name` its metadata's `dog_name` ("" without one), `type` the item's name, `variation` its `variationname` (null without one). `level` is the `lvl` of the last row of the connect's `dog_skills` whose cumulative `xp` is at most the metadata's `experience` (0 without one), `percent` the way to the next row (100 at the last, 0 without a table). The `unlocks` of the rows reached give the rest: `ribbons` is the number of `RIBBON` trophy unlocks, and `skills` lists each breed skill once, in order, as `skill` (the define without `DOG_SKILL_`, `LOYALTY_STAMINA`), `title` (as capitalised words), `type` (`loyalty`, `retrieving`, `scent` or `pointing`) and `level`. A dog of a breed group (by item id, as the original website grouped them: retrievers 501-506, 469, 470, 473, 474; scent hounds 622-625; pointers 842-845, 887, 888) lists the six loyalty skills and the group's six, each at level 1 and one more for every unlock of it reached; the unlocks of any other skill are left out. A dog of no group lists the skills it has unlocked, at the number of unlocks; 404 for an unknown or banned handle |
