| `GET /game/user/<id>/ranks` (API v2; also `/v1/game/user/<id>/ranks`, the current build), with a player's token, for a player he may see | `[{id, category_id, value}]`, one per rank row of the facts file, in its order: `id` the row's `id`, `category_id` 1 for an `animal` row, 2 for a `weapon` row, 3 for a `collectable` row, `value` the row's count from [the statistics](#the-statistics) (the restored ones plus the server hunts, the open one so far) counted as [the hunterscore](#the-hunterscore) counts it, but before its cap (a count above `cap` stays). Content type `application/vnd.game_data.user.rank+json; type=collection`. 404 (`application/vnd.goa.error`) for a user id that is unknown, banned or the recorded player's, one the caller may not see, and without a token |
| `GET /hunters/<handle>/ranks` on `web_host`, for the website's server | `{cap, ranks: [{id, category, name, value}], milestones: [{category, title, stepTitle, stepText, icon, steps, value}]}`: `cap` the most a row adds to the hunterscore; `ranks` the same rows and values as the game's reply, `category` `animals`, `weapons` or `collectables`, `name` the row's `title`; `milestones` one per connect `achievements` definition that has a `value` path and a `category` of 0 (`animals`), 1 (`weapons`), 2 (`exploration`) or 4 (`day-mission`), in the connect's order: `title` its `group_title` ("" without one), `stepTitle` and `stepText` its own `title` and `description` (the format strings of a step, `%d` standing for its threshold, "" without one; "%d hours", "%d total hours spent hunting." for the duration), `icon` its `icon_url` without the extension (null without one), `steps` its numeric `triggers`, `value` the counter its path names over the same statistics ([Achievements](#achievements)), in whole numbers. 404 for an unknown or banned handle |
| `GET /hunters/<handle>/achievements` on `web_host`, for the website's server | `{categories: [{name, level, done, total}], unlocked, recent: [{title, description, date}]}`, over the connect's `achievements` definitions ([Achievements](#achievements)): per category (its `category_title`) `total` is the number of thresholds of its definitions and `done` those with an earn time, `level` the definitions with every threshold earned; `unlocked` is all the thresholds with an earn time; `recent` the last 10 by earn time (newest first, then definition id), `title` and `description` the definition's with `%d` the threshold, `date` the earn day (UTC); 404 for an unknown or banned handle |
| `GET /hunters/<handle>/friends?page=<n>` on `web_host`, for the website's server | `{page, pages, total, rows: [{handle, hunterscore, avatar}]}`, 20 rows a page: the signed-in player's own friends ([Friends](#friends)) sorted by handle (upper case, ordinal), for his own handle only; a page past the end is the last; 403 for any other handle, known or not, and signed out |
| `GET /game/user/<id>/lodges` and `/game/user/<id>/lodges/<lodge>` (API v2; also `/v1/game/user/<id>/lodges[/<lodge>]`, the current build), with a player's token | The lodges of the account `<id>` names (the token's own, or a player he may see), a locked one too, each animal slot with its `trophy_details` ([Lodges](#lodges-and-trophy-visibility)). A user id the caller may not see, unknown, banned or the recorded player's, and a lodge the account has not, get the replay's empty reply: `[]` (404 for one lodge) on API v2, `null` under `/v1` |
| `POST /v1/Leaderboard/personal`, form `user_id`, `season_no`, with a player's token | `{status: "done", data: [{specie, score, distance}]}` of the account `<id>` names (the caller's, or a player he may see) for the season `season_no` ([Leaderboards and seasons](#leaderboards-and-seasons): 0 all time, 1 the restored harvests, 2 and later this server's seasons): for each species with a harvest of the account in that season, the harvest of the best score as `score` and the one of the longest range as `distance`. A record is `{distance, confirm_ts, weapon_id, animal_id, gender, texture, score, species}`: `distance` in millimetres, `score` as text with 4 decimals, `animal_id` the kill id (a restored one's original id). The saved replies of the original's own seasons are not read. A user id the caller may not see, none here, banned or the recorded player's, and a season with no harvest of the account, are `{status: "done", data: []}` |
| `GET /hunters/<handle>/best?season=` on `web_host`, for the website's server | `{rows: [{species, score, range, weapon, ts, kill}]}`: one row per species of the `Leaderboard/personal` reply above for `season` (0 all time, the default; 404 for a season that does not exist), the species' best score: `species` its name (`#<id>` where the catalogue has none), `range` the metres of that record, `weapon` the item's name ("" where unknown), `ts` Unix seconds, `kill` the id; hidden harvests are left out; 404 for an unknown or banned handle |
| `POST /v1/Expedition/list` and `/v1/Public_user/expedition`, form `user_id=<id>`, with a player's token | The hunts of the account `<id>` names ([The statistics](#the-statistics)): its server hunts and the hunts its backup saved, the player's own as before, another player's when the caller may see him. A user id the caller may not see, unknown, banned or the recorded player's is the empty reply |
| `GET /hunters/<handle>/hunts?page=<n>` on `web_host`, for the website's server | `{page, pages, total, rows: [{id, start, seconds, reserve, lodge, collectables, kills}]}`, 40 rows a page, newest first: the `Expedition/list` of the account (server hunts, then saved ones); `start` Unix seconds, `seconds` the time since it started while the hunt is open, `reserve` the catalogue's name, `lodge` the name of the hunt's start as the launcher's map calls it (see [The launch map](#the-launch-map)), "" for a hunt with no start or a place the catalogue does not list; a page past the end is the last; 404 for an unknown or banned handle |
| `GET /hunters/<handle>/hunts/<id>` (`latest`: the newest) on `web_host`, for the website's server | `{id, reserve, lodge, start, end, weapons, animals, collectables, achievements, distanceKm, seconds, antlers, kills}` of `Public_user/expedition` (`lodge` as in the history rows): `weapons` and `animals` are the statistics rows of the hunt's stats. A hunt that is open (`end` null) has the rows of its events so far (made at the read, [The statistics](#the-statistics)), `seconds` the time since it started and `distanceKm` 0 until it closes (the game reports the distance only at its end); its `kills`, `collectables` and `antlers` are listed as they happen. `collectables` are the pickups of a hunt made here, `{name, score, ts}` (event 24, one per `collectable_id`; `name` the catalogue's collectable name, `#<type>` without one, `score` as the pickup was scored, `ts` Unix seconds); a saved hunt has none (its backup holds per-type sums, no pickups). `antlers` are the shed antlers, `{species, double, score}` (`double` true for a pair): a hunt made here's event 36 pickups, a saved hunt's the `antler_collectables` of its backup. `achievements` are `{title, description}` (the connect definitions' texts with `%d` the threshold, [Achievements](#achievements)): for a hunt made here every threshold whose earn time is the hunt's close in whole seconds (a hunt's close is the earn time of what its statistics reach: the disconnect, the next launch or the timer's close; a threshold a restore or a server start earned is under no hunt; hunts closed together share the latest close), for a saved hunt the thresholds of its stats' `achievements` earned between its start and its end. A list with no entry is empty, and the website and launcher show no section for it. `kills` are `{id, species, weapon, ammo, distanceM, score, weightKg, when, sheet}` of the last hit; `sheet` is true for a kill made here and for a saved one whose zip held its score sheet page ([Pictures](restore.md#pictures)), false for any other saved kill; 404 for an unknown or banned handle, and for an unknown hunt |
| `GET /hunters/<handle>/score/<kill>` on `web_host`, for the website's server | The score sheet of `/user/<handle>/score/` as JSON, from the same inputs: `kind` (`score`: the typical antler sheet with `facts`, `rows` right, left and difference, `abnormal` and `sums`; `antler`: the tines sheet with `rows` left, right and points; `antler` without rows for a species whose sheet is no table), then `species`, `weapon`, `scope`, `ammo`, `distanceM`, `state`, `bodyPart`, `stance`, `platform`, `weightKg`, `type`, `woundTime`, `integrity`, `shotLocation`, `shots`, `when`, `expeditionId`, `score`, `harvestValue` and `gender` (`male` or `female`) (the last hit's data); 404 for an unknown or banned handle and for a kill made elsewhere (a saved one) unless the zip held its score sheet page: then the facts of the saved hunt (`species`, `weapon`, `distanceM`, `score` and the rest, `kind` `antler`, no `rows`: the worked sheet is in the saved page, which the game shows) |
| `GET /hunters/<handle>/gallery?page=<n>` on `web_host`, for the website's server | `{page, pages, total, rows: [{id, type, url, thumbnail, label, animal, kill, hunter, share}]}`, 24 rows a page, newest first: the account's gallery ([Gallery](#gallery)); `url` is the signed-in player's link of the picture: the original, on every gallery, by an opaque name; `thumbnail` is the same link (the game's square crop is only for `Gallery/list`; the grid scales the picture to its tile, so new and restored shots look alike); `kill` is the id of the kill the photo shows when `/hunters/<handle>/score/<kill>` answers for it, else null; `hunter`, on a trophy shot the account took of another member's kill only, that member's handle (the photo and `kill` are his); `share`, on the player's own rows only, the bare link of the original; a page past the end is the last; 401 `signed_out` without a session; 404 for an unknown or banned handle |

### Read limits

Each account has three caps, in memory (`ReadLimit.cs`; a restart forgets the counts): **120 reads a minute** of profiles and hunts (`State.ReadAllowed`: every `/hunters/` read and score page of a signed-in player, `Public_user/getByHostname` of another handle, every game read by `user_id` of another id, known or not; the player's own game reads are not counted), **600 pictures a minute** of other players (`State.PictureAllowed`, `/photos/`: a gallery page loads one picture per tile, so pictures have their own, larger cap and never use up the profile reads) and **30 friend searches an hour** (`State.SearchAllowed`, `GET /account/friends/search`). Past a cap the read is refused: 429 `rate_limited` on the website's routes, the reply of a user id that is none here in the game. An account that hits a cap raises one `🚨 Read limit hit` alert in the review channel ([Discord alerts](#discord-alerts)), at most once an hour: perhaps a script collecting the players.

## The facts file

`server/facts/facts.json` holds what the install does not carry, in this project's own format; it is copied next to the server (`facts/facts.json`). One JSON object, `"format": 1`:

| Key | Content |
|---|---|
| `animals` | The animal ids in catalogue order; the index is the animal's id and the index of `spawn_licenses` (the admin spawner's names) |
| `families` | `{family: [species id]}`: which score formula a species uses (`Scoring`) |
| `reserves` | `[{id, name, map: [left, top, right, bottom], species: [species id], populations: [...]}]` for all 12 reserves. `map` is the world box the reserve's map picture covers: world `x` and `z` of its left, top, right and bottom edges, a square give or take a few world units ([The launch map](#the-launch-map)). `species` is the species of the reserve (the keys of its `species_ammo`). A population is `{index, animal, density?, reserve?, endangered, license, groups: [{x, z, radius, males, females, enabled?}], random?: {males, females, min_size, max_size, avg_size, max_males, max_females, radius, single_gender}}`; `density` (default the animal), `reserve` (default the reserve; `-1` for the rows every reserve carries, the dogs) and `enabled` are left out when they have their default. The loader writes the client's rows from them (`Facts.ReserveConnect`) |
| `variants` | `{variant id: {min_score, max_score, css_multiplier}}`: the score bounds of every species the game scores (the ids of `families`, the non-typical deer 100, 101 and 106 included) and the multiplier of its gm$ ([Scoring and paying a harvest](#scoring-and-paying-a-harvest)) |
| `collectables` | `{type id: {cat?, score: {min, max}}}`: the score range (and the text format's `cat`) of a collectable type whose range the install's `collectable.json` lacks, used only then (see Pickups below). Today the fallow deer shed antler (2055, 40.6 to 88.9, the bounds of the original scores of restored single antlers) and the five pancake event items (1041 to 1045, 1 to 3: the install lists no other item of their event, so they take the range of the seasonal items it does list, the pot of gold 1 to 3 and the valentine and rose items 1 to 3) |
| `species_rules` | `{species id: {ammo: [item id]}}`: for the event creatures (the werewolf 50 and the wendigo 58), the permitted ammo when a recorded connect from before the event lists none ([Event creatures](#seasonal-events)) |
| `locations` | `[{id, reserve, type, x, y, z}]`: the start places and where they are |
| `events` | `{<event name>: {title, missions, items, community, ethical: {ammo: [item id], species: [species id]}, creatures: [species id], coats: {<species id>: {possessed: [coat], normal: [coat]}}, reserves: {<game reserve number>: {ammo: [item id], populations: [...]}}}}`: the event's panel ([Seasonal events](#seasonal-events)), the permitted ammunition of its possessed animals and creatures, its creatures, the coats that prove a possessed or a normal animal of a species, and what it adds on each reserve; a population is written as in `reserves[].populations`, with `reserve` the web's number of the reserve (the game's + 1) |
| `types` | `{item type: {id, slots, capacity}}`: the numeric type of an item type and the slots and capacity of its items |
| `groups`, `categories`, `client_types` | `{name: number}` tables for the item definitions of the archives; an item without a client type has its type's number |
| `labels` | `{locale key: English text}`: the words of the kill page (poses, animal states, body parts, organs, textures) |
| `textures` | `{texture id: name}` |
| `start` | `{items: [item id], free_ammo: {items: [item id], boxes}, wallet: {currency: amount}}`: the starting profile, and the ammunition every hunt tops up to `boxes` full boxes ([Starting data](#starting-data)) |
| `own_limits` | `{client_types: {<client type>: limit}, items: {<item id>: limit}}`: the most copies of an item a player owns ([Ownership limits](#ownership-limits)). A limit is a number (the most), `0` (no limit) or `"catalogue"` (the catalogue's own `ownMultiple` and `ownLimit`); an entry of `items` goes before the item's client type, and an item or a type the table lacks follows the catalogue |
| `taxidermy` | `{ems, gms}`: what a taxidermy or a skull mount costs in em$ and in gm$ (the original's 10 and 2,000 when the key is missing; 0 makes it free) ([Taxidermy](#taxidermy)) |
| `trophy_lodge` | `{lodges: [{item, engine, name}], catalogue: {<page>: reply}}` ([Lodges](#lodges-and-trophy-visibility)): `lodges` the store items that are a lodge (`item` the item id, `engine` the layout number of `lodge_engine_id`, `name` the name a new lodge gets); `catalogue` the replies of `/gameengine/trophylodge/` by page: `animal`, `skull`, `weapon`, `slot/animal`, `slot/weapon` and `lodge/<engine>`, the same for everyone |
| `ranks` | `{cap, rows: [{id, title, category, species \| items \| types}]}`: the rows of the hunterscore ([The hunterscore](#the-hunterscore)). `category` is `animal` (one species id), `weapon` (weapon item ids) or `collectable` (collectable type ids); `id` is the row's id in the game's ranks reply and `title` its name on the website's ranks tab |
| `store_avatars` | `[{item, id, name, gender}]`: the avatars the store sells ([Avatars](#avatars)): `item` the catalogue item that unlocks it, `id` the id of its picture (`256/<id>.png`), `name` and `gender` (`m` or `f`) as a list entry gives them |
| `daily_missions` | `{cycle, difficulties, templates}` ([Daily missions](#daily-missions)): `cycle` is the difficulty id of each day of the streak cycle (16 entries), `difficulties` `[{id, title, alias, cooldown, color, reward_id, reward}]` (the calendar's difficulty row and reward row, `reward` the gm$ of a day in the first cycle), `templates` `[{id, difficulty, title, giver: {id, alias, name?, image?}, objectives: [English title]}]`: the daily missions, their objectives written as the missions' titles ([Progress](#missions)). The 11 EASY templates (ids 49, 113, 121, 154, 174, 196, 245, 273, 291, 324, 335) and the MEDIUM ones 381 and 510 are the game's, with its givers, species, genders and objectives; the MEDIUM difficulty row (cooldown 20, blue, reward row 131, 500 gm$) is the game's too (confirmed). The other MEDIUM templates (301 to 303) and the HARD ones, HARD's cooldown and its reward row id are this project's own (made up) |

The file holds names, numbers and positions of the game's world only: no id or handle of a person, no saved reply of a server and no host name. The install is the source of everything it already carries; add to the facts file only what no install file has.

## Accounts

Every player is in `state.db`. `accounts` holds the game's user id, the Steam id (optional, unique), the handle (unique, case does not matter), the creation time, the time of the one-shot claim, a banned flag, whether the player plays seasonal events, whether the Discord invite was shown to the player, whether the restore offer was, whether the account has its start kit and the connect reply's `account` object; `tokens` maps a token to an account and keeps only its SHA-256 hash. No e-mail, real name or address is stored. `seasons` (schema 29) holds the leaderboard seasons, shared by all players ([Leaderboards and seasons](#leaderboards-and-seasons)). Every per-player table has an `account_id`. The ids a restored backup brings (item guids, kill ids) are unique per account, so two accounts can both own item 100 or kill 1; the ids the server hands out (expeditions, photos) are unique in the whole database and always looked up together with the account. A new account starts with the connect template's inventory, wallet and skills (`State.CreateAccount`; [Starting data](#starting-data)). A database from before accounts is refused: delete it.

`banned_steam` and `steam_deletions` (schema 32) are the two tables without an `account_id`: they are keyed by Steam id and outlive the account ([Delete my data](#upload-a-backup-zip), [Archive claims](#archive-claims)).

`discord_links` and `discord_rewards` (schema 40) are the tables of [Discord Linked Roles](#discord-linked-roles): the link of an account to its Discord account with the user's sealed tokens (it goes with the account), and the reward ledger, which keeps one keyed hash per Discord id paid and outlives the account.

`unethical_kills` (schema 41) flags the stored kills that `admin ethics` leaves in a state the ammunition rule would not give them, so they can be found later ([The ammunition rule on stored kills](#the-ammunition-rule-on-stored-kills)).

`host_bans` (schema 42, no foreign keys like `friends`) keeps the multiplayer bans of the hosts: the host's account, the banned account and the time ([The host's bans](#the-hosts-bans)); it goes with either account, and moves with an account that is given another user id.

`harvest_links` (schema 44, with the `photos` columns `kill_id` and `taker`) notes, on the harvester's account, each multiplayer harvest that counted for the shooter: the animal as the harvester's game knows it (`individual`, the harvester's latest event 7 of it), the shooter's account and his kill, so the trophy shot the harvester takes of it is the shooter's ([The harvester's trophy shot](#multiplayer)); it goes with either account, and moves with an account that is given another user id.

`purchases` (schema 46, made at every start when missing, guarded by the table alone) ties an inventory copy to what it cost, so it can be sold back ([Selling back](#selling-back)): the copy's `guid` and `item_id`, the currency (`em` or `gm`) and the price paid of that copy (a copy bought alone), or `restored` 1 with `paid` 0 (a copy a restore or an archive claim brought). It goes with its account, moves with an account that is given another user id, and a restore clears it with the items.

A request's account comes from its token. The tokens it carries are tried in this order, the first that resolves wins: the `X-Hun-Session` header (game host), `Authorization: Bearer` (API v2 host), `oauth_access_token` in the form or the query (API host). A token that is unknown, expired or of a banned account does not resolve. When none does, the request is the local account's if `offline_mode` is true, else it is refused: `{"errorCode": 5, "message": "token expired"}` with status 200 on the API host, status 401 on the game host (`{"err": 1}`) and on the API v2 host. A CORS preflight carries no token and is answered by the replay. Photo links name the account (`/photos/<account id>/<file>`) and need no token; the kill details page finds the account by the handle in its URL (on a local server any handle is the local account). The `admin` commands and the admin API act on the local account.

The database is in WAL mode with `synchronous=NORMAL` and a 5 s busy timeout, connections are pooled, every transaction starts with `BEGIN IMMEDIATE`, and the schema and its migrations run once at startup under `PRAGMA user_version`. A database call is scoped to one account: the SQL names it as `$a`, and a statement that touches a per-player table without it throws, so a forgotten filter fails in the tests and never reads another player's rows.

## Scoring and paying a harvest

A harvest (heartbeat event 3) is scored from the kill's spawn (event 7), its trophy measurement (event 22: the latest one after that spawn, since individual ids are reused across hunts and a female deer sends none, so a doe never takes the measurements of an earlier buck) and its hits (event 1). In a multiplayer session the harvest is the last shooter's, not always the harvester's ([Multiplayer](#multiplayer)). The next heartbeat carries update 6 with the score (`score_text` "Score: <score>", or "No score"), the kill's `css` and its kill id, and the kill's gm$ comes with update 15 (`CSS Reward`): `round(css)`, paid when it is above 0.

- **Disqualified** (`score_text` "Disqualified ", `score` -1, `trophy_shot` 0, no gm$, the usual notice) only when a hit used an ammunition that is not permitted on the animal (`State.AmmoCheck`): an animal possessed by a seasonal event permits only that event's list (its silver ammunition); a normal animal its species' permitted list (the connect's `ethical`, by species id; the non-typical deer use their species' list), which holds the event's silver only for the event's creatures (`species_rules`). Which kind an animal is: [Seasonal events](#seasonal-events). A species with an empty list, the dogs, has no rule. Every reader of a kill goes through that one check: the heartbeat's warning and a hit's skill XP, the score, the missions, the challenges, the daily missions, a multiplayer harvest credited to its shooter, `admin rescore`, `admin ethics`, the statistics, the hunterscore, the kill and hunt pages and the taxidermy.
- **The score** is one formula per family of the facts file's `families`, from the measurements below. The pheasant, the ducks, the geese and the ptarmigans get score type "A" when the last hit's animal state was flying and "G" otherwise; a tusked kill gets "T" when the tusks outweigh the weight and "W" otherwise.
- **The css** is `round(css_multiplier x 100 x (score - min_score) / (max_score - min_score), 1)` with the species' row of the facts file's `variants` (the operator's `variants_file` rows win); there is no clamp. A species with no family has no score, and one with no row has a score and no gm$ (none today): the dogs 22, 32 and 48, the sasquatch 31 and the t-rex 56.

| Family | Species | Score from | A female |
|---|---|---|---|
| `weight_lb` | coyote 4, red fox 14, arctic fox 23, werewolf 50, wendigo 58 | the weight in pounds | scores |
| `weight_mg` | the rabbits and hares 17, 20, 25, the ducks, geese and ptarmigans 15, 16, 27, 28, 29, 33, 34, 35, 47, 51 | the weight in grams (event 7 `feature`, in milligrams) | scores |
| `weight_kg`, `weight_g` | red kangaroo 21; roebit 53 and wood grouse 57 | the weight in kilograms; the weight in grams | scores |
| `skull` | bears 9, 13, 30, 37, wolf 36, bobcat 43, lynx 49, puma 52 | skull width plus length in inches (event 7 `feature`) | scores |
| `tusks` | feral hog 5, wild boar 10 | the weight in pounds, plus the tusk circumferences, plus 50 times the tusk lengths | a sow has no tusks: her weight |
| `turkey` | turkey 2 | the weight in pounds, plus 10 times the spurs, plus 2 times the beard (inches) | no score |
| `pheasant` | pheasant 7 | the tail plus the spurs in inches (event 22 `tail`, `left_spur`, `right_spur`) | the tail |
| `bc_deer` | whitetail 0, mule deer 1, blacktail 6, sitka 26, and the non-typical 100, 101, 106 | B&C typical or non-typical total | no antlers: no score |
| `elk` | Roosevelt 3, Rocky Mountain 38 | the typical total plus the crown points of both antlers (event 22 `crown_points` lengths) | no score |
| `moose`, `cic_roe`, `cic_red`, `cic_fallow`, `tines` | moose 8; roe 11, red 12, fallow 55; rusa 44, sambar 45, axis 54 | the family's total from the antlers | no antlers: no score |
| `caribou`, `bovid`, `sheep`, `cic_horn` | reindeer 18; bison 24, water buffalo 42, banteng 46; bighorn 39, Dall 41; alpine ibex 19, feral goat 40 | the family's total from the horns | scores when the animal carries horns (event 22 has a main-beam point) |

A kill whose family needs a measurement the heartbeat lacks (a skull with no `feature`, a deer without its event 22 or without a main-beam point) has no score.

**Checked against the original.** `server/Server.Tests/score-vectors.json` holds 1,233 score sheets recorded from the original, one per line: `{species, gender, features, weight, state, score, type}` (the measurements as `Scoring.Score` takes them, the weight in grams, the last hit's animal state, the original's score and score type; the non-typical deer as 100, 101 and 106). A male of every scored species (4 to 39 sheets each) and a female of 35. The other 22 have no female sheet: the 17 females without a score, and the pheasant, wild boar, Dall sheep, water buffalo and roebit, whose female scores. Left out of the file: 8 sheets (5 `bc_deer`, 2 `tines`, 1 `cic_horn`) whose stored score is not the total their own worked sheet prints; the formula gives the printed total, so a new export must leave them out again. `ScoreVectorTests` scores every row and compares at four decimals, with the precision the sheets drop allowed for: 0.001 for `weight_mg`, `weight_kg`, `caribou`, `cic_horn`, `cic_roe`, `moose` and `sheep`, 1.0 for `weight_g`; its output states the coverage. A change to a formula must keep it green.

The bounds report of `admin rescore` is the check for the five females with no recorded sheet (pheasant, wild boar, Dall sheep, water buffalo, roebit): after a rescore, a female far outside her species' bounds shows there.

## Seasonal events

The game build learns of an event from the connect reply's `ingame_event`: `{name, define, reserves}`, or `null` for none. It reads it once per start of the game, so an event never changes a hunt that is running. `reserves` are the game's own reserve numbers (the lodge id / 100, the launch map's reserve id minus 1); an empty list is every reserve.

**The operator's switch** is the `event` of settings.json: `{"name": "halloween_2026", "define": "HALLOWEEN_2026", "reserves": [0, 1, 2, 8, 9], "start": "2026-10-22T00:00:00Z", "end": "2026-11-10T18:00:00Z"}`. `name` (lower case letters, digits and `_`) and `define` (upper case) are the build's own names of the event, `start` and `end` a date and time with an offset (`Z` is UTC), `end` after `start`. The server checks it when it starts and refuses a wrong one. From `start` to `end` (exclusive) the connect reply carries the object; outside it, or with no `event`, `ingame_event` is `null` as it always was. A new or changed `event` needs a restart; the window opens and closes by itself.

**What the event adds** to a player's connect reply, on the reserves of `reserves` for which the facts file has an entry (`events.<name>.reserves.<game reserve number>`), while it runs:

- its `populations`: the event creatures, each on a density map the game ships, with `active_start` and `active_end` the event's window;
- its `ammo` as the `species_ammo` of every species of the reserve (the game uses that list only for a hint);
- its permitted ammunition: its `ethical` carries the event's list for the species the list names (below).

**The event's permitted ammunition, judged per animal.** During the event the original sent, on its reserves, a permitted-ammunition list (`ethical`) in which the species of its five reserves and the werewolf and wendigo permit only the three silver ammunitions. The facts file holds the list as `events.<name>.ethical` (`{ammo, species, formerly}`): the shipped `halloween_2026` names the species that have a possessed version (0 to 3, 5, 7 to 9, 14, 17, 20, 21, 23 to 26, 30, 40, 41, 43, 51 and 54) and the creatures 50 and 58, and the ammunitions 962, 963 and 1142. The coyote 4 and the blacktail deer 6 are not in it: the game has no possessed version of them (their event coats only mark an event session). `formerly` (4 and 6) lists the species the list also named before schema 41, when a hunt with the event permitted silver only on them too; only `admin ethics` reads it, to know the rule a stored kill was made under (`unscored`). `events.<name>.creatures` lists the event's creatures (50, 58), and `events.<name>.coats` (`{<species>: {possessed: [coat], normal: [coat]}}`) the coats (a sighting's `texture`, event 7) that prove a possessed or a normal animal of a species. A coat seen in both kinds of session is in neither list: the snowshoe hare's coat 73 (normal hares wear it too; its entry lists its normal coats 0 and 2 only), the feral hog's base coats (its entry lists the five event coats only), the female axis deer's coats (the entry lists the male event coat 43 only; the normal coats are shared by both sexes, so none is listed); the European rabbit has no entry (it keeps its normal coat in the event).

A kill is judged by what its animal was (`State.KindOf`), read from its sighting: the latest event 7 of its individual before the harvest. A harvest credited to its shooter ([Multiplayer](#multiplayer)) whose own events lack the sighting is judged by the harvester's, on every path: its score, its page (made from that sighting), the missions, the challenges, `admin rescore`, `admin mp-harvests` and `admin ethics` all read the sighting its page was made from (`State.StoredSpawn`). A stored kill is read by the species and coat of its page (the kill and hunt pages, the statistics, the hunterscore, the taxidermy and `admin ethics`).

1. an event's creature: its own list (`species_rules`: the three silver ammunitions, or what a recording holds), in any hunt;
2. a species no event's list names (the coyote and the blacktail deer among them): a normal animal, in any hunt;
3. a coat in the species' `possessed` list: possessed, in any hunt; in its `normal` list: normal, in any hunt;
4. otherwise (the hare's 73, any European rabbit, a hog's base coat, any female axis deer, a coat in no list, a sighting without one): possessed when the hunt of its harvest has an event whose list names the species, normal when it has none; in a hunt the schema-41 step could not decide (`expeditions.event_undecided`, below) the kill keeps the verdict it was made under: its species' list and the event's silver. A coat neither list of its species names (a whitetail deer's coat 6, for one) proves nothing and follows the hunt the same way;
5. a possessed animal permits the event's list only; a normal animal its species' list, with no silver. One hit outside it makes the kill unethical, also when the last hit is permitted. In multiplayer the account's own hits count, as for any kill.

The heartbeat's "Not permitted" toast and inbox message and a hit's skill XP follow the same decision at the hit (a hit's sighting is stored before it). A hit on an animal the player never saw (a shooter whose game sent no sighting of it) has no species, so it gets no toast and no rule at the hit; its harvest is judged by the harvester's sighting. The rule reads no reserve and no date: a coat proves the kind, and the hunt's event is set only where and when the event ran for the player. The data is checked when the server starts, as the rest of `facts.json` (`Facts`): a coat in both lists of a species, a `coats` key that is not a species id written as a plain number (`"05"` is refused), a `coats` entry that is not an object or that is for a species the event's `ethical.species` does not name (or for a creature), an id that is no whole number or no species of `animals`, a `coats` entry whose two lists are both empty, an empty `ethical.ammo` or `ethical.species` stop the start with an error naming the entry. A `coats` entry may leave out one of its lists: a missing list is an empty one.

**The hunt's event** (`expeditions.event`, schema 39) is decided once, by the hunt's first connect: the event's name when that connect gets the event's content (the event runs, the player plays seasonal events, the hunt's reserve has an entry), else `''` (none); a later connect of the same hunt (the game started again inside it) never changes it, and the hunt keeps it to its end, after the event's window closes, the player changes the setting or the server restarts. Every connect of the hunt sends the hunt's list in its `ethical`: with the event, its silver for the species the event's list names (the possessed versions and the creatures); without it the normal lists, so no silver but the creatures'. So the game's hints agree with how the hunt's animals whose coat proves nothing are judged (the game uses that list only for its own hints and counters). A tutorial opens no hunt, so its events follow the latest hunt's: starting one marks that hunt (`expeditions.event_until`, the last event row its event judges), and the tutorial and anything after it in that hunt fall back on no event (its connect sends the normal lists).

**In a multiplayer game the event is the host's** (#996). The host's game creates the hunt's animals, so a game has one event setting, the host's: a player who joins a session as a guest gets the host's decision whatever his own "Play seasonal events" setting is, and his own setting applies only to his single-player hunts and to the games he hosts. A guest's first connect takes the event the host's hunt recorded (`expeditions.event` and `event_sent` of the host's open hunt, below) and records it as his own hunt's decision; his reply then carries `ingame_event`, the event's populations and ammunition and the event's list in `ethical` exactly when the host's did, and his kills of animals whose coat proves nothing are judged by that event (above). When the host's hunt has no decision yet (the host has not connected), the server decides as it would for the host's own connect: the host's setting, the event's window and the reserve, never the guest's setting. The guest's hunt keeps the decision to its end, as every hunt does (below): the window closing, the host's or the guest's setting changing, a server restart, the session ending and a reconnect of the game change nothing. His next hunt, a single-player one or a game he hosts, decides by his own setting again. A host's own hunt is unchanged. **The record says what was sent** (#1032, schema 51). A hunt's first connect records two things: `expeditions.event`, the event's name when that connect sent the event's content (the event runs, the player plays it, the reserve has an entry) and `''` when it did not, and `expeditions.event_sent`, the event's name when it sent the event itself (`ingame_event`: the event runs and the player plays it, with or without content on that reserve), `''` when it sent none. `event_sent` is added by a step that runs at every start, guarded by the column; a hunt decided before it (`NULL`) sent the event itself exactly when it sent content, so its `event` is read for both. **Every connect of a hunt that already has its record sends what it says**, for a host, a guest and a single-player hunt alike: `ingame_event` when `event_sent` names the configured event, and the event's populations and ammunition when the reserve has content; never what the player's current setting, the event's window or the session say. So the host's own game that starts again inside his hunt (after the window closed or after he changed his setting), a guest's after the session ended or the server restarted (that connect is a single-player one, but it reads his hunt's record, not his own setting) and a reconnect inside the session send the same as the hunt's first connect, and a host and his guests agree for the whole hunt. On a reserve where the event has no content the hunt's record is `event` `''` and `event_sent` the event's name, for the host and for every guest: all get the event itself and no animal groups and no silver, on every connect, and the games list gives such a game its `event`, the one its guests get (`event_differs` compares the same: what the caller's own hunt would send). **Community missions** (a kill's count toward an event's community mission) follow the same record: a kill counts when the hunt's `event_sent` (else `event`) is the running event, the host's for a multiplayer hunt, whatever the player's setting is now, and not for the events after a tutorial started in the hunt (`event_until`, as the kills' judging reads it); a hunt with no record (no connect yet) counts for a player who plays events. Every other path (the kills' judging, the statistics, the games list) reads the recorded decision too.

**Hunts from before the rule (schema 41).** The hunts before schema 39 have `''`. The schema-41 step (guarded by the `unethical_kills` table, so it runs once; one range of event rows per hunt) gives every closed hunt recorded `''` the event its own sightings prove (`Facts.Proves`: a creature, or a coat of a `possessed` list), and marks it `expeditions.event_backfilled` (so a hunt whose first connect recorded the event can be told from one the step set): the event when at least one sighting proves it and none proves a normal animal (a coat of a `normal` list) or another event; otherwise the hunt stays `''` (no deciding sighting, or a mix). A hunt with no deciding sighting at all, on a reserve the event has an entry for, that ended after the first hunt with the event started, also gets `expeditions.event_undecided` (the event's name): its kills whose coat proves nothing keep the verdict they were made under (rule 4), on every path, and `admin ethics` flags those the rule could judge otherwise. The database cannot tell a hunt from before schema 39 from a later one whose first connect recorded `''`, so the step reads every closed hunt recorded `''` alike. It then clears the stored statistics of the closed hunts that hold a kill, so the start-up's `UpdateHunterscore` of every account stores them again by the rule, and records when it ran and the last event row before it (`schema_marks`, version 41: the kills made before the rule, `admin ethics`). **Stored once and recomputed:** a kill's `ethical` (hunt page, kill page), the open hunt's statistics and the challenges are computed on every read and follow at once; the closed hunts' statistics (`expeditions.stats`), the profile's ethical counts, the hunterscore and the Discord linked-role value follow at that start. The step writes no score, harvest page, wallet row, trophy, lodge, mission, challenge, daily mission or skill, and a second start changes nothing. The start-up recount can raise an achievement counter when an account's recounted statistics are higher than what it had (achievements are awarded from the closed hunts' statistics at every start): it never lowers one or changes an earn time. A stored kill's score, board place and taxidermy follow the rule only through `admin ethics` ([The ammunition rule on stored kills](#the-ammunition-rule-on-stored-kills)); missions, challenges, daily missions and skills already completed or paid stay.

**Deploying schema 41.** The first start already moves the hunterscores, the profile counts, the pages' verdicts, the challenges' progress (read on every read), the Discord linked-role value (pushed at the next sweep) and the achievement counters the recount raises (they can rise, never fall; no earn time changes), while stored scores, leaderboard rows and personal bests stay until `admin ethics --apply`, and taxidermy is refused at once for a kill the rule disqualifies. So the operator runs the dry run on a copy of `state.db` first, compares it, deploys, runs the dry run and `--apply` right after the deploy (players may keep hunting: [The ammunition rule on stored kills](#the-ammunition-rule-on-stored-kills)), then restarts the server so the boards show the change at once rather than within their cache time. On a copy of the community server's database the first start took about 45 to 57 seconds, and the dry run and the apply about two and a half minutes each. Rolling back needs the database backup taken before the deploy: an older server refuses a database of schema 41.

A reserve of the event without an entry gets only `ingame_event`, and so does every reserve outside the event's `reserves`. The shipped `halloween_2026` has an entry for each of its five reserves. What the game does by itself when it reads the event is the same on all five, and the server sends nothing for it: the clock is frozen at night (1.25 h; 4.7 h on reserve 8), the weather is harsher (a default severity of 1.25, and Whiterime Ridge has its own, stronger weather block), the lodges are decorated, the ambient sounds change, and the event's own animal files give the species of the reserve their event coats and behaviour. The creature rows are the original's, index, group sizes, random groups and radius included; only the window is the server's (`active_start` and `active_end` are the `event`'s `start` and `end`, never the original's):

| Game reserve (launch map) | Event creatures the server adds | Why this reserve |
|---|---|---|
| 0 (1, Whitehart Island) | a feral hog (index 3: 12 males and 24 females, no random groups, beside the normal Roosevelt elk 3) and a werewolf (9: 9 and 16, random groups 22 and 36, radius 15) | the game ships a werewolf and a feral hog density map for it |
| 1 (2, Logger's Point) | a werewolf (110: 18 and 30, no random groups) | the game ships a werewolf density map for it; its feral hog is the normal one |
| 2 (3, Settler Creeks) | a werewolf (208: 9 and 16, random groups 22 and 36, radius 15) | the same |
| 8 (9, Bushrangers Run) | a werewolf (805: no group, random groups 25 and 25, radius 30) | the same |
| 9 (10, Whiterime Ridge) | a werewolf (908) and a wendigo (909), both on the snowshoe hare's map, and a feral hog (914) | the game ships no werewolf map for it, so both monsters use the snowshoe hare's, as the event's own rows do; the sizes are the event's |

A creature is only ever put on a density map the game ships for that reserve (a missing map fails the hunt): the werewolf's maps are the game's reserves 0, 1, 2, 8 and 10, the feral hog's 0, 1, 2, 6, 8, 9 and 11. The rows of all five reserves are the original's. All five entries carry the three silver ammunitions, the ones reserve 9 carries, as the `species_ammo` of every species the reserve lists. The event has nothing else for any of them: no seasonal collectable has a `count_per_area` above 0 in its connect replies, and `ingame_store` is the same. A connect recorded during an event may carry its creatures; the server removes the rows the facts file lists for any event (matched by index and creature, as the original's feral hog 3 on reserve 0 shares its index with the normal Roosevelt elk, which stays) before it adds the running one's, so a player who does not play events never gets them.

**Scoring and paying an event creature** is the same as for any species ([Scoring and paying a harvest](#scoring-and-paying-a-harvest)). A werewolf or a wendigo scores by its weight in pounds (the family `weight_lb`, like the coyote) with `min_score` 190, `max_score` 390 and `css_multiplier` 1 (the facts file's `variants`), and a kill with a hit of any ammunition outside the species' permitted ammunition is disqualified. The permitted ammunition of both is the three silver ones (the connect's `ethical`, which is also what the game is sent). The facts file's `species_rules` give that list when the recorded connect lists no ammunition for them; what the recording itself holds wins. Silver is permitted otherwise only on the event's possessed animals (above).

**The player's setting**, "Play seasonal events", is the account's `seasonal_events` column (schema 17), on by default. Off, that player's connect has `ingame_event: null` and none of the content above, and so has a tutorial's. `GET /account/events` and `POST /account/events` (the account routes below) read and set it; the launcher's Settings menu, launch map and tutorials page call them ([launcher.md](launcher.md#seasonal-events)). The same `GET` tells the launcher which event runs, so it can show the event's picture of a reserve ([images.md](images.md)).

**The event's missions** are in the facts file, `events.<name>`: `title` (the panel's heading), `missions` (the catalogue's ids of the event's personal missions), `items` (the event's store item ids) and `community` (`[{id, title, target, gm, em}]`). A personal mission is an ordinary mission: the player activates it and the mission engine ([Missions](#missions)) counts it. The shipped `halloween_2026` lists the mission 1201 ("Progressive Pursuits", 30 objectives on the event's creatures) and as store items the three Wolfsbane weapons (960, 967, 1143) and the three silver ammunitions (962, 963, 1142). A personal mission or store item the server's catalogue lacks is left out of the panel. Its `community` is empty: the numbers of its community missions are not known, so it has none until the file lists them.

**Community missions** are shared objectives of the event. `title` is read like a mission objective's (`Harvest ...`, `Spot ...`, `Locate ... tracks`, `Arrive ...`: [Missions](#missions)), `target` is how many the original asked of all its players, and the server asks `ceil(target / event_community_divisor)` (default 20, [settings.json](#run-it)), because the community is far smaller. While the event runs, each hunt event of a player who plays events that meets a mission counts 1 for that player in the table `community_progress` (schema 19: event, mission id, account, count, paid), until the sum over all players reaches the divided target; after that nothing counts. A player who opted out counts nothing and sees no panel. When the sum is reached, every player with a count is paid the mission's `gm` and `em` once through `AddCurrency` ("gm$ community reward", wallet history), at their own next heartbeat (the reply's update 15 carries the new balance, a notice follows): one heartbeat changes only its own player's wallet, and `paid` makes it once. A contributor who does not send a heartbeat before the operator switches the event off is not paid.

**The event panel** is `GET /account/events/panel` (the account routes below): for a player who plays seasonal events while an event runs, `{name, title, start, end, community, contributors, missions, items}`, otherwise `{"event": null}` (401 when signed out). `start` and `end` are unix seconds; `community` is `[{id, title, progress, target, gm, em}]` with the divided `target` and `progress` at most that; `contributors` the 10 players (not banned) with the most counts, `[{handle, count}]`; `missions` the player's personal missions, `[{id, title, state, objectives: [{title, done}], reward}]` with `state` `active`, `available`, `completed` or `locked`; `items` `[{id, name}]`. The launcher and the website show it on the dashboard ([launcher.md](launcher.md#seasonal-events)). The game's own screens show no panel: the game asks nothing of an event beyond the connect reply's `ingame_event`, and `Me/poll` and `Application/application` keep `campaign_stylesheet`, `community_campaign_stylesheet` and `active_campaign` false.

## Dog level rewards

A dog's level rewards are paid from its heartbeat event 23 for an owned dog item, which comes every 60 s and carries the dog's cumulative `experience`. Each connect `dog_skills` row whose `xp` the stored experience (missing: 0) is below and the event's is at or above adds the `SILVER` amounts of its `rewards` unlocks to the `gm` wallet, and the heartbeat reply carries one update 15 with the new balance. The stored experience only grows (an event whose `experience` is not a number leaves it as it is) (`max` of both), so a repeated or lower value pays nothing, and a dog restored or bought with a high experience pays only for levels crossed afterwards. The ribbon trophy and the skills are not paid: the game and the website show them from the experience.

## Missions

On a community server `Mission/missions` and the calls the missions page sends answer from the catalogue's `missions[]` and `mission_groups[]` (the recorded application reply: without a recording there are no missions, and every call keeps the empty reply of [the table above](#recordings-on-a-community-server)) and from the account's rows (schema 13): `missions` has a row for every active (state 2) or completed (3) mission with the ids of its finished objectives in the order they were finished, `mission_prefs` has the account's three switches and favourite mission packs. A row also remembers (schema 30) `since`, the last event before the mission was activated here (null for a row of an earlier schema), `seen`, the events after which its harvests are checked, `earlier`, the objectives finished before `seen`, and `kept`, the objectives a restore brought (`*` for a mission completed before the restore) or a harvest of another player's kill finished ([Multiplayer](#multiplayer)); since schema 35 also `rules`, a JSON object from a finished objective's id to a fingerprint of its rule when it was finished (an objective finished before has none), which the [audit](#missions) reads. A local server replays its recordings and tracks nothing. Every call needs the player's own token.

| Call (form `oauth_access_token` and) | Answer |
|---|---|
| `POST /v1/Mission/missions` | `{active, completed, available, states, auto_mission_activate, activate_only_favourite, hide_completed_favourite, favourites}`. `available` is derived: every dependency completed, not active, not completed. `states` has an entry for every id listed and for no other: `{state: 1}`, `{state: 2, objectives: [finished ids]}`, `{state: 3}` |
| `POST /v1/Mission/activate`, `mission_id` | `true` once the mission is active; `false` for a mission that is not available (unknown, a dependency open, already started) |
| `POST /v1/Mission/activate_all_available` | `true`: every available mission becomes active |
| `POST /v1/Mission/update_automatic_activation` (`auto_mission_activate`), `update_activate_only_favourite` (`activate_only_favourite`), `update_hide_completed_favourite` (`hide_completed_favourite`), `true` or `false` | `true`; the switch is stored |
| `POST /v1/Mission/update_favourite`, `group_id`, `is_favourite` | `true`; the group is added to or removed from the favourites. `false` for a group id that is no number |

**The website.** The missions page reads the same rows through `GET /account/missions` and writes through `POST /account/missions/<call>`, the six calls above ([Upload a backup zip](#upload-a-backup-zip)): the session cookie names the account, and a write is the very handler of the game's call, so the two surfaces always show one state. The page's titles, texts, rewards and filters come from the definitions, so it needs the same recording. A mission that is `locked` is one whose dependencies are not all completed (a pack's opener no longer needs the Whitetail Deer missions 0, 1 and 2, the community's decision: players are not beginners; the dependencies inside a pack stay; missions 0, 1 and 2 need no order either, and the game's catalogue reply (`client_application`) drops those ids from every `dependencies` list too, so the in-game page agrees; with `auto_mission_activate` on, finishing 0, 1 or 2 no longer activates the openers: the player activates them, with Activate or Activate All); its reply carries `needs`, and the page says which missions to finish first. `objectives` are the titles and `objectives_done` one flag per objective, true when the account's row holds that objective (every one of a completed mission); the page ticks it. The flags are a field of their own so a launcher of an earlier release, which reads `objectives` as titles, keeps working. The in-game page needs nothing more: it works the same out of the definitions' `dependencies` and lists the titles of a locked mission under "complete the following to unlock".

**Progress.** The game keeps no mission state and a definition has no condition field: the English title of an objective is turned into a rule when the server starts (`State.LoadMissions`). A leading `Then`, `Finally`, `First`, `Lastly` or `After ...` (up to its comma or colon: `After that,`, `After spotting the Wolves,`) makes the objective ordered (it counts only when every objective before it is finished). `At any time,` and `At any point while in <reserve>,` in front of a title say nothing more and are taken off before the first word is read; the reserve is then read as `in <reserve>` at the end of the title. So is `At any point during the same hunt,`, except in a mission with a `Start at ...` objective before it: only that wording waits for that objective (printed `after objective N`; in a mission played in one hunt, `singleExpedition`, it is the hunt that started at the lodge: the reader does not check the hunt itself, see the conditions below). The first word gives the kind: `Harvest`, `Take` or `Kill` (heartbeat event 3), `Spot` (event 11), `Locate`, `ID`, `Identify`, `Find` or `Register` with a clue word (`tracks`, `droppings`, `call`, `blood`, ... event 12), `Arrive`, `Visit`, `Walk`, `Head to`, `Head over to`, `Head out to`, `Travel to`, `Go to` or `Make it to` (event 101 with `direction` `enter`) and `Start` or `Begin at` (a hunt starts at the lodge, `Launch/start`). The species named in the title (the catalogue's longest species name found in it; a variant counts as its species) and the gender (`male`, `buck`, `bull`, `stag`, `rooster`, `tom`, `ram`; `female`, `doe`, `cow`, `hen`, `ewe`) must match the animal's event 7 (event 3's `extended_species` for a harvest). Titles, names and defines are compared in lower case without accents or punctuation (a title's `Zurnhutte` is the catalogue's `Zürnhütte`; the server runs with invariant globalization, where `string.Normalize` leaves every letter as it is, so `State.Letters` maps the Latin letters itself). A place matches by name when its words (the event's `location_name` or the lodge's name or define, without its leading reserve words and trailing number, down to two words) are in the title. An arrive objective whose title gives no coordinates is also met by the catalogue's place whose English name the title holds as whole words, in the mission's `reserves` (a mission that lists none: any reserve), when the player enters it: the game sends the place's `define` (`REGION_EAST_LODGE` for `Test East Lodge`), whose words the title does not hold. When the title names several places (`the Upper Test Camp` holds `Test Camp` too) only the longest name counts; `coverage --rules` prints them as `places: <defines>`. An arrive objective whose title gives no coordinates is also met, when the hunt is in one of the mission's `reserves` (a mission that lists none: any reserve), by a place whose name matches less strictly: without the game's location prefix (`ProtocolLiterals.LocationPrefix`), with or without a final `s` of its last word (`quillon fall` meets `..._QUILLON_FALLS`), or by its last word alone (`zorbel` meets `REGION_FROZEN_ZORBEL`), unless that word, singular or plural, is a common one (`lodge`, `tower`, `campsite`, `camp`, `site`, `point`, `bay`, `lake`, `pond`, `creek`, `river`, `falls`, `fall`, `hill`, `pass`, `island`, `bridge`, `road`, `area`, `north`, `south`, `east`, `west`), has fewer than 4 letters or is a number. A title that gives coordinates (`(x -4633, y -11152)`; the colons, the brackets and the first `x` are optional; the title's x is the map's x and its y the map's z; a number with a fraction below 100 is in thousands of metres) is not matched less strictly by name, nor by the name of a catalogue place (it may name a place beside the one it means: `Test Hill Watchtower`); it is met in those reserves by any `enter` event (event 101, whose `player_x` and `player_z` are the position) within 200 m of that point, whatever the place's name; `coverage --rules` prints it as `within 200 m of (x, z)`. An arrive objective of `mission_rules.json` (`arrive; place: NAME`) is met by that name in any reserve and, when the title has coordinates, as such a title is. A start (`Start at ...`) matches by the strict name only. Each event finishes the first unfinished objective it meets of every active mission that was activated before the event. A mission with `singleExpedition` forgets its finished objectives when a hunt starts. A harvest, a spot and a clue are read further, as the next paragraphs say; an arrival is read by its words and coordinates and a start by its words only (`singleplayer` and time limits are not checked); an objective whose first word is none of the above never finishes.

A catalogue place is named by the title only when the name is the whole of the place's name in the title: a name that a capitalised word of the title continues or follows (`Test Hill Watchtower` where the catalogue lists `Test Hill` only, `Fort Test Hill`) is not the place the title means, so the objective is met by no place and `coverage --rules` prints it as not understood (`no place of the catalogue matches it`), unless a less strict name match below meets it. A comma, a full stop or a bracket ends a name: `Visit the Test Lake, Test Hill` and `Arrive at Test Hill (Test Reserve)` name `Test Hill`. The words `the`, `at`, `in`, `lodge`, `reserve` and the verbs that open a title do not count as a continuing name. A mission that lists no reserves takes the name in every reserve.

**Announcements and pay.** A finished objective that is not the last queues `update 3` `Mission "<mission title>", objective "<objective title>" completed.` for the next heartbeat. The last one completes the mission: its `N gm$` and `N em$` rewards are paid through `State.AddCurrency` (so each has a wallet row, `gm$ reward` or `em$ reward`), `update 15` with the new balance goes into the reply of the heartbeat that carried the event, and `update 3` `Mission <title> completed.` into the next one (after the harvest's trophy score). Item and trophy rewards (only the event mission lists any) are not delivered. The missions it unlocks become active when `auto_mission_activate` is set, or, with `activate_only_favourite`, when they are in a favourite mission pack.

**Restored states.** The saved `Mission_missions.json` (`extras`) is copied into the rows at restore and, for an account restored before, on its first mission call: `completed`, `active` with the finished objective ids of `states`, the three switches and `favourites`; `available` is not stored. Ids without a definition and favourite mission packs that no longer exist are kept as they are (they unlock nothing and cannot complete). A restore replaces the rows with the zip's, or with none.

**Conditions of a harvest.** The rest of the title is read for what it asks of the kill, from the events of the animal since its spawn (event 7): its hits (events 1, the last one is the killing hit) and its harvest (event 3). Every condition the title names must hold. A sentence that only narrates (`Other organs may be hit`, `to blame it for making the noise`) or says nothing (`on any map`) is taken out and decides nothing.

| Words of the title | Checked against |
|---|---|
| a weapon's name (`using the .22 Air Rifle`), a class (`any shotgun`, `any bow or crossbow`; a bare `bow` is no crossbow) with the name words before it (`any 12 GA Shotgun`, `a Compound Bow`) | the last hit's `weapon_id` |
| a weapon's name (the whole name, or all its words in any order before a class noun: `a Test Ember Compound Bow`) | every member of the named weapon's [weapon family](#weapon-families): the finishes of the name, the variants the game gives another name (an engraved twin, the `Test Magnum Revolver` of the `Test Revolver`, the sawed-off of the semi-automatic shotgun of one maker). `A or B` takes the union of the two families. The words of a class (`any handgun`, `any semi-auto shotgun`, `any .243 rifle`: a class noun with words that are no whole name) name no weapon and stay the class's weapons |
| a weapon's name whose words all lie in a longer weapon's name (`Test Revolver` and `Test Magnum Revolver`) | also the longer name's weapons (the shorter name's words in order, each with its family) and the weapons the mission's description names; a title naming the longer name stays exact |
| an ammunition's name (`using .17 HMR HV ammunition`, `Slug Shells`, `a .243 ammunition`) | the last hit's `ammunition_id` |
| `permitted` or `ethical` ammunition | no hit of the kill was not permitted (the kill is not disqualified) |
| `with one shot`, `single shot` | the animal has exactly one hit |
| `double lung shot`, `lung`, `heart`, `heart or lungs`, `brain` | the bits of the last hit's `organ2` (heart 1, right lung 2, left lung 4, brain 16; double lung needs both) |
| `at 100% Harvest Value` | event 3's `harvest_value` is 100000 |
| `over`, `under`, `at least`, `at most`, `a maximum of`, `N or more`, `between A and B` metres | the last hit's `distance_cms`; the feet and pounds in brackets are not read |
| `a score of N or higher`, `scoring more than N`, `N point score` | the harvest's score (the one its update 6 shows); a kill with no score meets none |
| `at least 8 typical points`, `more than N` / `less than N typical points`, `at least ten typical points`, `a 12-point Elk` (at least 12) | the typical points of both antlers added: the `points` of the trophy event's (22) `left_antler` and `right_antler`, the main beam and the tines, as the score sheet counts them, not the abnormal points. The trophy event is the animal's own: the latest one of its individual after its spawn, in its hunt and before the individual's next spawn, one sent after the harvest included (the start finishes the objective then). A kill with no trophy event, a trophy with a side's list missing, a non-typical rack (`extended_species` 100 and up) and a female never meet it; `buck`, `bull` and the other male words need a male |
| a weight in `kg` or `lbs` with `at least`, `more than`, `or lower` ... | event 7's `weight_grams` |
| `airborne`, `standing`, `walking`, `running`, `moving`, `charging`, `fleeing` | the last hit's `animal_state` (`while standing` is the player's pose, not this; flying 6 and 11, standing 3, walking 4, running 5, moving 4, 5 and 10, charging 9, fleeing 5, 6, 10 and 15) |
| `unspooked`, `spooked` | the animal's first hit's `animal_state` (calm: 1 to 4, 7, 8, 11, 12; fleeing: 5, 6, 10, 15, 16). A bird in the air is 11 while it is not spooked and 6 once it is: the game's own names of the two are `FLYING_NOT_SPOOKED` and `FLYING`. For the animals on the ground it is an approximation: the data has no threat state, only the animal's state at its first hit |
| `from a Tree Stand`, `Hunting Tower`, `Ground Blind`, `Waterfowl Blind`, `Tripod`, `Shooting Tripod Rest` (also a list of them); `without a tower, blind, ...` | the last hit's `location` (tower 1, tree stand 2, ground blind 3, tripod 4, waterfowl blind 5, rifle rest 6; 0 for none). A spot reads its own `location`, which carries the stand as a hit's does |
| `from Tower 8` (with the reserve in the title or, without, the mission's `reserves`; a note in brackets after it, `(the central tower)`, or its place, `(x 8504, y -25)`, which must be the tower's within 50 m) | a tower stand as above, and the player in the place of that tower: the catalogue's hunting tower of that name in that reserve, whose `define` the game names when the player is at the tower (event 101, below). A number no tower of the reserve has, or other coordinates, leave it not understood |
| `without a scope`, `iron sights`; `scoped` | the last hit's `scope_id` is -1; is not |
| `in <reserve>` | the reserve of the hunt the harvest happened in |
| a quoted name (`"Patient Zero"`) | an animal the server places ([Named animals](#missions), with `spawn_named_animals`): the harvest or the spot of an animal of its population, at any distance. Otherwise, with the title's coordinates: an animal of the species (the title's, else the mission's own list) and gender within 1000 m of them (the last hit's `x` and `z`, else the spawn's: metres in the reserve's map frame, the title's `X` and `Y` being the map's `x` and `z`; the 1000 m are a guess, a title's place has no extent), together with the title's other conditions; event 7's `special` is always 0, so it is not required and any such animal there counts. Without coordinates: an animal whose `special` is not 0, which none is: never met. The words that name the place before the coordinates are not read |
| a short name of a species (`Elk`, `Whitetail`, `Blacktail`, `Pintail`, `Ptarmigan`): every species with that word in its name, or, when the mission lists some species of its own, those of them; one of several species (`a Red Fox or a Coyote`: `or`, not `and`); `any animal except Red Fox` (or `except an Elk`: every species of that short name); `Mallard duck` | the species of event 7 (a harvest: event 3's `extended_species`); a spot too |
| `fine`, `dandy` | nothing: they stand for the gender word beside them |
| `any traditional bow` (longbows, recurves and cable-backed bows: no compound bow, no crossbow), `any gun` (a firearm: no bow, no crossbow), a list of calibres (`any .243 or .223 rifle`: either), what is left out after `except` (`any rifle except a muzzleloader`, `any weapon except a rifle and a shotgun`: every weapon but the classes and names listed; no weapon after it: not understood) and `(no crossbows)`; weapons listed in brackets after a class as examples (`any semi-auto shotgun (20 GA Semi-Automatic Shotgun, ...)`) are not read | the last hit's `weapon_id` |
| the note in brackets of an ammunition (`7mm-08 Ammunition (HV)`: only that item, without it every item of that name), `a tracer arrow`; `without using any type of buckshot` | the last hit's `ammunition_id` (is one of them; is none of them) |
| `a scope`, `a scoped weapon`; a sight by its name (`a rifle mounted with an Aimpoint sight`: the sights whose name has the words before `sight`; a `sight` that names none is not understood) | the last hit's `scope_id` is not -1; is one of those sights |
| `weight below 270` (no unit: kilograms), `at a range of A m - B m`, `bringing in a total score of at least N`, `200 <unit> libras (or X kg ...)` (the kilograms in the bracket) | as the weight, the distance and the score above |
| `without using a stand`, `without using a platform or blind`, `Stands, blinds, towers and shooting tripod rests are not allowed`; `from a stand`, `from a platform` | the last hit's `location` is 0 (none); a bare `stand` is any of 1, 2, 4, 5 and 6, a `platform` any of 1 to 6 |
| `lung and/or heartshots only`; `Only organs may be hit`; `No other vital organs or bones should be hit` (after a named organ: only the named ones); `without hitting the skull`, `the liver`, `the intestines`, `without penetrating any bones`; `the heart must be hit with the killing shot` | `organ2`: every hit has organs and they are among those named (`Only organs`: the soft organs, not the bones); no hit of the kill has the bit (the bones are the spine, the shoulder blade, the neck bones, the skull and the hip bone: bits 10, 21 to 26, 28 and 29); the last hit has the heart's |
| `swimming`, `idle`, `still`, `emerging from or diving into a burrow exit`, `charging you`; `should not be running away or charging`; `that was shot while ...` | the last hit's `animal_state` (swimming 7, idle and still 1, 2, 3, 8 and 12: an approximation, the states of an animal that stays in its place, burrow 13 to 15, charging 9); the last one must not be 9 or running away (5, 6, 10, 15) |
| `while prone`, `while crouching`, `while standing upright`, `while standing`, `while standing up` | the last hit's `pose`, its low nibble: 2, 1, 0. A spot carries none: it is never met |
| a coat right before the species (`a blonde Grizzly Bear`, `a dark Grey Wolf`, `an Orange female Axis Deer`) or with its frame (`a Chocolate colored`, `a Grey coloured`, `with fur variation Piebald Black`, `(Blue color variation)`); `with a color other than Common`, `with any fur variation other than common`; `Halloween` | event 7's `texture`, the coat's id, is one of the ids of that name; is none of them. The names are the game's own English names of the ids (`Common` is 0, 1, 3, 35, 36 and 42; `Dark` 26, 28 and 32; `Piebald` 5 and 37 to 39; `Chocolate` 7 and 30; ...); `Blue` is `Common Blue` (23); the Grey Wolf's four `Common` coats are told apart by their colour: `common` 0 (grey), `dark` 1 and 42 (black and tan), `brown` 3; `Halloween` is any coat of the Halloween 2026 event (43 to 48, 54 to 59, 67 to 82). A spot is read for its coat too |

**Conditions of a clue.** A clue objective is read for the kind of clue it names, the animal and how many clues it needs; the kind is checked against the event's `track_type`.

| Words of the title | Checked against |
|---|---|
| `tracks`, `track`, `footprints`, `footprint`, `footsteps` | a footprints clue: the original's daily missions with these titles (`ID tracks from a Grey Wolf`) counted footprints only |
| `tracks` of one animal's trail: a number of them with `Locate` or `Find` (`Locate 3 Elk tracks`) or `from the same animal` / `one and the same` (`Identify 3 Grey Wolf tracks from the same animal`) | a clue of any kind in the table below, rooting and water bubbles too: a trail goes on with every clue of its animal (the footprints rule holds for `ID 3 tracks from a Moose`) |
| `droppings`, `dropping`, `scat`, `a droppings track` | a droppings clue |
| `call`, `audio clue`, `audio call`, `audio` | a heard call |
| `blood`, `blood trail` | a blood trail |
| `bedding`, `bed` | a bedding clue |
| `any clue`, `a clue` | a clue of any kind in the table below but rooting and water bubbles |
| several of the above (`footprints or droppings`, `tracks, droppings or calls`, `any clue (footprints, droppings, or audio)`) | a clue of any of the kinds named (`tracks` among them are the footprints): the kinds in brackets after `any clue` are the ones it takes |
| `3 tracks`, `five tracks`, `3 sets of tracks`, `from the same animal` | that many clues of those kinds of one animal in the hunt, after the mission was activated, the one just found counted (without a number, one) |
| a species name, a short name (`Elk`, `Sitka`, `Ptarmigan`), `any species of Ptarmigan`, `any animal except ...`, a gender word, `in <reserve>` | as for a harvest: the species and gender of the animal's event 7, the reserve of the hunt |
| `another`, `a second`, `a third` right before the animal (its name or short name, a gender word between: `another Roosevelt Elk`, `another male Red Deer`) | a clue of another animal: the `n`-th different animal among such clues, as for `different` below. `second` is 2 and `third` is 3; for `another`, `n` is 1 and the mission's earlier clue objectives for the same species, gender and kinds of clue, however they are worded (`a bison`, then two `another bison` are 1, 2 and 3), the first having no condition. The animals are counted in the hunt when the mission is a single-hunt one or the title says one hunt; otherwise in every hunt since the mission was activated, an animal's number reused by the next hunt being another animal (so the same animal in three hunts meets `a bison` and two `another bison`). The count is of every animal that left such a clue since the activation, not of those that finished the objectives before: when the first of these objectives cannot take a clue yet (ordered after another objective, or with a condition the others lack), animals that leave clues meanwhile are counted, and one of them can then finish two of the objectives. No objective of the recorded catalogue is affected (the first is never ordered and has the others' conditions); an entry of `mission_rules.json` that makes it wait would be |
| `another`, `more`, `one more`, `second`, `third` before a clue word (`another track`, `another set of tracks`, `more tracks`, `a third set of tracks`), `a set of`, `a pile of`, `unique` | nothing: each clue finishes one objective, in order (`a second different ...` is read below) |

The kind of a clue is its `track_type` (`State.ClueKinds`): 0 a blood trail, 9 a heard call, 12 bedding, and for each species an id for its droppings and one for its footprints (a trotting or a fleeing animal leaves the footprints' id): 1 and 2 for a whitetail, 3 and 4 for a mule deer, 16 and 17 for a moose, and so on up to 102 and 103 for a wood grouse. A few species leave another's (a blacktail and a Sitka deer a mule deer's, a red deer an elk's), which the species test tells apart. Rooting (20) and water bubbles (31; the red deer's are 25 and the wendigo's 32, the ids of the roe deer's and the rabbits' droppings) are counted by a trail only: no other objective, `any clue` included, is met by them. A `track_type` with no kind in the table, a fraction, none, or 25 and 32 of an animal that is not known is no clue: it meets no objective. The animal of a clue is the latest spawn (event 7) of its individual in the hunt it was found in.

**Several events of one hunt.** Some objectives are about several harvests or clues of one hunt. The event that meets one is the last of them: the harvests (or clues) of its hunt received after the mission was activated (for the daily mission, since the day began) that meet the objective's other words (species, gender and conditions) are counted, this one included, as the clue count does. The time of an event is when the server received it (`events.ts`); a window gets 10 seconds of grace, because events reach the server in batches some seconds after they happen, and an event with no time (stored before schema 14) is within none.

| Words of the title | Met by the harvest or clue that is |
|---|---|
| a number before the animal and a time (`two Snowshoe Hares with 5 minutes of the first harvest`, `3 Moose within 15 minutes`, `two Dall Sheep within 5 minutes of one another`, `5 ... in 5 minutes`); `an Elk 5 minutes after another` (two) | the `n`-th such harvest of the hunt whose first is at most that many minutes before it |
| a number and `within N minutes of each harvest` | the last of `n` such harvests of the hunt, each at most `N` minutes after the one before |
| an ordinal of a harvest (`a second`, `a third`, ... `a tenth`; `thirdn` is read as `third`) when the title says `same`, `one` or `single` `hunt` (or `expedition`, `session`, `outing`) or the mission is a single-hunt one | the `n`-th such harvest of the hunt. Elsewhere an ordinal says nothing more, as before: each harvest finishes one objective, in order, so the second one of a mission may come in another hunt; a spot's ordinal says nothing either |
| a number and `with a single shot` (`Kill two Rusa Deer at the same time with a single shot`) | the `n`-th such harvest of the hunt whose killing hit has the `shot_id` of this one's: one shot killed them all |
| clues of `different` animals: a number or an ordinal and, when the title gives one, a time (`tracks from three different Elk within a span of 5 min`, `3 clues from different Axis Deer`, `footprints of a second different Feral Goat`) | a clue of the hunt that makes `n` different animals (individuals) among such clues, those received at most the minutes before it when the title gives a time; always counted in the hunt, as the clue count is |

A plural of a species name is read as the name (`Hares`, `Foxes`, `Wolves`, `Geese`). A number that is no time and no shot leaves the title not understood. The live heartbeat, the [start's confirmation](#missions), the rollback, the audit and the stuck report judge these with the same code (`State.Sequenced`); the stuck report shows how many of `n` a refused event made (`Within: 1 of 2`). `coverage --rules` prints them, and an [overridden rule](#missions) states them, as `in the hunt: 2` (an ordinal), `in the hunt: 2 within 5 min`, `in the hunt: 2, each within 10 min of the one before`, `in the hunt: 2 killed by one shot` and, for clues only, `in the hunt: 3 different animals` (with ` within 5 min` when timed) and `in the mission: 3 different animals` (the animals of every hunt since the activation, as an `another <animal>` objective of a mission that is not a single-hunt one asks); the number is at least 2.

**What the stored events show.** The meanings of the event fields above were guesses until they were checked against the stored events of the community server, counted on 2026-11-23: 13,592 hits, 9,123 harvests, 11,250 spots and 27,614 clues of 22,078 animals. What they settle, and what they leave open:

- **Flying birds: changed.** States 6 and 11 are sent for birds only (4,181 hits, none of another species), so `airborne` stands. The challenge conditions name them `FLYING` and `FLYING_NOT_SPOOKED` ([Challenges](#challenges)), and the hits agree. Of the 3,838 hits on flying ducks and geese, those within 10 seconds of the player's earlier shot (a hit or a miss: every shot sends a photo request, event 4, with its `shot_id`, and the times are those the server received the events at) read 6 in 2,685 of 2,745, and those with no shot in the 20 seconds before read 11 in 960 of 1,069. When one shell hits several times the states only go from 11 to 6 (17 shots, the other way never), and the birds that fly only once they are flushed (pheasants, grouse, ptarmigans, turkeys) read 6 in 335 of 343 hits in the air. The earlier rule had the two the wrong way round (6 calm, 11 fleeing): it refused the birds a shot had flushed as `spooked` and took them as `unspooked`. Of 285 stored harvests of a Mallard shot in the air, the 198 first hit with 6 are spooked now and the 87 with 11 unspooked. `fleeing` and `running away` take 6 in the place of 11 as well (no title asks either of a bird).
