- **Other animals, `spooked` at the first hit: an approximation, as before.** With no shot of the player in the 60 seconds before it, the first hit finds the animal fleeing (5, 10, 15, 16) in 183 of 3,623 cases; within 10 seconds of another shot in 267 of 820, while 362 of those 820 still read a calm state (an animal that stopped, or walks). The state follows the shot but misses some: nothing else in a hit tells, and the rule is unchanged.
- **`idle`, `still` and the moving states: fits.** By the distance an animal covered in the 3 to 60 seconds before a hit, the states 1, 2, 3, 8 and 12 are the slow ones (medians 0.0 to 0.4 m/s; 1,232 hits), then 4 (0.7 m/s; 793), 10 (2.2; 47), 5 (3.8; 567) and 6 (7.4; 604). 7 is seen on ducks and geese only (27 hits), 12 on hares and rabbits (101), 13 and 15 on the burrowing rabbit (41); 14 was never sent. So the five are the states of an animal that stays in its place. Whether a title's `idle` or `still` takes all five or only the standing one (3) is not in the events: the rule stays an approximation.
- **Stands: confirmed.** A hit's `location` matches what the player stood on: 1 within 15 m of a tower of the catalogue (465 of 641 hits; 22 of the 8,260 with 0), and, where the player's own placing of an item (event 25) is among the stored events, 2 by a tree stand (191 hits), 3 by a ground blind (6), 4 by a tripod stand (39), 5 by a waterfowl blind (461), 6 by a shooting tripod rest (6), never another item. A spot carries the same value: 3,557 of 11,250 spots have one, and where the spot or the player's shots within a minute of it name a stand, the two are equal in 2,689 of 2,733 cases.
- **Stance: fits.** `pose` is 0, 1 or 2 in every hit; 2 is sent from the ground (1,342) and from towers (7), never from a tree stand, a tripod, a blind or a rest (0 of 4,691 hits there), as lying down would be. 0 against 1 is not told by the events.
- **Positions and coordinates: the frame confirmed, the point fits.** A hit's `distance_cms` is 0.99 times the distance between its two positions (median of 12,223 hits), so positions are metres. The titles give 119 different coordinates, and players entered 150 places (event 101): 71 of the 119 lie within 150 m of where a place is entered when `X` and `Y` are read as the map's `x` and `z`; read the other ways, 2 of the 119 do with the two swapped and 5 with `z` negated. Of the 13 written with a point, 6 do when `6.273` is read as 6273 (`-9.683, 5.535` is 1 m from where its place is entered) and 1 when it is read as 6.273; the others are places of animals, which no event marks. The 1000 m around a named animal's place stay a guess for the objectives the server does not place ([Named animals](#missions)).
- **Weights: confirmed.** `weight_grams` gives each species its weight (a Mallard 0.7 to 1.5 kg, a Moose 320 to 620 kg), and a weight without a unit is kilograms: `below 270` and `below 380` split the cows (260 to 285 kg; 122 of 350 below) and the bulls (315 to 490 kg; 52 of 193) of their species, where pounds would let none through. The `200 libras (or 7,2 kg)` are reached by 67 of 952 geese.
- **Typical points: fits.** Both `points` lists added give the racks hunters name: 6, 8, 10 and 12 for 636 of 732 whitetail bucks, and every bound a title sets (2 to 18) lies inside what its species reaches. The events hold no score sheet to compare the count with.
- **Beard, spurs, tusks: units known, read by the operator's file** ([Overridden rules](#missions)). A male turkey's `beard` is 76,000 to 381,000 and its spurs 0 to 29,364 (236 animals), thousandths of a millimetre as the score reads them (3 to 15 inches; up to 1.2 inches); a boar's tusks are the first `points` length of each side in millimetres (40 to 187; both reach 4 inches for 280 of 844 males). A turkey hen sends no trophy event (0 of 221), so `a hen with a beard` cannot be read from the events.
- **Places: the game names them.** The game sends an event 101 when the player enters (`direction` enter) or leaves a place it knows (`location_name`): 15,219 of them, 150 places. Some are the catalogue's locations (a hunting tower's is its `define`, entered within about 30 m of the tower), others the areas of mission titles (`east of ...`, `north of ...`, the area of a mission), which no catalogue lists. Where the events tell where such an area lies, it is what the title says: the one named north of a lodge and the one named north of a tower are entered at the tower's line, so the first is the band between the tower and the lodge. A condition `place` is met when the latest event 101 of that place in the hunt, before the killing hit (a spot or a clue: before it), is an enter.
- **Harvest value and sights: confirmed; `special`: always 0.** `harvest_value` never exceeds 100000 and equals it in 6,693 of 9,123 harvests; `scope_id` is -1 (7,000 hits) or a sight of the catalogue. `special` is 0 for all 22,078 animals: its meaning is still unknown, and no stored animal could meet a named animal's objective that has no coordinates.
- **Clues: confirmed.** A heard call (9) is found 182 m from the player (median of 5,539; 14 within 10 m), every other kind at the player's feet (22,063 of 22,075 within 10 m). Each `track_type` seen has a kind in the table, and in every species that left both, the droppings' id is the rarer one (2,213 against 14,379 footprints). Five ids of the table were never sent: 38, 39 and 93, which were not verified (the European rabbit left 32 and 33, the wendigo 92 and 69), and 88 and 89. No snow goose left a call (0 of its 38 clues), where the other geese left 59.

**What is not understood.** What is left of a title after the conditions above, the species, the gender, articles, `any`, `permitted` and `in the same hunt` are taken out makes it not understood: the size of a beard, spurs or tusks and a place by its direction (`north of ...`) or its area (these the operator's file states, below), `brown` for a coat of another species than the Grey Wolf (`a brown Black Bear`; the game's coat names are read for any species, one that never has that coat too, which is then never met), event creatures, coordinates, a count with no time or shot (`two Whitetail Deer`), a time for one harvest, `without spooking it`, a named animal without quotes, a weapon spelled another way, a weight in a unit the title does not convert. A spot is read for its stand, its reserve, its place, its species, its coat and a name only: any other condition, a count or a time among them, makes it not understood. A clue is read for the words of its paragraph and of the one above: a clue word with no kind in it (`feeding`), a short species name no species has, `different` animals without a number, a place, coordinates or a time span without `different` make it not understood. An arrive objective with no coordinates that no place of the catalogue (`locations`, by English name, define or the words of either) can meet is not understood either, whatever area the game may send for it: a place the game names with other words than the title's is met only by an entry of `mission_rules.json`. Such an objective, and one of a kind the server does not read, is never met: a harvest of its species no longer completes it. The harvest stays among the stored events, and [it is confirmed later](#missions) when a version reads the title. `coverage` ends with the missions' report: the objectives of the catalogue and how many are understood by kind, how many missions have every objective understood, the conditions read, how many objectives are not, and each objective that is not, with its mission id and title (an arrive objective no place of the catalogue meets says so).

**Reading the rules.** `coverage --rules` prints, after the counts, one line per objective, sorted by mission and objective so that two runs print the same bytes and a change to the reader shows as a diff: `mission 86 objective 0: <title> => <kind>; species: ...; male|female; ordered; <conditions>`. The conditions are in words, with names instead of ids (weapon, ammunition and sight names, coat names, states, stands, the reserve, organs), their bounds (`distance: >100 m`, `>` and `<` exclusive, `>=` and `<=` inclusive) and are sorted. An objective that is not understood has `not understood, left over: <words>` before the conditions it did read. An objective whose rule is the operator's (below) has `(override)` after its id: `mission 86 objective 0 (override): <title> => ...`. A pull request that changes the reader has the diff of this output in its body (docs/agents/mission-reader.md).

**The weapons of an objective.** Under `weapons:`, before the rules, `coverage --rules` prints one line for every objective that has a weapon condition, in the same order: `mission 61 objective 0: <basis>: <id> <name> (<finish>); ...`, the accepted weapon items by id. The basis is `class` (`any bow`), `named weapon` (every member of the named weapons' families), `named weapon + class`, `any weapon but` (every weapon but the refused ones) or `named finish` (the operator's file only). An objective without a weapon condition has no line there; the audit tables below list it as `none`.

**Warnings.** The report ends with `warnings:` (only when there are some), one line each, by mission and then kind. A warning is a lead, not an error: the `weapons` list is the page's filter and is wider than the titles for some missions, and the server starts all the same.

- `weapons`: the mission's `weapons` list has a weapon that no objective accepts, while the objectives accept a weapon of its family (all the words of one name are in the other: the title says `the Heavy Recurve Bow`, the list holds the Recurve Bow too). Objectives that ask nothing of the weapon are not counted; none naming one, no warning.
- `description`: the mission's `desc` names a weapon or an ammunition (by the item's name, the longest name only) that no objective accepts, while the objectives name others of that kind (the title says `.44 Magnum Revolver`, the text `.44 Revolver`).
- `species`: the rule of a harvest, spot or clue objective accepts no species of the mission's `species` list. Not given for an `any species except` rule: a title like `any animal except an Alpine Ibex` leaves the mission's own species out on purpose.
- `never met`: a condition of an understood objective that no event can meet: an empty set of accepted ids, a lower bound above the upper one, a named animal without a place (it needs a `special` other than 0, which the game never sends).

**Overridden rules.** The operator may state the rule of single objectives the reader gets wrong or cannot read (a typo, one place, one named animal, a weapon the title names loosely) in `mission_rules.json` in `recordings_dir`, beside the recorded catalogue ([ADR 0012](adr/0012-the-operator-may-state-an-objectives-rule.md)). It is the operator's data, never committed, and is read at the start only when the catalogue is a recording; without it nothing changes. It is a JSON object (`//` comments and trailing commas allowed, a key twice is not): each key is `<mission id>/<objective id>`, each value a rule in the words `coverage --rules` prints after `=>`, so a printed line can be copied, edited and printed again. An entry replaces the reader's rule of that objective whole; the title, texts and rewards stay the catalogue's.

```json
{
  // the title names the carbine by a short name the reader does not know
  "12/0": "harvest; species: Test Deer; male; weapon: Test Carbine, Test Shotgun; distance: >100 m",
  "12/1": "track; species: Test Deer; clues: footprints x3; reserve: Test Reserve"
}
```

The parts are separated by `;`, names by `, ` and are the catalogue's English names (the case is not compared; a name the catalogue lacks, which `coverage --rules` prints by its id as `item 5`, is refused, but for `coat <id>`); the first part is the kind:

| Words | Meaning |
|---|---|
| `harvest`, `spot`, `track` | the kind (a track is a clue objective); starts keep the reader's rule |
| `arrive[; ordered]; place: <NAME>` | the kind of an arrival the title cannot name (an "Info message" when the player is in an area; a place the game names with other words than the title's): met when the player enters the place the game names `NAME`, and no other, and, when the title has coordinates, near them as for the other arrive titles (the game's area may be larger or smaller than 200 m); `ordered` as for the other kinds (a "Then arrive" objective, or "back"); nothing else may follow |
| `species: <names>`, `species: any species except <names>` | one name: that species; several: any of them |
| `male`, `female`, `ordered` | the gender; counts only after every objective before it is finished |
| `weapon: <names>` | the last hit's weapon: a weapon's name is every member of its [weapon family](#weapon-families) (as in a title); `Name (Finish)` is that one item and no other, and an entry with one has the basis `named finish` in `coverage --rules`. `coverage --rules` prints a name bare when the set holds the whole family of every item of that name, else each held item as `Name (Finish)`, so a printed line, copied into the file, is the same set |
| `ammunition: <names>`, `not ammunition: <names>`, `sight: <names>`, `any sight`, `no sight` | the last hit's ammunition (is one of them; is none of them) and sight (every item of a name: skins and variants; `Name (Variant)` is the one variant of items that share a name) |
| `permitted`, `one shot` | as the title words of the same name |
| `organs, one of: <mask> \| <mask>`, `organs only: <mask>`, `avoids: <mask>, <mask>` | a mask is `soft organs`, `bones`, or organs joined by `+`: `heart`, `right lung`, `left lung`, `liver`, `brain`, `intestines`, `spine`, `shoulder blade`, `neck bones`, `skull`, `hip bone`, `bit <n>` |
| `harvest value: <bounds>`, `distance: <bounds> m`, `weight: <bounds> kg`, `score: <bounds>`, `typical points: <bounds>` | one or two bounds separated by a space: `>=N`, `>N`, `<=N`, `<N` |
| `beard: <bounds> mm`, `spurs: <bounds> mm`, `tusks: <bounds> mm` | the trophy event's (22) `beard` and the shorter of `left_spur` and `right_spur` (thousandths of a millimetre as sent, compared in millimetres: 3 inches is `>=76.2`), and the shorter tusk (the first point of `left_antler` and `right_antler`, in millimetres); a kill with no trophy event never meets them |
| `state: <word>`, `first state: unspooked`, `not state: charging, running away` | the words of the table above: `airborne`, `standing`, `walking`, `running`, `moving`, `charging`, `fleeing`, `swimming`, `burrow exit`, `idle`, `still` |
| `pose: <names>`, `stand: <names>` | `standing`, `crouching`, `prone`; `no stand`, `hunting tower`, `tree stand`, `ground blind`, `tripod`, `waterfowl blind`, `tripod rifle rest` |
| `ordered`, `after objective <n>`, `unordered`, `together` | the order: after every objective before it, after objective `<n>` of the mission (an earlier one) only, or none (`unordered` undoes the order the title implies: `Then`, `After visiting X`, `At any point during the same hunt` with a start; the reader's order stays when the entry names none of the three); at most one of the three, and `together` at most once and only with another objective of the mission that has the same words |
| `arrive; place: <NAME>`, `arrive; places: <NAME>, <NAME>`, `arrive; within 200 m of (<x>, <z>)`, `arrive` | an arrive objective: the place the game names, the catalogue places of a title, or a point; each once, as `coverage --rules` prints them (with the order words). A bare `arrive` keeps the title's own arrival rule (as `coverage --rules` prints an arrival read by its words) and is refused on an objective whose title is not an arrival |
| `weapon: <names>; weapon barrels: <weapon> = <ammunition>, ... \| <weapon> = ...` | for a weapon of two classes (a combination gun that is a shotgun with its shell and a rifle with its rifle round): the ammunition each weapon counts with for the class its noun names, as `coverage --rules` prints it. It follows a `weapon` condition, is given once, and names weapons of that condition only: a second one, a weapon outside it or a weapon the catalogue lacks stops the start |
| `reserve: <names>` | the reserve of the hunt |
| `place: <NAME>` | the player was in the place the game names `NAME` (capital letters, digits and `_`, as event 101's `location_name` sends it; no catalogue lists them, so a misspelt one is never met): at the killing hit, a spot or a clue ([What the stored events show](#missions)) |
| `named animal near (<x>, <z>) m, <n> animals, otherwise place <NAME>`, `footprints of each of <species>, <species> within <r> m of (<x>, <z>)` | a group of `n` animals the server places (1 to 20; one animal when left out; with placement off the objective is met within 500 m of the point or by a hunter in `NAME`), and a track objective met by a clue of footprints of each species within `r` metres (1 to 5000) of the point; a number outside these bounds or too big to read stops the start with the entry's key |
| `named animal near (<x>, <z>) m`, `named animal, no place` | as a quoted name |
| `coat: <names>`, `not coat: <names>` | the coat names and words the reader reads (`blue`, `halloween`, the Grey Wolf's own); `coat <id>` for a coat the game names nowhere |
| `clues: <kinds> x<count>`, `clues: any x<count>` | kinds `blood`, `footprints`, `droppings`, `call`, `bedding`, `other` (rooting and water bubbles); the count of clues of one animal |

A spot rule checks only `stand`, `reserve`, `place`, `species`, `coat`, `not coat`, a named animal and `in the hunt/mission: <n> different animals`; a track rule has one `clues` and may check `reserve`, `place`, `species`, `coat`, `not coat`, `footprints of each of ...` and `different animals`; a harvest rule no `clues`, no `footprints of each of` and no `different animals`. Loading fails loud: an entry for a mission or an objective the catalogue does not have, a key or a value of another shape, an empty rule, another kind, a word that is no part of a rule, a name no item, species, coat or reserve has (a `place` only by its form), a condition its kind cannot check, a gender or a `species` given twice, more than one of `ordered`, `unordered` and `after objective <n>`, `together` twice or without a twin, a number out of its bounds, or two bounds of one side stops the start (and `coverage`) with `mission_rules.json "<key>": <reason>`. Nothing is skipped. `serve` prints `missions: N objective rules from mission_rules.json`; `coverage` counts the overridden objectives as understood and lists them (`objectives overridden by mission_rules.json: N`, then one line each with `(override)`). **A printed line is the same rule when copied into the file**: for every objective whose rule is read (`not understood` ones are not), the line `coverage --rules` prints, loaded as its entry, gives the rule the title gave (`TestEveryPrintedRuleReadsBackAsTheSameRule`, which also runs on the real catalogue when `MISSION_CATALOGUE` is set, compares the rules themselves, `State.RuleShapes`, not their printed words). To that end an arrive entry keeps the title's reserves and, unless it names one `place:`, the title's words (the game's areas the catalogue lacks are still met by them); a weapon of two classes prints `weapon barrels: <weapon> = <ammunition>, ... | ...` (the ammunition it counts with for the class its noun names); and an entry with none of `ordered`, `after objective <n>` and `unordered` keeps the order the title gave, while `ordered` or `unordered` replaces it (and `after objective <n>` waits for that objective only). An overridden rule is judged like any other: at the heartbeat, by the start's re-check below (which completes the objective from the harvests and clues already made), the rollback, the audit and `missions-stuck` (which takes two objectives for equal when their rules are the same words, not their titles).

**Cases.** `MissionCaseTests` plays each objective's cases (a scenario that must finish it, near misses that must not, written from its wording) through this engine against a catalogue and the operator's rules: here an invented sample (`server/Server.Tests/mission-cases/`), and the real catalogue's cases, which are the operator's data, from the paths of `MISSION_CASES`, `MISSION_CATALOGUE` and `MISSION_RULES`. The format, how to write them and how to run them are in [mission-cases.md](mission-cases.md).

**Confirmed later.** At its start the server checks every account's active missions again (`State.ConfirmMissions`): the open harvest, clue and arrive objectives against the harvests, clues and places entered (event 101) stored since the mission was activated (`seen`; for a single-hunt mission those of the latest hunt), in order, as the heartbeat would have. An objective that a harvest, a clue or a place entered meets under the rules of this version is finished now, with the toast and the rewards of that finish (gm$ and em$, and the missions it unlocks, activated as at that event); the server prints how many it finished. An ordered arrive objective waits for the objectives before it, but counts a place entered at any time after the activation, one entered before they were finished too (the row keeps no event for them). A player loses nothing by waiting for a version that reads their title: a harvest or a clue made while its objective was not understood (a title with `At any point during the same hunt,` in front, a stance, a sight, a short species name) finishes it at the next start. A mission of an earlier schema (`since` null) counts the harvests and clues from the first start of schema 30, and the objectives it had finished then are `earlier`: no later harvest or clue is given to them again. Only harvest and clue objectives are checked again; a spot newly understood counts from the start of the version that reads it.

**What a deploy does to missions in progress.** The start's check above only ever finishes objectives: it never reopens an objective or a mission and never takes stored progress away (a finished objective, and any mission completed, is not checked again; an objective an event meets only under the old rules stays finished). What it can change under new rules: an open objective that a stored harvest, clue or place now meets is finished and paid with the mission's gm$ and em$ when it was the last one (the muzzleloader as a rifle, the family of a weapon, a place by its display name, `N tracks from a <species>` counting any animals, `N typical points`, a stand the title does not refuse, a `together` group: one stored kill finishes every objective of the group). The rules that only tighten (`permitted` on a named ammunition, `spooked` at the killing shot, `platform`, a start order such as `At any point during the same hunt` or `... first.`) finish nothing and reopen nothing on their own: an objective already ticked stays ticked whatever order its rule now has; `ordered` and `after objective <n>` decide only what an open objective waits for. `admin missions-recheck [--account <id or handle>]` is the dry run of this check (it prints one line per account and mission with the objectives it would finish and the pay, then a total). It writes nothing of its own: each account's check is made in a transaction that is rolled back, the ids the new rules give named animals (with `spawn_named_animals` on) are worked out in memory and `named_animals.json` is not written, and it does not create a missing `state.db`. Loading the state still does the start's normal upkeep on `state.db` (the purge of expired items, the start kit, the hunterscore, the migrations; idempotent, the writes a start makes), so run it on a copy of the data, with the server stopped, before the deploy. Afterwards `admin missions-rollback --apply` would reopen what the old rules finished and these do not, and the audit flags `rule changed` on every objective whose printed line changed (a coat set that no name reads back as itself is now printed as `coat <id>`, a weapon of two classes has its `weapon barrels`), so run neither blindly. An operator file with `first state: spooked` is still read (every state word of `first state` is the animal's state at the first hit, as before); install the new `mission_rules.json` before the restart, so that the entries bind the start's check.

**Named animals.** A named animal is no animal the game marks (event 7's `special` is always 0), so with `spawn_named_animals` on the server places it, and the objective is met by that animal and no other. At the start every harvest or spot objective whose rule has a named animal with a place gets a **population**, when its rule also gives one species (the title's, else the mission's only one), at most one coat that is one coat id (a coat word shared by several ids, such as `dark` or `piebald`, and a `not coat` are not placed: the operator's file states the one, as `coat 32`), one reserve (its `reserve` condition, else the mission's only listed one) and a place that is not (0, 0) and within 16384 m on each axis. The gender is the rule's, male when it has none. The population id is `(reserve - 1) * 100 + 60 + n`: an animal (reserve, species, gender, coat, place) takes the next free `n` of its reserve, in mission and objective order, the first time it is placed, and keeps it in `named_animals.json` in `data_dir`, so an id never moves when a rule or the catalogue changes and a harvest stored before still counts. Objectives that name the same animal share it; an animal that is no longer named still holds its place, and a reserve has 20 (a 21st keeps the 1000 m rule). Delete a line of the file only with the harvests of its animal in mind: its id may go to another animal. The named condition of a placed objective holds for a harvest or a spot whose animal's spawn (event 7) has that `population` (the harvest says `server_spawn`), whatever its place; every other condition of the rule still has to hold. `admin missions-rollback`, `missions-audit` and `missions-stuck` judge it the same way.

The hunt's reply (the connect) gets the animals: for each placed objective of the hunt's reserve that is open (not finished, and, when ordered, every one before it finished) on an active mission of the player, one entry of `spawn`: `{population_id, species, spawn_map_id, group: [record 0, the animal]}`. Record 0 is the dummy the client never applies (no animals, no place); the animal is one male or one female, `x` and `z` the title's place, `radius` 0, `texture_id` its coat or -1, `active` 1, no weight window. `spawn_map_id` is the density map of the species' own population row on the reserve, and the entry is left out when the reserve has no row of that species (or one with an unknown density map), when the reserve's rows already have that id (the entry would replace that population), or when the species' `spawn_licenses` is 2 (the client would drop it). Every player's own connect gets their own entries, but the client sets up the animals only in the world of the host of a multiplayer game: a guest with the mission active sees the host's animals, and a harvest of the host's named animal meets the guest's objective too (the ids are the same for everyone); a host without the mission places none. The animal comes back in every hunt until its objective is finished. A hunt with no such objective gets the reply as it was, byte for byte, and so does every hunt with the setting off, where the 1000 m rule above stands. With the setting on, `coverage` adds a line with how many objectives have a population and how many named-animal objectives keep the guess. Turning the setting on changes which kills meet these objectives from the next start: the start's re-check finishes nothing from old harvests for a placed objective (none is of the spawn), and `admin missions-rollback` reopens what the 1000 m guess had finished. The setting is off by default because a malformed entry could stop a hunt from starting for the player, and the game has to be seen to accept this one first ([#838](https://github.com/yinyin99/hunter-resurgence/issues/838)).

**Rollback.** The earlier rules finished objectives whose titles had conditions the server did not read. `admin missions-rollback [--apply] [--account <id or handle>]` checks each finished harvest or clue objective of every mission that the rules above fully understand against the harvests and clues since it was activated (since the account's first event when the activation is not known; for a single-hunt mission the single hunt that confirms most of the objectives it holds) under the rules above; one that no harvest or clue meets is reopened (a harvest that met an antler condition, a harvest of another coat than the title's or a clue of another kind than the title's finished it wrongly: it is judged now). A reopened objective whose coat only an event gives (`Halloween`) can be finished again only while that event runs. An objective with words the server does not read ([What is not understood](#missions)) cannot be judged and stays finished; the total line counts these as `kept: not understood`. A completed mission that loses an objective is active again, and the missions it had made available are activated, so nobody is locked out. The gm$, em$ and items already paid are not taken back, and a mission or objective a restore brought, and an objective a harvest of another player's kill finished for its harvester ([Multiplayer](#multiplayer)), both `kept`, are never touched: no event of the account's stands behind them. A mission that was reopened and is finished again pays its rewards again. Without `--apply` it prints one line per account with a change and the total, and writes nothing (a dry run); `--apply` changes the rows and writes a record per account in `mission_rollbacks` (schema 30: the time, and JSON with the missions and objectives reopened and the missions activated). Run it once, after `backup`, with the server stopped; a second run of the same version finds nothing more, but a version that reads more titles can judge the objectives it kept as `not understood`: run it again once after such a release.

**Audit.** `admin missions-audit [--hours N] [--account <id or handle>] [--discord]` lists the mission objectives completed in the last `N` hours (default 24) that look suspicious, for every account or one, and writes nothing (it takes no lock, so it runs in the live container; on a copy of the community server's database it took about 15 seconds). It judges each held harvest and clue objective as the rollback does (the same `State.JudgeMission`, today's rules); the event that meets it is the one that completed it, and its time is when the server received that event (`events.ts`): no completion time is stored per objective. An objective earlier rules finished early and a later event meets therefore counts as completed at that later event. One line per flagged objective, `flag | mission <id> <title> | <objective> | <handle> | <what met it>`, grouped by flag, then a line with the count of each flag:

- `borderline`: the harvest value, distance, weight, score or typical points of the kill that met it is within 5% of a bound of the title (`distance 103 vs min 100`; several on one line, separated by `;`). 100% harvest value has nothing above it and is never borderline.
- `unmet`: no stored harvest or clue meets it under today's rules, and its rule is the one it was finished under: a bug; should be 0. It has no event to date it, so it is listed while a harvest or clue of the account since its mission's activation falls in the window (only those finish one).
- `rule changed`: the same, but the rule it was finished under is not today's (an override, a stricter title reading, a new catalogue), so the finish was valid then and is not a bug. When an objective is finished the row keeps a fingerprint of its rule (`missions.rules`, schema 35: a hash of its line of `coverage --rules`); an objective whose fingerprint differs from today's, or that has none because it was finished before schema 35 (and today's rule does not meet it, which is what is judged here), is `rule changed`. The rollback reopens such an objective all the same. With `--discord` it is one line with the count, not one line per objective.
- `fast`: completed less than 2 minutes after its mission's activation. The activation time is not stored either: it is the time of the first event the server received after the activation (`since`), which for a mission activated between hunts is the next hunt's first event. A row older than schema 30 has no `since` and is never fast.
- `shared`: the one harvest or clue that met it met objectives of 3 or more missions.
- `spike`: one line per mission (`every objective`, the number of accounts) completed in the window at least 5 times and more than 3 times its average for a window as long over the 14 days before. A completed mission is dated by its latest harvest or clue objective; one with none (spots, places, starts only) is not counted.

Missions a restore brought and objectives with words the server does not read are not judged. `--discord` prints the same report as Markdown (the header and the counts in bold, one list item per line, `*`, `_`, `~` and backticks escaped) in messages of at most 2000 characters, Discord's limit, separated by a line `---8<---` (the `rule changed` objectives as one line with their count); the community server posts it once a day ([deploy.md](deploy.md#missions-audit)).

**Stuck.** `admin missions-stuck [--days N] [--account <id or handle>] [--discord] [--min-candidates N] [--min-sample N] [--min-share P]` lists the objectives the server reads (`coverage` counts them) that players attempt and never complete, so they need not report them. It writes nothing and takes no lock, like the audit. For every harvest, spot or clue objective with no word the server does not read that is open on an active mission of an account that played in the last `N` days (default 14), and that is next in line when it is ordered, the **candidates** are the harvests, spots or clues of those days that have its kind, species and gender (a title that names several species counts the events of those species) and that could have finished it: those after the mission was activated (a single-hunt mission forgets its progress when a hunt starts: an event of an earlier hunt counts only when a condition refused it), for an ordered objective after the event that finished the one before it (a harvest or a clue is the event the replay gave it; a spot, a place entered or a hunt started leaves none in the row, so the report takes the first such event that meets it, searched after the one before it when the objective is ordered and from the mission's activation when it is not, and an ordered objective with a predecessor it finds no event for is not listed), and none that today's rules give to another objective (the replay of `State.JudgeMission`: an event finishes one objective). The first of several equal open objectives of one mission takes the event, so only it is listed; two rules are equal when their `;`-separated parts are, in any order and case (an entry of `mission_rules.json` may write them another way). A spot that finished one spot objective is not told apart from a refused attempt at another spot objective of the same animal. Each candidate is judged by the conditions of the objective's title (the same `Meets` as `JudgeMission`, today's rules), and the report counts how many each condition refused (`Weapon 20/20, FirstState 18/20`; the names are those of the checks: `Weapon`, `Ammo`, `Distance`, `Stand`, `Coat`, `Clue`, ...). An account completed an objective here when its row holds it; a restore's completions are not counted. An objective is listed when it has at least `--min-candidates` (default 5) candidates and no completion, or when one condition refuses at least `--min-share` percent (default 90) of at least `--min-sample` (default 10) candidates, even if someone completed it: completions do not hide an objective that most attempts fail, and the line's `N completed` tells the operator that some players did finish it; the thresholds are options, not settings (the community server's daily post uses `--min-candidates 50`: [deploy.md](deploy.md#missions-audit)). The lines are sorted by candidates, most first: `mission | objective | candidates and completions | open for N accounts: the first 5 handles | the conditions with their refusals`, then, for the condition that refuses most, the three values it saw most often (the weapon or ammunition names, the reserve names, the ids of states, poses, stands, coats, scopes and organs, the distances, weights, scores, harvest values and typical points in whole numbers, the number of shots, `not permitted`, the kind of clue or how many clues of a count it had, a named animal's `special`, or `away from the place` for a title with coordinates). `no condition refused` shows a candidate every condition accepts that did not finish the objective: a bug, or an event the start-up confirmation above has not checked yet. `--discord` prints the report as the audit does (the community server posts both: [deploy.md](deploy.md#missions-audit)).

## Mission texts

The titles, descriptions and objectives of the missions (and of the mission packs, the daily missions and the event's missions) are **display only**: the rules ([Missions](#missions)) always read the English objective titles of the catalogue, so a mission completes the same in every language. A text is looked up by key in the language's **original** translation (what the game's original served for `lang=<code>`), else in **our** translation (machine translated, reviewed where a speaker is at hand), else it stays English, per text: a title, a description and an objective each fall back on their own.

**Languages.** The 18 launcher languages that are not English: `cs da de es fi fr hu it nb nl pl pt-BR pt-PT ro ru sl sv tr`. A request names one by `lang` (or `locale`), as the launcher's code (`pt-BR`) or the game's locale (`pt_BR`, `fr_FR`, `en_us`); a regional tag is its language (`de-AT`), `no` and `nn` are `nb`, a bare `pt` and any unknown code are English.

**Who sends what in which language.**

| Reply | Language from |
|---|---|
| `GET /account/missions`, `GET /account/missions/daily`, `GET /account/events/panel` (the website's and the launcher's pages) | the query `lang`, which the pages fill with their UI language (the news feed's `?lang=`) |
| `Application/application`, `Application/client_application` (the old build's `POST` and the current build's `GET`/`POST`; the in-game missions page reads them) | the game's own `lang` in the form or the query. A community server patches the recorded reply: the `missions` and `mission_groups` texts only, everything else (ids, rewards, dependencies, the other tables) is the recording's. A local server replays it unchanged |
| `Daily_Mission/get_daily_missions_calendar` | `lang` or `locale` (the current build adds `?locale=`); English without one |

The toasts of the heartbeat (`Mission "…", objective "…" completed.`) stay English: a heartbeat carries no language. The names of a mission's reserve, species and weapon on the website's page (`reserve`, `species`, `weapon`) are the catalogue's English ones too.

**Files.** The folder of the setting `mission_texts_dir` (default `<data_dir>/mission-texts`: on the community server the data volume, [deploy.md](deploy.md#the-community-server)). **It is not in the repository:** the original's translations and the English texts are the game's own text, which the repository never holds ([CONTRIBUTING.md](../CONTRIBUTING.md)), and the operator's machine translations are made from them. The operator builds the folder (below) and uploads it with the other data; the server reads it at its start. All flat JSON objects `{"<key>": "<text>"}` with one entry a line, UTF-8, LF:

| File | Holds |
|---|---|
| `en.json` | every English text by key (the reference the other files are made from) |
| `<lang>.original.json` | the original's texts that **differ** from the English one. Loaded by the server |
| `<lang>.mt.json` | our translations, in the same keys. Loaded by the server; **the original's text wins** where both have one. `{}` until someone translates |
| `todo/<lang>.json` | exactly the English texts that neither `<lang>.original.json` nor `<lang>.mt.json` has: what is still to translate. A text the original left identical to the English one is in it (the original may have meant it, a translator decides) |
| `names/<lang>.json` | the protected names: English name to the word the game shows in that language |

**Keys.** `m<mission id>.title`, `m<mission id>.desc` (the catalogue's HTML kept, tags included), `m<mission id>.o<objective id>`; `g<pack id>.title`, `g<pack id>.desc`; the daily missions of the facts file, `d<template id>.title` and `d<template id>.o<n>` (n from 1, the order of the template's objectives; the page's description is the title and a full stop); the event's community missions, `c<event>.<id>.title`. An empty text is no text.

**Protected names.** What the game shows for a name stays: reserve names, places of the map (lodges, harbours, lakes, towers, campsites), species, weapons and items are kept in English unless the game's own texts for that language translate them (`names/<lang>.json`, built from the language's catalogue reply: species, items, reserves, locations). A translation must hold each protected name its English text holds, either unchanged or as the game's word; case, spaces and punctuation do not count, and an inflected form counts (every word of the game's name is looked for without its last one to three letters). The original's own texts are not held to this (they transliterate some names).

**Regenerate.** `dotnet run --project server/Server -- mission-texts <folder> <command>` needs no settings file; `<folder>` is the setting's folder (`build` creates it):

| Command | Does |
|---|---|
| `build <english catalogue.json> <apps folder>` | `<english catalogue.json>` is the English `Application/application` reply, `<apps folder>` holds the same reply per language as `app-<locale>.json` (`app-de_DE.json`, `app-pt_BR.json`, …; `app-en_US.json` is skipped). Writes `en.json` (the catalogue's texts and the facts file's daily and community ones), every `<lang>.original.json`, every `names/<lang>.json`, an empty `<lang>.mt.json` where there is none, then does `todo`. Run it when the original's replies or the catalogue change; the `.mt.json` files are never touched |
| `todo` | rewrites every `todo/<lang>.json` from `en.json` and the two files of the language: run it after adding translations |
| `check` | for every `<lang>.mt.json`: each key exists in `en.json`, no text is empty, each protected name is kept. One line per problem (`de m12.o0: protected name lost: <name>`), exit 1 when there is one; it passes on empty files. The tests run it on invented files |

To add translations, put `"key": "text"` entries in `<lang>.mt.json` (any order), run `check`, then `todo`.

## Daily missions

On a community server `Daily_Mission/*` answer from the facts file's `daily_missions` table and the account's row of `daily_missions` (schema 15: one JSON row with `frozen`, `day`, `date`, `done`, the finished `objectives`, the broken streak (`has_broken`, `larger`) and `days`, the days completed here by difficulty id). Without templates in the facts file, and on a local server, the calls keep the empty replies of [the table above](#recordings-on-a-community-server). Every call needs the player's own token.

| Call (form `oauth_access_token` only) | Answer |
|---|---|
| `POST /v1/Daily_Mission/get_daily_missions_calendar` | `{is_frozen, mission_end, broken_streak_info: {has_broken_streak, streak_larger}, calendar}`. `is_frozen` is 1 until the player activates a streak, `mission_end` the unix second the UTC day ends at. `calendar` has 7 entries, `day_number` 1 to 7: the block of 7 streak days that holds today (days 1 to 7, then 8 to 14 shown again as 1 to 7, and so on), each with `difficulty` (`{id, title, alias, mission_cooldown, color}`), `rewards` (the `SILVER` row: its `amount` is the day's gm$) and `protected` false. The entry of today has `today` true and the mission: `date`, `species` (the define of the first objective's species), `character` (the giver: its name the giver's `name` of the facts file, else made from the alias, its `image` the giver's `image` of the facts file, else the alias in lower case and `.jpg`), `rewards_translations`, `mission` (`id`, `title`, `description`, `gender` 0 male or 1 female (the first objective's, 0 when it names none), `species`, `reserves`, `objectives`) and `objectives`, the JSON string `[{objective_data: {id, title, update_message, conditions, completed?}, event_type}]` (`completed` once the objective is finished; `event_type` `CLUE`, `SPOT` or `CONFIRMKILL`). The other entries have `date`, `species`, `character`, `mission` and `objectives` null and `rewards_translations` `[]` |
| `POST /v1/Daily_Mission/activate_user_progress` | `{"success": true}`. A frozen account becomes active at day 1 with today's date, keeping the mission it showed, and the broken streak is let go; an account that is not frozen is left as it is |

**The day's mission.** The templates of the day's difficulty, in the file's order, are taken in turn by the date (`templates[date.DayNumber % count]`), so every player on the same day and difficulty has the same mission. The difficulty is the cycle's entry for the streak's day (0 first): `E E E M E E E M E E E M E E E H` in the file. The reward is the difficulty's `reward` gm$, 10 % more for each completed cycle of 16 days, up to 10 cycles (110, 550, 1100 in the second cycle). Objectives are read like a catalogue mission's ([Progress](#missions): a title gives the kind, species, gender, clue kind and count, and order; the condition text the page shows is made from the rule), with two differences: a harvest is the player's own, and a count of clues adds up those found since the day began (the condition the page shows for a clue names droppings when the title does, else footsteps; a harvest's is the confirmed kill `a.ethical() and a.killedBy(e.getUserId()) and ...`). The templates are the facts file's: the game's EASY ones and two MEDIUM ones, and made-up MEDIUM and HARD ones. A MEDIUM mission may have two objectives (381: a male's tracks and a female's "in the same hunt"); ponytail: they count in either order and from any of the day's hunts, as a daily mission is not reset by a hunt. A template with an objective that is no harvest, spot or clue, or that names no species of the catalogue, is left out (logged at the start): it would never complete, or accept any animal.

**The streak.** A new account, and a restored one that was frozen, is frozen: the calendar keeps the date it was made on (the restored one's date) and the mission picked for it, so the mission stays the same however many days pass, and its events count for nothing until `activate_user_progress`, which moves the date to today and keeps that mission for the first day. An active account's day is its date: a completed day is followed by the next one on the next date; a date that passes with the mission not completed, or a date skipped, freezes the account and breaks the streak, which starts again at day 1 on the next activation. A streak of at least one day that breaks is kept as `broken_streak_info` (`has_broken_streak` true, `streak_larger` the days it had) until the next activation; resuming it with the service item is not part of the server.

**Completing a day.** The last objective of today's mission pays the reward through `State.AddCurrency` (wallet row `Reward for Daily Mission`, `update 15` with the new gm balance in the heartbeat reply that carried the event), sends `update 17` `{mission_id, difficulty_id}` (no toast), marks the day done and adds a day to `daily_missions.<difficulty id>.days` of the account's [statistics](#the-statistics), so the achievements of `daily_missions.EASY.days`, `MEDIUM` and `HARD` follow at the next recompute ([Achievements](#achievements)). The entry stays as it is, with its objectives `completed`, until the day ends.

**The website.** The daily missions page reads the same state through `GET /account/missions/daily` and starts a frozen streak through `POST /account/missions/daily/activate` ([Upload a backup zip](#upload-a-backup-zip)), owner only. The server has no item to resume a broken streak, so the page offers none.

**Restored calendars.** The saved `Daily_Mission_get_daily_missions_calendar.json` (`extras`) is read the first time the account's daily missions are used: `is_frozen`, `broken_streak_info` and, for a frozen account, the date of today's entry; an account that was not frozen starts its streak at day 1 on that first day. A backup holds no streak length, so a restored streak always starts again; the restored `daily_missions.<id>.days` counters are the base the days counted here add to. A restore makes the calendar and streak again (from the zip's calendar, or as for a new account) and keeps the days counted here, like the hunts.

## Challenges

On a community server `Challenge/get_user_challenges` answers from the account's rows, and the hunt's events complete the challenges of the recorded `Challenge/get_challenges` list (the reply of the recorded player's list; without that recording there are no challenges and the empty reply stands). A challenge is a permanent, one-time goal with a badge and no pay: completing one writes a row and queues a notice, and changes no wallet and no statistics. The call needs a player's token and answers for the player himself or a player he may see ([Public reads](#public-reads)): the account's rows are read with the `user_id`, in the form of a `POST` or in the query of a `GET` (the current build's pages use the second). Without a token, and for a user id the caller may not see, unknown, banned or recorded, the reply is `{}`; a local server tracks nothing and leaves the call to its recordings.

The rows are the `challenges` table (schema 14): one per completed challenge (`challenge_id`, the definition's `objectives` text with `"completed": true` added to the objective, the definition's `objectives_hash`, `challenge_date` the UTC day), unique for an account and a challenge. The reply is an object keyed by the challenge id as text: `{id, user_id, challenge_id, objectives, objectives_hash, completed: 1, challenge_date}`; a challenge that is not completed has no entry (the page reads a missing id as not completed), and a completed one is not reset by the hunt (a restore replaces the rows). The heartbeat's events also carry the receive time now: `events.ts`, set at schema 14 (older events have none and are outside every time window).

**Rules.** An objective's `conditions` is a Python expression over the animal `a` of the event, the events `e` of the hunt and the constants `c`; the server reads the subset below when it starts (`State.LoadChallenges`) and an objective it cannot read (an unknown event type, call or constant) is left out: it never completes. A challenge completes with its first met objective (every definition has one). The objective's `event_type` says which heartbeat event tries it: `KILL` event 2, `CONFIRMKILL` event 3, `SHOT_HIT` event 1, `CLUE` event 12, `COLLECTABLE` event 24, after the event is stored. Distances are millimetres (event 1 `distance_cms` x 10). The animal is the event's `individual`: its latest spawn (event 7) gives the species, `texture`, and the hits (event 1) since it are its wounds.

| Expression | Value |
|---|---|
| `or`, `and`, `not`, `==`, `!=`, `>=`, `<=`, `>`, `<`, `True`, `False`, numbers, text, `{...}` and `[...]` lists, `set([...])`, `name=value` arguments | as in Python |
| `c.species.<DEFINE>`, `c.variant.<DEFINE>` | the catalogue `species[]` entry with that `define` (a variant is the id of its own entry) |
| `c.item.<DEFINE>` | the catalogue `items[]` entry with that `define` |
| `c.collectableCategory.<CAT>` | the catalogue `collectables[]` ids whose `cat` is `<CAT>` |
| `c.state.FLYING`, `c.state.FLYING_NOT_SPOOKED`, `c.organ.HEART`, `c.texture.ALBINO`, `c.clue.BLOODTRAIL` | 6, 11, 1 (the bit of event 1 `organ2`), 2, 0 |
| `a.species()`, `a.variant()`, `a.texture()` | the spawn's species number (variant 100 and up count for their species; a harvest's `extended_species` for `a.variant()`), the same, its `texture` |
| `a.ethical()` | no hit has an ammunition the species does not permit |
| `a.range('minimum')`, `a.range('maximum')` | the shortest and the longest hit in mm, 0 without a hit |
| `a.wounds.count()` | the hits so far, the current one included |
| `a.wounds.ammo(set)`, `.weapon(set)` | there is a hit, and every hit's `ammunition_id` or `weapon_id` is in the set |
| `a.wounds.scope(False)`, `.scope(True)` | there is a hit, and every hit has no scope (`scope_id` -1) or has one |
| `a.wounds.organ(list)` | a hit has one of the organ bits set in `organ2` |
| `a.wounds.state(set)` | the event's own `animal_state` (event 2), else the last hit's, is in the set |
| `e.animalCountWithFilters(<species>, seconds=N, same_shot=True)` | the deaths (event 2) of that species (any without it) in the last N seconds by receive time, else in this hunt (since the hunt's `first_event`), and with `same_shot` only those of the shot (`shot_id` of the death, else of its last hit) that killed the animal of the event |
| `e.animalCount(N)` | the harvests (event 3) of the animal's own species in the last N seconds |
| `e.collectableCountWithFilters(collectable_category=<ids>)` | the pickups (event 24, one for each `collectable_id`) of this hunt whose `type` is in the ids |
| `e.trackType()`, `e.individualCount()` | the clue's `track_type`; the clues (event 12) of that type for that animal in this hunt |

Where the definitions do not say, a hit-wide condition holds for every hit except the organ (any hit), and a count of harvests counts the animal's own species.

**Announcement.** `update 3` (`type` 100, `timer` -1, the missions' colour and icon) with the definition's challenge-level `update_message` goes into the next heartbeat's reply, like a mission's notice.

**Restored rows.** The saved `Challenge_get_user_challenges...json` (`extras`) is copied into the rows at the restore, and at the schema 14 migration for the accounts restored before: each entry with `completed` 1 or `true` keeps its `objectives`, `objectives_hash` and `challenge_date` as saved (what is no text is kept as none); entries with `completed` 0 or `false`, keys that are no number and values that are no object are skipped, and a restore replaces the rows with the zip's, or with none.

## The hunterscore

On a community server the server computes every account's hunterscore and stores it in the account's `account` object (and in a restored account's Me/me profile); the recorded player's account keeps its recorded value, and a local server computes nothing. The value is the sum over the rows of `ranks` in the facts file of min(`cap`, the row's count):

- **animal**: the ethical kills of harvested animals of that species (variants, ids 100 and up, count for their species);
- **weapon**: the ethical kills made with any of the row's items, all ammo summed;
- **collectable**: the pickups of the row's types.

The counts are the account's statistics ([The statistics](#the-statistics): `animal.<species>.ethical_kills`, `weapon.<item>.<ammo>.ethical_kills`, `collectables.<type>.collected`; a key or counter that is not a number counts 0, a counter counts at most a billion, and a row adds at most `cap`), so a server kill counts once, through its hunt: once harvested (event 3) and ethical (no hit with an ammo not permitted on its species), for its species and for the weapon of its last hit. A pickup counts once, through its hunt, for its type: a heartbeat's event 24 (`collectable_id`, `type`) or event 36 (a shed antler, type 2000 + its `species_id`, a variant counting for its species); one `collectable_id` counts once per hunt, however often the game repeats it, and an event whose type or species is no 64-bit whole number counts 0. Nothing else counts: no missions, competitions, challenges, achievements or trophy scores. An id that is no whole number counts 0 (a kill without a species counts for no species, a weapon that is no id counts for the species only); a kill, a hunt or restored statistics the server cannot read back (a number that is not finite) count 0 and are logged (`statistics of account <id>: …`, `statistics: …`, `hunterscore of account <id>: …`). The stored value is always the computed one, never a backup's own.

A hunt's kills are credited when it closes: the client's disconnect, the next `Launch/start` after a quit, or a close after `HEARTBEAT_TIMEOUT` without a heartbeat. A timer in the server (every 30 s, every account, no game call needed) makes that last close, so a player who quit through the game's menu is credited within about `HEARTBEAT_TIMEOUT` + 30 s; a connect or expedition read closes it too. A heartbeat that comes after such a close reopens the hunt (it is open again with its kills so far), and its later kills are credited when it closes again. The hunt in progress counts too, at most one heartbeat behind: a heartbeat that brings a hit, a miss, a spot, a track, a harvest or a pickup of the open hunt recomputes the hunterscore with that hunt's counts so far (the same counts as when it closes, so nothing is counted twice). Only the achievements wait: a threshold is earned, with its time, when the hunt closes, so the hunt's page lists it. The game shows the new value at its next connect. A restore and every server start recompute it too, so the stored values follow the facts file of the running server.

**The board** is every account not banned, the recorded player's left out, highest first; equal hunterscores take consecutive places in account id order (a missing hunterscore is 0). `Me/me`'s `rank` and `/auth/me`'s `rank` are the account's place on it. The website's server and the launcher read it on `web_host`, with the player's `hr_session` cookie (401 `signed_out` without one): `GET /hunters?page=<n>` gives `{page, pages, rows: [{rank, handle, hunterscore, avatar}], around: [...]}`: the top 100, 30 rows a page (a page past the end is the last), and `around` the rows of the player's own window past the top ([Who sees whom](#who-sees-whom)); `GET /hunters/<handle>` (case does not matter) gives `{handle, hunterscore, rank, membership, avatar, background}` (the membership's title, `""` without one, the URL of the avatar picture, [Avatars](#avatars), and the header picture the player chose, null without one), or 404 for a handle that is not an account of a player here ([Public reads](#public-reads)). The website's server asks over `SERVER_URL`; on the public domain the reverse proxy routes `/hunters` and `/hunters/*` to the server ([docs/deploy.md](deploy.md#the-community-server)).

**The minigames' boards**: `GET /leaderboards/<lanebandit|bustthrough>?page=<n>` on `web_host`, the same way, gives `{page, pages, rows: [{rank, handle, value, avatar}], around: [...]}`: the top 100, 30 rows a page, and the player's window past it: the best scores, highest first (a best is the higher of the restored statistics' `lane_bandit_score` / `bust_through_score` from a backup and the best heartbeat event 17 `{game_id, score}` of the account's hunts, game 0 Bust Through, 1 Lane Bandit; a score that is not a whole number is ignored; the connect reply's `user_stats` carries the same two bests wherever the server builds it from its own rows), equal scores in account id order; an account without one, or with 0, is not on it, nor is a banned one or the recorded player's. A player who never restored a backup is on them once a hunt reported a score. Another kind answers 404. Each account is read on its own (two queries per account and page view).

## Leaderboards and seasons

The species boards rank every hunter's best harvests, per species, by **score** and by **range** (the longest last hit, in metres). A board is of one **season**: 0 is all time (every harvest below; never stored), the others are the `seasons` table (schema 29: `no`, `start`, `end` in Unix seconds UTC, both inclusive, `end` null for the current season).

| Season | Holds |
|---|---|
| 0 | all time: every harvest of seasons 1 and later |
| 1 | up to 2026-11-11 23:59:59 UTC: the harvests of the restored accounts that a restore brought, with their original time. These are the saved all-time bests of each species (the best score and the best range, `Leaderboard/personal` of season 0 as the backup saved it); the saved replies of the original's own seasons are not read. An account with no saved bests (a claimed archive account has none) brings its restored trophy list and the animals mounted in its restored lodges instead (a trophy in both counts once, a hidden one not at all): the best score of each species counts, with the trophy's own time. These rows have no shot distance, so they rank on the score boards and the Hall of Fame's score only, never on a range board, and their weapon is unknown |
| 2 | from 2026-11-12 00:00:00 UTC, the first day of the community server: this server's harvests (`harvests`). The current season until the operator starts the next |
| 3 and later | this server's harvests made from the day the operator started it |

A harvest belongs to the season its kill time falls in: a restored harvest of 2025, or one at 23:59:59 on 2026-11-11, is in season 1 and in season 0; one at 00:00:00 on 2026-11-12, or today's, is in season 2 and in season 0. A harvest of this server made before 2026-11-12 (a test hunt) is in season 2, since season 1 is the restored harvests only. A hunter has one row per species on each board: the best of its harvests of the season (a tie goes to the earlier kill, then to the lower account id). Left out everywhere, the boards and the personal bests: hidden harvests (the lodge editor's visibility), harvests the server refused (score 0: a disqualified kill, also one `admin ethics` disqualified afterwards), banned accounts and the recorded player's. The page of a kill sheet (`kill`) exists for this server's kills only; a restored row has `kill` 0.

**Board names.** A deer has two boards: one for its typical racks (species 0, 1 and 6) and one for its non-typical racks (100, 101 and 106), named as the original's page names them: the catalogue's own name, "Mule Deer (Typical)" and "Mule Deer (Non-Typical)", the same for Whitetail Deer and Blacktail Deer; every other species keeps its plain name. That name is the board's on the overview, the Hall of Fame, the personal bests (`GET /hunters/<handle>/best`) and the original's `leaderboards_all`; the website and the launcher translate it (the `Term.species.<id>` texts) and the typical and the non-typical board draw the same picture (`species-mule-deer`). That one name (`State.BoardName`) is also the species' name in every other list and item: the statistics tab (a species' typical row before its non-typical one), the hunting statistics and the operator's view (the two racks apart), the hunt page, the gallery and the harvest page (`speciesName`); an unknown species is `#<id>`. Only sentences (the ammunition and skill toasts, the inbox) and the missions keep the plain name, "Mule Deer".

**Typical and non-typical boards.** The whitetail, mule deer and blacktail each have two boards: the typical one (species 0, 1, 6) and the non-typical one (the variant 100, 101, 106), as the original kept them. A harvest of this server ranks by its variant (the kill's `extended_species`), in every season and all time. The original's saved bests (`Leaderboard/personal`) have no row of a variant, only typical species, so they are read as saved and a restored account with them has no restored non-typical rack on a non-typical board. They are kept as saved because none needs moving: of the saved replies of season 0 (`Leaderboard/personal`) held in the live database (22 accounts) and in the crawl of the original (546 players with a reply), the best score of whitetail, mule deer or blacktail is above that species' typical `max_score` (215, 246, 200) in 0 of them. `max_score` is the top of the trophy-rating range, not a hard cap; the score rule rests on the check against labelled racks of the original, where no typical rack was routed wrongly. A restored trophy (an archive claim) has the typical species whatever its rack and nothing else to tell it by, so it is told by its score: above the species' `max_score` (the facts file's `variants`, or the operator's `variants_file`) a typical rack cannot be, so it ranks on the non-typical board of that species; at or below it, from the variant's `min_score` up, it could be either, so it stays on the typical board and counts as unsure. The count is logged at the start (`leaderboards: N restored trophies could be non-typical racks ...`) and again whenever a restore or a claim changes it.

**Starting a season**: `POST /admin/season/start` (the [Admin API](#admin-api)) ends the current season one second before now and starts the next number now; harvests made from then on rank in it and the ended season gets a Hall of Fame. The original started a season on the first day of March, June, September and December since 2023; that is the proposed rhythm here, started by hand on those days.

**The Hall of Fame** of an ended season is, per species, the best score and the best range of it. Season 0 and the current season have none.

**Web calls** (`web_host`, JSON, with the player's `hr_session` cookie: 401 `signed_out` without one; `season` is a number, 0 for all time, the current season when left out, 404 for one that does not exist):

| Call | Gives |
|---|---|
| `GET /leaderboards/seasons` | `{current, seasons: [{no, start, end}]}`, oldest first; 0 is not listed |
| `GET /leaderboards/<score\|range>?season=` | `{boards: [{species, name, top: [Row]}]}`: a block per species with a ranked hunter, in species id order, the best 3 |
| `GET /leaderboards/<score\|range>/<species>?season=&page=` | `{page, pages, rows: [Row], around: [Row]}`: the best 100 of the species (its id), 25 a page, and the player's window past them ([Who sees whom](#who-sees-whom)) |
| `GET /leaderboards/<score\|range>/<species>/me?season=` | The `Row` of the hunter of the `hr_session` cookie, with its place on the whole board; 404 when it is not ranked, 401 when signed out |
| `GET /leaderboards/halloffame?season=` | `{rows: [{id, species, score: Row, range: Row}]}`, species id order; empty for season 0 and the current season |

`Row` is `{rank, handle, value, weaponName, ts, kill, avatar}`: `value` the score, or the range in metres; `weaponName` the weapon's name ("" where unknown); `ts` the kill's Unix seconds; `kill` its id for `/hunters/<handle>/score/<kill>` (0 for a restored one); `avatar` as on the hunterscore board. The hunterscore board and the minigames' boards above are unchanged.

**The original's calls** are answered from the same boards on a community server, for the pages that ask them: `Page_content/leaderboards_all` (`type` 1 score, 2 range: a block per species of the current season with its best 5), `Page_content/leaderboard_details` (`type`, `species`: the best 100, then the player's window past them), `Page_content/leaderboards_hall_of_fame` (`season_no`, default the season before the current one; a score is a text, a range a number; `[]` for the current season), `Leaderboard/current_personal` (`user_id`: the account's places, `[{species_id, score: {total, rank, animal_id, score}, distance_min: {...}}]`; `[]` for a player the caller cannot see), `Leaderboard/personal` ([Public reads](#public-reads)), and `season` in the reply of `Application/application` and `Application/client_application`, which is the current season. Each needs a player's token (else the expired-token reply).

**The boards are kept.** The boards are computed from the restored bests, the restored trophies and lodges, and the hit events of the harvests, with one connection per account (several seconds with the accounts of a live server, much of it parsing the restored lodges and trophies of the claimed accounts), so the server keeps them in memory. One scan reads every account once and makes every board at once: the score and the range board of all time and of every season. The set is kept for 15 minutes. The overview, a species' page, `/me`, the Hall of Fame and the original's board calls all read it. No request waits for a cold board: a background task computes the set when the server has started, and again 14 minutes after each computation (`State.KeepBoardsWarm`), and the old set answers until the new one is ready. Only a read that comes before the first computation has finished, or right after a drop (below), computes (on the request); callers that arrive meanwhile wait for that one computation. A new harvest therefore shows within 15 minutes, a restore or a ban the same way; `POST /admin/season/start`, deleting an account and hiding a harvest drop all kept boards at once. Every board reply of `web_host` is `no-store`: it is for one signed-in player.

## Who sees whom

No one can list every player of a community server, signed in or not. Every board (the hunterscore, the species boards of every season and all time, the Hall of Fame, the minigames') answers its top 100 (`State.BoardTop`) and, to a signed-in player, the rows of his own place and the 50 above and the 50 below it (`State.BoardAround`) that are past that top (`around` on `web_host`, the records after the 100 of `Page_content/leaderboard_details`). A page beyond the top answers its last page; no reply carries how many players a board has. `State.CanSee(caller, other)` is the rule of the reads that name another player: a player sees himself, his friends, and whoever a board shows him (the top 100 of any board, or within 50 places of his own on a board where he ranks).

## The statistics

`Statistics/total` and the connect reply's `user_stats` are the account's restored statistics, a base that play never changes (empty for an account that restored nothing), plus the sum of its server hunts. When a hunt closes (the ways in [The hunterscore](#the-hunterscore)), its statistics, the `stats` that `Public_user/expedition` shows for it, are stored with it: its events up to the next hunt's start, its harvests and pickups; a server start first stores those of any closed hunt that has none, and a harvest or pickup that lands in a hunt after its close (a heartbeat between a disconnect and the next connect) has that hunt counted again at once. The hunt in progress has statistics too, made from its events so far: each heartbeat that brings a hit, a miss, a spot, a track, a harvest or a pickup stores them again, flagged `open` (the close replaces them), and the sum adds them, so the profile's statistics, ranks and hunterscore are at most one heartbeat behind. `Public_user/expedition` and `GET /hunters/<handle>/hunts/<id>` make an open hunt's at the read instead: `duration` is the time since it started and `distance` stays 0 until it closes (a hunt's distance is told below). Making an open hunt's statistics reads only that hunt's events; the sum then adds the account's stored hunts, as at every close. The sum adds per species `spots`, `tracks`, `kills` and `ethical_kills`, per weapon and ammo `hits`, `misses`, `kills` and `ethical_kills`, per collectable type `collected` and `sum` (and the larger `max`, [Pickups](#the-statistics)), and the hunts' `distance` and `duration`; a weapon and ammo's `distance` (its longest hit) is the longest of the base's and the hunts'. A restored counter that is no number is replaced by the hunts' sum. A later restore replaces only the base: the server hunts stay and still add on top. On a community server every account has them; on a local server a restored account does (any other account replays its recording). The days of the account's completed [daily missions](#daily-missions) add to `daily_missions.<difficulty id>.days` the same way.

**A hunt's distance.** It is the game's own `distance_travelled` from its `disconnect`. The game sends none when the player quits through its menu, closes the window or ends the game from the launcher, so the server measures the walk too: every heartbeat carries the player's position (`xpos`, `ypos`, `zpos`, metres, `ypos` the height), and the open hunt keeps its latest horizontal position (`xpos` and `zpos`: `last_x`, `last_z`) and a running `walked`, the sum of the distances between consecutive heartbeats (schema 31). A step adds nothing when it is the hunt's first position, when the position is all zeros (the game does not know where the player is yet), when more than `HEARTBEAT_TIMEOUT` passed since the heartbeat before (the hunt had closed and this heartbeat reopened it), or when it is faster than `MaxSpeed` (25 m/s) over the time between the two heartbeats: a fast travel to a lodge or a tent moves the player in one heartbeat and is no walk. The step is a straight line, so a corner cut between two heartbeats (every 20 to 60 s) is a small error. When the hunt closes (the `disconnect`, a close after `HEARTBEAT_TIMEOUT` without a heartbeat, or the next `Launch/start`) its `distance` is the `disconnect`'s `distance_travelled` when it came with one, else `walked` in whole metres; a hunt with no position keeps none (0 in its statistics). A hunt reopened by a late heartbeat measures on and takes its distance again at its next close. A hunt closed before schema 31 keeps the distance it has: no positions were kept for it. `GET /hunters/<handle>/statistics` sends `distanceKm` (the restored statistics plus the server hunts) and `distanceKmHere` (the server hunts alone: the hunts `huntSecondsHere` counts, those started since `playtime_since`, the sum of their `distance`; the open hunt adds 0 until it closes, as in `distanceKm`).

`Expedition/list` pages the account's server hunts, newest first, by `offset` and `limit`; each shows its `collectables` (its pickups and shed antlers), and `Public_user/expedition` lists its shed antlers as `antler_collectables` `{id, species, score, type, ts}` (`type` `single` or `double`, as the original servers sent them; the in-game page reads `type`). The recorded ones, if any, follow them, and a page past the server hunts is the recorded page. `total` is the server hunts plus the recording's `total`, and `has_more` is true while a later page has entries. `Public_user/expedition` answers the hunt named by `expedition_id`, else the latest; an unknown id gets the reply an account without a server hunt gets (the recorded one, `null` on a community server).

**Pickups.** A pickup (heartbeat event 24) or shed antler (event 36) is answered with update 7 `{collectable_id, score, update: 7}` in the same heartbeat; the game asks every second until it comes. `score` is a text the HunterMate shows as is, and the game closes on a number. The score is `min + (max - min) * f` of the type's `score` range in the install's collectable definitions (`collectable.json` in the archives), or, for a type it gives none, in the facts file's `collectables`; a type with neither scores 0, `f` the share the game drew for the pickup: `scale` / 100, or the antler's `antlers_score_percentage`. The game draws `scale` as a half-normal value, and the original servers' scores of restored hunts spread over each range the same way. The text follows the site's own formats: `29g / 1.03oz` for a mushroom, `450mm / 17.72in` for a feather, `915y` for an artifact or recent fossil, `12m.y` for an old fossil, the score for an antler, `5 points` for event items, the rounded score otherwise. The score is kept with the event, and a hunt's statistics hold per type `collected`, `max` and `sum` (a text, three decimals, as the original servers); the profile totals add the counts and the sums and keep the larger `max`.

A restored account ([Import a backup zip](#import-a-backup-zip)) whose backup zip saved hunts also lists them: `Expedition/list` is the server hunts, then the saved hunts in the order the backup zip saved them, one paged list whose `total` is both counts and whose `has_more` is true while a later entry exists (the recorded reply is not used; a zip that saved no hunt keeps the recorded answers). `Public_user/expedition` with a saved hunt's id answers its saved reply, and without `expedition_id` answers the newest saved hunt (the highest id) while the account has no server hunt. A saved hunt is shown only: it is not added to the statistics or the hunterscore again, the restored statistics already hold it.

## Server playtime and hunting statistics

The hunt time of a profile (`Statistics/total`'s `duration`, the website's `huntSeconds`, the game's profile page) is the restored total plus the server hunts, as [The statistics](#the-statistics) say; it stays that. **Server playtime** (`huntSecondsHere`, `GET /hunters/<handle>/statistics`) is the sum of the server's hunts alone, read from `expeditions` (`State.ServerHunts`), so every hunt already played counts at once, with no migration and no estimate:

- a hunt that started before `playtime_since` (default the opening day, 2026-11-12 00:00 UTC) is left out;
- an open hunt lasts until its last heartbeat (so a game that crashed is not counted on and on); a closed one from its start to its end;
- a hunt that had no heartbeat after its start (no `last_seen`), open or closed, counts 0 rather than a guess. The count of such hunts is written to the server's log each time the figures below are computed (`hunting statistics: <n> hunts closed without a heartbeat count 0 s`), not on a profile read.

The hunterscore and the achievements keep using the statistics' `duration`, not this.

**Hunting statistics** come from the same hunts, the harvests (`kills`, `harvests`: the hunt a harvest was made in is the `expedition_id` of its page) and `wallet_log`. A harvest's gm$ is its `CSS Reward (Animal Id: <kill>)` rows as they were paid, so a [time-limited bonus](#harvest-gm-bonus) is counted as paid: the figures of a period that had one are higher. The mission (`gm$ reward`), community (`gm$ community reward`), daily (`Reward for Daily Mission`) and dog (`Dog level reward.`) rewards are not harvest rewards: only the operator's view lists them, apart, per player. Per reserve: the playtime of the hunts there, the harvest gm$ of those hunts, gm$ per hour, gm$ per harvest and harvests per hour. Per species: the harvests, their gm$, gm$ per harvest and harvests per hour, over the playtime on the reserves where its players harvested it (a player's playtime on a reserve counts once per species, whatever the number of harvests); `bestReserve` is the reserve where the species paid the most gm$ per hour at the base (the comparable figure, so a bonus never picks it), from the reserves where at least 5 players harvested it.

- **Players see no per-player hunting statistics**, on their own profile or another's: only the playtime is on the profile.
- **The operator** sees every player's figures, names included: `GET /admin/hunting` ([Admin API](#admin-api)), with the 5-player floor off.
- **`GET /stats/hunting`** is one page for every signed-in player (the same rule as the other player reads of [Who sees whom](#who-sees-whom); it follows the sign-in rule of #750 with its default): server-wide figures only, no name, handle or rank. A row built from fewer than 5 players (`State.HuntingMinPlayers`) is not shown, so no one player can be singled out. `since` is `playtime_since` as an ISO UTC time.
