- Both are computed on demand and kept for an hour (`State.HuntingTtl`), so a new figure shows within the hour. The recorded player's account and banned accounts are left out. Each account is read on its own connection, like the [boards](#leaderboards-and-seasons).
- **Comparable figures and events.** Next to the paid `gmPerHour` and `gmPerHarvest` (harvest gm$ as paid, a bonus included), each row has `comparableGmPerHour` and `comparableGmPerHarvest` built from each payment's base (the amount without any bonus, [Reward bases and events](#reward-bases-and-events)), and the page has `events`: the events (name, kind, start, end, multiplier) that overlap the period since `playtime_since`. While `events` is not empty the paid figures include their bonuses and are not comparable with a period that had none; the comparable ones are. `GET /admin/hunting` has `events` too, and `baseGm` next to `gm` of every reserve and species row.

## Achievements

On a community server the server awards the achievements an account's [statistics](#the-statistics) reach; the game works out an unlock itself and never tells the server. Whenever the hunterscore is recomputed (a hunt's close, a restore, a server start; the recorded player's account is left out, as there), every definition of the connect reply's `achievements` is checked: its `value` path names a counter of the statistics, and each of its `triggers` thresholds at most that counter which has no earn time yet gets an earn time: the close time (Unix seconds) of the hunt that has just closed (the latest one when several closed together, as a server start or a timer can close more than one), else the current time. A hunt's page lists the thresholds whose earn time is its close ([Public reads](#public-reads)). The account's row keeps the counter, in whole numbers, as its `value` (a larger stored one, a backup's, stays); a row is made once the counter is above 0. Earn times already stored, a restored backup's too, are never changed, so the game does not announce an achievement again. A restore replaces the rows with the backup's, and the check right after it awards again what the server hunts reach. `Achievement/stats` (the user id of the player or of a player he may see, [Public reads](#public-reads)) and the connect reply's `user_stats.achievement_stats` carry the rows, and the profile page header (`earned_achievements`) counts the thresholds with an earn time (the achievements tab's `unlocked` counts those of the connect reply's definitions).

A path's segments are split on `.`. The first is the root: `animal`, `weapon`, `collectable` and `daily_missions` are those objects of the statistics (`collectable` is `collectables`), `distance` is the metres / 1000 (km) and `duration` the seconds / 3600 (hours). The segment after an object root names its entry: a species define of the application catalogue, an item define of the install's item definitions, a daily mission kind (`EASY`, `MEDIUM`, `HARD` are 1, 2, 3), or the id itself; the entry is that exact id (a species variant, id 100 and up, is its own entry, unlike in the hunterscore), and later segments are keys as they are. `*` sums every child, `[a,b]` the children listed, and the last segment is the counter (`ethical_kills`, ...). So `animal.*.ethical_kills` is every ethical kill, `weapon.<ITEM>.*.ethical_kills` those of one item with any ammo. A name that is unknown, an entry that is missing or a counter that is no number counts 0.

## Friends

On a community server two players are friends when both have an account here and either one's restored friend list (`User/friendslist` and `Me/friends` of the backup, kept in `extras`) holds the other, or when one accepted the other's request here. The `friends` table keeps one edge per pair and direction: `request` (the account asked the other) or `friend`. A restore adds the edges of its lists (`friends`, incoming `requests`, outgoing `pending`): a `friend` edge replaces a request, any other edge the pair has already stays, so the order of two players' restores does not matter and a friendship removed here comes back with the next restore of a list that holds it; an edge to a player without an account here counts once that account exists, and never for a banned account or the recorded player's. Deleting an account drops its edges both ways. A local server replays its recordings.

**Old user ids.** A restored list names players by the user id they had in the game, but an account that claimed an archived profile ([Archive claims](#archive-claims)), or had a zip restored onto it after it played, keeps its own id. So each end of an edge is the account that now holds that profile (`Store.Holder`): the account with that id (it kept its own, or took the zip's), else the account a zip of that user id was restored onto (its `restored_ids` row), else the account whose restored claim has that `claims.archived_uid` (the first one, when several do). An id nobody holds yet is stored as it is. When its profile is claimed or restored later, the edges that named it move to that account in the same transaction (`Store.ReconnectFriends`), so two friends who both claim are friends here whichever claimed first. At every start the server moves the edges that name the old id of each restored zip or claim the same way (idempotent, no schema step, so the work follows the claims and restores, not the friends table), which also connects the edges of claims made before this rule. When a zip of a profile is approved for another account after one claimed it, that account becomes the holder: the edges made from then on go to it, and the friends the claimant already has stay with the claimant. A moved edge replaces the old one, so a friendship removed here is not made again by a start. A zip restored with `import backup --account` onto an account with another id leaves no record of its user id, so edges to it wait until that profile has an account of its own.

| Call | Answer |
|---|---|
| `POST /v1/Friend/add`, form `oauth_access_token`, `friend_user_id` | Accepts the other player's open request (they are friends, the request is gone), else asks them; nothing more when they are friends or asked already. `true`; `{"errorCode": 400}` for the player's own id, one with no account here, and any id other than a player of the player's friends, requests (either way), multiplayer session or one he may see ([Public reads](#public-reads)): asking any id would name that player in the website's list of the requests sent |
| `POST /v1/Friend/remove`, same form | Drops every edge of the pair: declines or cancels a request, or ends a friendship on both sides. `true`; `{"errorCode": 400}` as above |
| `POST /v1/Me/friends`, `oauth_access_token` (form, or a JSON body) | `{friends, friendCount, requests, requestCount, pending}`: `friends` and `requests` (the players who asked) are entries `{id, handle, avatar, fullbody_avatar, hostname, hunterscore, online}` (`fullbody_avatar` 0 without one, `online` as `Public_user/getByHostname` has it for a friend, always false for a request), sorted by `handle` in upper case, ordinal; `pending` the user ids the player asked |
| `POST /v1/User/friendslist`, form `user_id`, `offset`, `limit`, with the player's token | `{total, friends}` of the token's own account only (a player sees only his own friends): every friend in `total`, one page of the same entries in `friends`. `limit` 1 to 50 (above 50: 50; missing, empty or below 1: every friend); `offset` missing, empty or negative is 0, past the end an empty page. Another player's `user_id` (known or not): `{"total": 0, "friends": []}`; no `user_id`: `{"errorCode": 400}`; an empty or non-positive one: `null`; a non-numeric `user_id`, `offset` or `limit`: `{"error": 1, "code": "handlerError"}`; without a token: token expired |

`Me/poll` and `Me/me` carry `friend_requests`, the number of `requests`. The current build's `/v1/friend/add?locale=<locale>`, `/v1/friend/remove`, `/v1/me/friends` and `/v1/me/poll` with a JSON body are the same calls ([The current build](#the-current-build)).

## Messages

The player's own inbox, on a community server. `Chat/conversations` lists the server's notice conversation (sender `Server`, user id 1024, the notices of [Upload a backup zip](#upload-a-backup-zip)) first, then the conversations a backup held (`Chat_conversations.json` in `extras`) exactly as saved, owner-only (the token's account). `Chat/conversation` with `user_id` answers the notice thread (and marks it read), or else the saved `Chat_conversation__user_id=<id>.json` of that partner as it is, or an empty thread; reading a restored thread marks nothing, so a restored conversation keeps the `read` it was saved with. Sending messages between players is not part of the server.

The website reads the same through `GET /account/messages` and `GET /account/messages/thread` ([Upload a backup zip](#upload-a-backup-zip)), read only. A message is the player's own (`mine`) when its `user_id` is not the partner's.

**Messages from the operator.** `admin message --account <user id or handle> "<text>"` writes the notice sender's message to one account, `admin message --all "<text>"` to every account (it prints the number of accounts and asks `[y/N]` first; `--yes` answers it and is needed when the input is not a terminal, as in `docker compose exec -T`). The admin API does the same: `POST /admin/accounts/<account>/message` and `POST /admin/message`, body `{text}` ([Admin API](#admin-api)). The text is plain text of 1 to 2,000 characters, stored and shown as written (nothing is read as HTML); a name that is no account is refused and nothing is sent. The message is the row a review decision writes (sender `Server`, user id 1024), so the game's inbox and the launcher's and website's messages show it as an unread message of the notice thread, with the notices; nobody else gets anything. The unread count is the `unread_messages` of `Me` and `Me/poll` and `Chat/conversations` lists the thread: the heartbeat carries no message, so a hunt in progress shows it when the game next asks for them, otherwise at its next start.

`admin messages --sent [--account <user id or handle>]` lists what was sent, newest first: time, recipient (`account <id> <handle>`, or `all (N accounts)` for `--all`: one line per message, not per account) and text; with `--account` the messages to that account and those to everyone. The log is the `admin_messages` table (schema 33); a line to one account goes with that account when it is deleted.

**Replies.** `admin messages [--account <user id or handle>]` lists the replies of players to the server, newest first: time, account, handle and text, from every row of the player's `messages` that does not come from the notice sender. **No client can write one today:** the game's `Chat/message` is answered with an error (and `Chat/get_message_settings` says the player takes no private messages), and the launcher and the website only read the inbox, so the list stays empty until a client can. A message that asks a question must therefore say where the answer goes (the Discord server): write that into the text.

## Gallery

A photo the game asks for (heartbeat event 4) gets a row in `photos` and an upload link; the file it sends is stored as `photos/<account id>/<id>-<photo_type>-<token>.jpg`, the thumbnail as `<id>-<photo_type>-<token>-thumb.jpg` (none for the automatic shot screenshot, `photo_type` 14). `<token>` is the first 32 lower-case hex digits of an HMAC-SHA256 of `photo:<account id>:<id>`, keyed with the secret the server keeps beside its database (`state.db.jwtkey`): the picture and its thumbnail, uploaded in either order, share it, and a link cannot be guessed from the sequential photo ids and the public account ids. The row's `file` holds the name and every link is built from it. A requested photo that was never sent is no photo. The gallery of an account is its uploaded camera photos (`photo_type` 1) and trophy shots (`photo_type` 2) and the pictures a restore unpacked (below), newest first; every other type of an uploaded photo (the automatic screenshots the game takes by itself, the avatar picture) is stored but never listed, zipped or linked. A photo shows the kill of the animal it was asked for while (the same `individual` and the same latest event 7 as the kill's hits; `photos.spawn` holds that event, as the schema of version 8 added): `animal` is that kill's species name and `label` its score (both `""` without a kill), `thumbnail` the link of the small picture (the picture's own link when the game sent none), `url` the full picture. `Gallery/list` links the game's host; the website's `GET /hunters/<handle>/gallery` links `https://<web_host>/photos/<account id>/<file>`, which the server serves on `web_host` too (the reverse proxy routes `/photos/*`, [Behind a reverse proxy](#behind-a-reverse-proxy)). **At start**, every server renames the photos still stored under the earlier names `<id>-<photo_type>.jpg` (and their `-thumb` files) to their token names and updates their rows, whatever the type; a file that is gone only updates the row, and a second start changes nothing. The earlier links stop working.

**On the website's host** a picture is served to a signed-in player only: the `hr_session` cookie, or the link `GET /hunters/<handle>/gallery` gives that player (`?viewer=<account id>&until=<Unix seconds>&key=<key>`, `<key>` the first 32 hex digits of an HMAC-SHA256 of `photo-view:<viewer>:<account id>/<name>:<until>`, keyed with the same secret; it holds until the end of the next day (UTC), so the launcher's pages, which send no cookie, load it). Without either it answers 401 `signed_out`, and `GET /hunters/<handle>/gallery` itself answers 401 without a session. The gallery and the hunt page link each picture by an opaque name, `/photos/<account id>/display-<hex>.jpg` (`<hex>` the first 32 hex digits of an HMAC-SHA256 of `photo-display:<account id>/<file>`, so it never tells the original's name, which the game's host serves without a session or a read limit): the original, at full size, for every signed-in player. Another player's read of it counts in the reader's read limit (429 `rate_limited`). The owner's Share window hands out the bare link, which shows the picture to a signed-in player and answers 401 to anyone else. The pictures are sent with `Cache-Control: no-store`. The imported avatar (`avatar.jpg`) is served to anyone, as before. The game's host serves the originals as before (the game uploads them and `Gallery/list` links them); the website's hunt page (`GET /hunters/<handle>/hunts/<id>`) gives a kill's `photo` (its trophy shot, as the keyed link above) to every signed-in player on any hunt.

| Call | Answer |
|---|---|
| `POST /v1/Gallery/list`, form `oauth_access_token` (a player's token), `user_id` (the player's own without it), `offset`, `limit` (missing or below 1: all) | `{total, photos: [{id, type, url, thumbnail, label, animal}]}`: nothing current calls it (the game has no gallery screen; the website and the launcher read `GET /hunters/<handle>/gallery`, and delete with `POST /account/gallery/delete`; only the backup tool and the restore use the original's endpoint, against the official servers' answer or a saved copy): the player's own, or a player he may see ([Public reads](#public-reads)); anyone else, a banned account, the recorded player's and an unknown id are `{"total": 0, "photos": []}` |
| `POST /v1/Gallery/delete`, form `oauth_access_token`, `photo_id` | `true` after deleting the player's own photo (its row and files), `false` for anything else, another player's photo included |

A kill's `photo` in `Public_user/expedition` and the kill details page is the link of the newest trophy shot (`photo_type` 2) of its animal, `null` without one: a camera photo or an automatic screenshot is never a kill's picture. A restore unpacks the pictures of its zip into the same folder ([Pictures](restore.md#pictures)): its gallery pictures are rows with `photos.saved` set (schema 10: the saved `label` (the entry's, else its kill's score), `animal` (the species) and `kill_id`, listed where no kill is found here; a real `Gallery/list` entry's `animal` is an object `{scoreType, species_id, score, id, species}`, null for a camera photo, and the details are read from it) and get the links above; a saved kill's `photo` is the link of its stored picture, else of the newest restored trophy shot (type 2) whose saved `kill_id` is the kill's id (set when a saved hunt is answered, in the game reply and the website's hunt page, never written to the saved reply); the game's kill details page (`/user/<handle>/score/?id=<kill>`) of a saved kill is its saved page, sent with `Content-Security-Policy: sandbox` so that no script of a zip runs on this host. A restore again replaces them all; the photos taken here stay. A restore that makes the account adopt the backup's user id (`review approve`) moves `photos/<old id>/` into `photos/<new id>/` with its rows: the folders are merged, and on a name clash the account's own photo is kept; the photo links follow, as they are built from the account id. The website's gallery tab and its delete and download are in [Public reads](#public-reads) and [Upload a backup zip](#upload-a-backup-zip).

**A trophy shot of another member's kill** (multiplayer, [The harvester's trophy shot](#multiplayer)) is the shooter's photo: its row is in the shooter's `photos` with `kill_id` his kill and `taker` the harvester, and the file the harvester's game uploads is stored in the shooter's folder under his name (`photo:<shooter's id>:<id>`), once: an upload to a part (the picture or its thumbnail) that is already stored answers 404, so the harvester cannot replace it afterwards. It is that kill's picture (`photo` above) and shows in the shooter's gallery with its species and score, and he can delete it. The harvester's gallery lists it too, as the same picture with the shooter's kill (`animal`, `label`; on `GET /hunters/<handle>/gallery` also `kill`, his kill's id, and `hunter`, his handle), except while the shooter is banned (hidden like a banned account everywhere else); the harvester's zip holds it, but `Gallery/delete` and the Share window are the shooter's only. A restore that gives either account another user id moves the link with it.

## Avatars

Every account shows one picture: an avatar of the game's list, or the one imported from its backup. The list is the recorded `Avatar/getAvatars` ([Starting data](#starting-data)): its ids (`1000000` and on). Their pictures are this server's own, `<game host>/media/avatar/256/<id>.png` (`<game host>` is `https://<game_web_host>`; the website and the launcher get the website's copy, below): the server never hands out the list's own address, which names the original's avatar host. The account holds its choice in its connect `account` object: `avatar_url` (the picture's address), `face` (the avatar's id) and, where the object has them, `avatar` and `avatar_id` (the same id); a restored profile's `Me/me` has the address as its `avatar`, and `Public_user/getByHostname`, `Me/poll` and the friend entries read it from there. The game reads these values in its connect reply, so a changed choice shows in the game at its next start.

**A reply for the website or the launcher** (the boards, a profile, the friends, the messages, `GET /account/avatars`, `Me`) gives an avatar of the list as the website's own copy, `<site>/images/avatars/256/<id>.png` (`<site>` is `https://<web_host>`, the stock copies of [images](images.md)); the imported picture is `<site>/photos/<account id>/avatar.jpg`. A reply for the game, the in-game board pages included (`leaderboards_all`, `leaderboard_details`, the Hall of Fame), keeps the game host.

An address is an avatar of the list when its path ends with the path of that avatar's picture in the list, whatever its host: an account's `…/media/avatar/256/<id>.png` on the web host is the list's `…/256/<id>.png`.

- **A new account** has the default avatar, `1000000`, and the `gender` of its list entry (`m` is 1, `f` is 0; the male default gives a male body). The game draws the character from `gender` and `face` of the connect `account`, so a choice shows at the player's next game start.
- **Choosing a listed avatar** (`POST /account/avatar`, which the website, the launcher and the sign-up carousel use) sets `gender` from its list entry together with `face`. Choosing the imported picture changes only the picture (`avatar_url`, the restored profile's `avatar`, and `avatar`/`avatar_id` where the account has them) and keeps `gender` and the current `face` when that is a 3D head of this gender, so a head the player chose stays with his own picture; only a `face` that is no such head gets the default head of the gender. To change the head under his own picture, the player chooses a listed avatar (its head) and then his picture again; the settings say so ([docs/website.md](website.md#avatars)).
- **`face` is always a 3D head.** The avatar list has three series of 18 pictures (`1000xxx`, `1001xxx`, `1002xxx`) of the same 18 heads; the game builds the body (arms, hands) only from the first series' ids (and the store's own head). So a listed picture `id` writes `face` `1000000 + id mod 1000` (same head, same gender: picture `1002007` gives face `1000007`) and keeps the picture id in `avatar`, `avatar_id` and `avatar_url` for the pages. A restore, a claim and a picture import of an imported picture give the default face of the account's gender (the first listed head of that gender); choosing the imported picture later keeps the head (above). A restore, a claim, a picture import and an avatar choice all write `face` and `gender` together by this rule, so the two always agree.
- **`admin faces [--apply]`** fixes the accounts from before: an account whose `avatar_url` is a listed picture takes that picture's head and gender; with the default address `1000000` (every account starts with it, and a restore without a picture writes it) the account's `face` says, when it is a listed avatar; any other account (its own picture) keeps its gender, and keeps its `face` when that is a 3D head of this gender (the store's own head too), else gets that gender's default face. It prints the counts, and without `--apply` changes nothing. The pictures (`avatar`, `avatar_id`, `avatar_url`) are not touched, so a woman with the default address still shows `1000000` on the pages. A second run finds none.
- **Store avatars.** The store sells avatars (catalogue category Services, sub-category Avatars) that the recorded list does not hold. The facts file's `store_avatars` names each one's picture id, and the server adds it to the list after the recorded avatars (only when the catalogue has its `item`), with `unlock_item_id` the item and `unlocked` false. An account can choose it once it owns the item (an inventory row, however it got it); until then it is shown locked and refused. The picture is served like every other avatar's (the operator's `avatar-<id>.png`, else the stock copy `avatars/256/<id>.png`). A restored profile that names one keeps it.
- **At start**, a community server gives an account whose `avatar_url` is an avatar of the list under another address (a row from before: the original's avatar host, which the game no longer reaches) this server's address, in its `avatar_url` and its restored profile's `avatar`; its other values stay. A second start changes nothing.
- **At start**, a community server also gives an account that never restored anything (no restored id, restore audit or claim) the `gender` of its listed avatar when they disagree. A second start changes nothing. Restored accounts are brought in step by `admin faces`.
- **A restore** ([Pictures](restore.md#pictures)) sets them from the zip's profile: the picture's address is an avatar of the list: `avatar_url` is this server's address of that avatar (`<game host>/media/avatar/256/<id>.png`) and `face` that avatar's 3D head (`id mod 1000` on the first series' ids, above); else, when the zip holds the picture, it is the account's *imported avatar*: stored as `photos/<account id>/avatar.jpg` (besides its `images_` copy), `avatar_url` is its link on the game host (`/photos/<account id>/avatar.jpg`) and `face` the default 3D head of the profile's gender; else (no picture in the zip, or no address) the default avatar's address and that same face. The restored `gender` is the profile's, or the listed avatar's when the picture is one. A restore again replaces the imported avatar; the account's choice of a game avatar is replaced like the rest of the profile.
- **At start**, a community server gives an account whose `avatar_url` is the address of a picture the player uploaded on the official site, and whose restored copy (`photos/<account id>/images_…`) is in its photo folder, that picture as its imported avatar (a copy at `avatar.jpg`, `avatar_url` and the restored profile's `avatar` the link above; its `face` stays). A picture with no copy stays as it is and shows the default avatar.
- **The picture** of the imported avatar is the photo link `/photos/<account id>/avatar.jpg`, on the game host and on `web_host` ([Gallery](#gallery)), served as `image/jpeg` whatever the bytes are.
- **The game's avatar pictures** `<game host>/media/avatar/256/<id>.png` are the operator's `avatar-<id>.png` in `images_dir`, else the stock copy `avatars/256/<id>.png` there, served as `image/png`: `GET /media/avatar/256/<id>.png` on `game_web_host` (where an account's `avatar_url` points) and `GET /256/<id>.png` on `avatar_host`. Only an `<id>` of the list is answered (written as a number, no leading zero), so the path never leaves the folder: any other id or path, and any other host, is a miss. The notice sender's picture (`256/10000000.png`, the avatar of a notice in the inbox) is answered the same way (`avatar-10000000.png`, else its stock copy, else the default avatar's picture). An id with no file of either kind is served the default avatar's picture; without that file too it is a miss. The folder is read at each request, so a new file shows at once. A local server and a community server answer the same.
- **An uploaded avatar the zip does not hold**, only its link: fetched from `<avatar_uploads>/uploads/thumb/242x242/<uid>.jpg` (without `avatar_uploads`: the base of the recorded avatar list's address) under the rules of `picture_link_hosts` and stored as the imported avatar, as above ([Uploaded avatars](restore.md#pictures)); `admin avatars [--account <id or handle>] [--dry-run]` does it for the accounts already restored: every account (or one) whose avatar is the default or a dead upload link gets the uploaded avatar its restored data names (the `avatar` of its profile, saved Me/me or Public_user/getByHostname reply, the first upload link wins; with none of them, the archived uid of its claim). The default avatar `1000000` is never the player's choice, even where the restore wrote it into the profile: it is ignored, so a save whose profile says the default but whose saved Me/me names an upload, or whose claim has no avatar in its data, is fetched. It prints one line per account, `<id> <handle> <result>` (`set`, `no uploaded avatar`, `fetch failed`; `--dry-run` fetches nothing and prints `would fetch the avatar of user <uid>`). An account whose avatar is already a stored picture, or a stock avatar other than the default, is not touched. The avatar host must be in `picture_link_hosts`, except for a uid with a saved copy (`avatar_uploads_dir`, [Run it](#run-it)).

What the website is told (`avatar` in `GET /hunters/<handle>`, the board rows, the friends and conversations of `/account/` and the friends tab of a profile; also `GET /auth/me`) is one URL: the imported avatar's link on `web_host`, this server's address (`<game host>/media/avatar/256/<id>.png`) for an avatar of the list, whatever address the account stored (a row from before names the original's avatar host), and `null` for anything else (an address that is none of the list, an imported picture that is gone), where the page shows its default avatar. A list reply carries each player's, so a page of players takes one request. The notice icon of the heartbeat (`destination`) and the notice sender's avatar are on `<game host>/media/avatar/` too.

**The stock copies** are kept so the avatars outlive the original's avatar host: `<images_dir>/avatars/256/<id>.png` for every id of the list (256 pixels square, the only size the game and the website ask for), and `10000000.png` (the notice sender), also served by the website at `/images/avatars/256/<id>.png` (a subfolder is not a slot: it is not in `/images.json` or the missing list). To refresh them after the list changes, copy the new ids' pictures from a saved copy of the avatar host into that folder (or add `avatar-<id>.png`, which wins over the stock copy and is the website's slot picture); the folder is read at each request, so nothing restarts. Uploaded avatars (`/uploads/thumb/242x242/<uid>.jpg`) are not stock copies: a restore reads the saved copy `<avatar_uploads_dir>/242/<uid>.jpg` (`avatar-uploads/` next to `settings.json`, in the folder the server already mounts), else fetches it from `avatar_uploads`, and keeps it as the account's imported avatar; the sweep `admin avatars` does the same.

| Call | Answer |
|---|---|
| `GET /account/avatars` | `{current, ids, locked, imported, face, own}`: the player's avatar as above (`null` for none), the ids of the game's avatars it can choose in the order of the list (the free ones and the store's it owns), `locked` the store avatars it cannot yet, `[{id, item, name}]` (`item` the store item that unlocks it, the launcher and the website link it), the link of the imported avatar (`null` without one), `face` the 3D head the game draws the player with (the account's `face`: it is the id of the listed avatar of the same number, `null` for an account with none) and `own` true while the current picture is the imported one. 401 when signed out |
| `POST /account/avatar` | Form `id`: an id of the list, or `imported`; same checks as buy. Sets `avatar_url`, `face`, `avatar` and `avatar_id` (and the restored profile's `avatar`) as a restore does, and for a listed avatar `gender` (account and restored profile) from its list entry (`imported`: the link above only; `face` and `gender` stay when the face is a 3D head of that gender, else the gender's default `face`), and answers like the `GET`. `404` `invalid` for an id that is not in the list, a store avatar whose item the account does not own, or `imported` without an imported avatar; 401 when signed out |
| `POST /v1/Avatar/getAvatars` (API host, the game's own call) | `[{id, define, name, url, gender, unlocked, unlock_item_id}]`: the recorded list, then the store avatars. `unlocked` is true for a free avatar and for a store avatar whose `unlock_item_id` the token's account owns (without a token: false) |

## Wallet history

`Wallet/transactions` (em$), `Wallet/silver_transactions` (gm$) and `Wallet/spent` answer the account's own history on a community server; another player's `user_id` gets the empty reply, and a local server replays its recordings. A page is 50 rows from the form's `offset`, newest first by `created`: the rows of the restored backup zip's pages (`Wallet_transactions`, `Wallet_silver_transactions` in `extras`, as saved) and this server's own rows. A row is `{id, created, type: {id: C|D, name}, amount, preBalance, postBalance, summary, mission, items, qty}`: `amount` is never negative (`type` gives the direction), `items` are the catalogue's definitions of what was bought (one entry for each kind, `qty` the copies of each). `Wallet/spent` is `{spent, silver_spent}`: the restored totals (`Wallet_spent`) plus the payments made here. `Wallet/bank_transactions` is still the empty reply: the server has no bank.

This server's rows are the `wallet_log` table (schema 11): `State.AddCurrency` writes one row for every change of a balance, in its transaction: a purchase (summary `In-game payment.`, with the item ids; the game's `Inventory/buy` and the website's [buy](#buying-from-the-website)), a kill's gm$ reward (`CSS Reward (Animal Id: <kill id>)`; the operator's `/admin/rescore` pays a missed one the same way), a dog's level reward (`Dog level reward.`, [Dog level rewards](#dog-level-rewards)), a mission's reward (`em$ reward` or `gm$ reward`, [Missions](#missions)), a daily mission's (`Reward for Daily Mission`), an event's community mission's (`em$ community reward` or `gm$ community reward`) and an operator's adjustment ([Admin API](#admin-api); `Adjustment by the operator.`). A change that is refused (a balance too low) writes none. A taxidermy or a skull mount is a purchase of the store item 744 or 1111 (`Taxidermy (immediate)` in the catalogue; [Taxidermy](#taxidermy)), so it counts in `Wallet/spent`; one the operator undoes is given back with `Taxidermy undone by the operator.`; a price of 0 writes no row.

The website reads the same rows with the session cookie: `GET /account/wallet/ems` and `/account/wallet/gms` (query `offset`) answer the array above, `GET /account/wallet/spent` the totals; 401 `signed_out` without a session.

## Reward bases and events

A payment made under a bonus keeps what it would have been without it, and which event changed it, so a figure over a period that had an event can be compared with one that had none, and an event's effect can be measured.

- **`wallet_log.base`** is the amount without any bonus and **`wallet_log.bonus_event`** the `events_log` row that changed it (empty: none); `amount` stays what was paid (schema 34). `State.AddCurrency` takes both (`baseAmount`, `bonusEvent`); without them the base is the amount and there is no event. A harvest's gm$ (`CSS Reward`) passes them while a [harvest bonus](#harvest-gm-bonus) runs: `amount` is about `base` times the multiplier (the CSS is rounded to one decimal before it is paid, so a small amount can be off by one). The mission, daily and community rewards are paid with base = amount and no event, as no mission bonus exists yet: the mission bonus of #830 passes its own `baseAmount` and `bonusEvent` (an event of kind `mission_bonus`, applies to `mission_reward`) to `AddCurrency` at the same call sites, so a harvest bonus and a mission bonus that overlap stay apart.
- **`events_log`** (`id`, `name`, `kind`, `start_ts`, `end_ts`, `multiplier`, `applies_to`; Unix seconds, the period is `start_ts` ≤ t < `end_ts`) is one shared table. `kind` is `gm_bonus`, `mission_bonus`, `seasonal` or any word the operator uses; `multiplier` and `applies_to` (`harvest_gm`) are empty for an event without a money bonus, such as a seasonal event whose animals and reserves change how players hunt.
- **A bonus in `settings.json`** (`harvest_gm_bonus`) is written as soon as the server knows it, at the start or when the file changes, if it has not ended (a bonus that starts later is written already): name `name`, default `Harvest gm$ x<multiplier>`, kind `gm_bonus`, from its `from` until its `until` (the time it was read, when it has no `from`). It is the same event as an existing one of the same kind, multiplier and end, whatever its name and start, so restarting the server, saving the file again, or a backfill, never writes it twice.
- **`admin event add <name> <start> <end> [--kind seasonal]`** writes a period without a bonus (ISO times, no zone means UTC; an identical one is not written twice); **`admin event list`** prints the events, one per line: `id | name | kind | start .. end | multiplier`. Seasonal events are not guessed: the operator adds each one the server ran (for example the Halloween 2026 period) with `admin event add`.
- **`admin backfill-rewards --events <file> [--apply]`** gives the payments made before the log existed their base, once. Run it with the server stopped, after `backup`, and only after the deploy of this schema: the first start of the new server, and the dry run too, adds the columns and the table to the database. Without `--apply` it writes nothing and prints a line per event and the counts. The file is a JSON array of events, the dates being the operator's: a bonus has `multiplier` and `"applies_to": "harvest_gm"`, an event without a bonus only `name`, `start` and `end` (and an optional `kind`). For each bonus, a harvest payment (`CSS Reward`) inside its period that has no base yet gets base = paid ÷ multiplier (rounded as the payment was) and that event; every other payment that has no base gets base = amount; the events are written (an existing one of the same bonus gets the file's name and start). A payment that already has a base, made since the deploy, is never touched, so the bonus that was running at the deploy changes only the payments before it, and a second run changes nothing.

  **A rescore inside a period.** `admin rescore` pays a missed harvest without the bonus, so such a payment must not be divided. The backfill checks it: a `CSS Reward` row inside a period whose payment came more than 10 minutes from the time the server received its harvest event (`events.ts` of the kill's `individual`) is reported, `account <id>: CSS Reward (Animal Id: <kill>) paid <n> at <time> long after its harvest (a rescore?): not divided, base = amount`, and gets base = amount and no event. A row whose harvest event has no time (made before schema 14) is taken as the heartbeat's own. Read those lines in the dry run before `--apply`.

  **Harvests that paid 0.** A harvest whose gm$ rounds to 0 (no score, or a CSS under half a gm$) has no payment row, so the payments counted are fewer than the harvests made (for the ×2 weekend, 2,669 payments for 2,678 harvests); the totals are over the payments.

  For the ×2 weekend and the ×1.25 bonus that was running at the deploy, save this as `events.json` in the data folder:

  ```json
  [
    {"name": "Harvest gm$ x2 weekend", "kind": "gm_bonus", "applies_to": "harvest_gm", "multiplier": 2, "start": "2026-11-21T21:48:00Z", "end": "2026-11-23T05:00:00Z"},
    {"name": "Harvest gm$ x1.25", "kind": "gm_bonus", "applies_to": "harvest_gm", "multiplier": 1.25, "start": "2026-11-24T13:19:51Z", "end": "2026-11-27T15:00:00Z"}
  ]
  ```

  and run `admin backfill-rewards --events events.json` (the dry run: check the counts), then `admin backfill-rewards --events events.json --apply`. The total of `base` over the weekend's harvest payments is then the harvest scores' gm$ at ×1.
- **Statistics** ([Server playtime and hunting statistics](#server-playtime-and-hunting-statistics)) keep the paid figures and add `comparable` ones built from `base` (a row from before the backfill, with no base, counts its amount), and list the events that overlap the period.

## Placed items and stacks

A heartbeat changes an item's place and units, one account's rows only:

- **Placing**: event 25 `{item, position_x/y/z, direction_x/y/z}` (millimetres and x 1000, numbers within ±2,000,000,000, the restore's limit of 2,000,000 metres, rounded to whole numbers: the units of a restore, [restore.md](restore.md#field-rules)) stores `__position`, `__direction` and `__reserve` in the item's metadata; the reserve is the one of the account's newest hunt (the web's number, from 1; none without a hunt). A position that is not three numbers is ignored. Event 8 `{item, slot: 23}` then puts the item in slot 23.
- **Picking up**: event 8 `{item, slot}` with any other slot sets the slot and removes the three keys, so a restored placed item stays picked up at the next connect.
- **Moving units**: event 33 `{item_from, quantity, item_to?}` takes `quantity` units from a stack and adds them to `item_to`, a stack of the same item; with no `item_to` nothing moves (the client's way of splitting a stack is not known). A stack that reaches 0 units moves to slot 0, as for event 6. Nothing moves when a stack is unknown, has no units, holds fewer than `quantity`, is of another item, or `item_to` is `item_from`.

The connect reply gives a placed item its `position` and `direction` (the restored rows' shape, without the reserve). `Inventory/list` and `Inventory/dict` (`{<item id>: [{id, slot, units, metadata, location}]}`; own user id only, another gets `{}`) give an item in slot 23 with a position `location: {reserve, pos: {x, y, z}, dir: {x, y, z}}`, `pos` in metres and `dir` the direction divided by 1000 (left out when the item has no direction); every other item has `location: null`.

### Bait feeders

A placed feeder (an item whose catalogue row has `bait_item`) keeps its timers in its metadata, in Unix seconds, and every reply that carries the metadata (the connect, `Inventory/list`, `Inventory/dict`, the admin inventory) works its shown fields out for the time of the reply, so nothing has to run in between:

```
bait_amount(t)   = max(0, bait_amount_org - floor((t - start_bait_ts) / 8640))      # 10 units a day
penalty_remaining = ceil((1800 - (t - shot_ts)) / 60) minutes, while t - shot_ts < 1800
feeder_status(t) = Penalty  if bait_amount > 0 and penalty_remaining > 0
                   Active   if bait_amount > 0 and t - delay_start_ts >= 14400      # 4 h warm-up
                   Inactive otherwise
bait_attractiveness(t) = 100 if Active, else 0
```

A row without `bait_amount_org` and `start_bait_ts` (a feeder never filled) is left as it is.

- **Bait added in a hunt**: event 23 with `bait_amount` on a feeder. When the amount is above what the feeder holds, `bait_amount_org` is the new amount and `start_bait_ts` and `start_attr_ts` are now. An empty feeder restarts its warm-up (`delay_start_ts` now, `bait_attractiveness_org` 0); one that still holds bait keeps `delay_start_ts` and gets `bait_attractiveness_org` 100, so it stays active. Only an item whose catalogue row has `bait_item` is a feeder; event 23 `bait_amount` on any other item is merged as it is. A lower or equal amount (the game also sends the amount when bait is eaten) changes nothing. The amount is clamped to 0 to 50.
- **`Inventory/refillBarrel`** (the Bait Sites page's Refill, community server only): JSON `{barrel_id, currency, max_amount}`. The bait the account owns for the feeder's kind (the `define` the feeder's `bait_item` names) is moved into it up to `max_amount` (at most 50), by the rule above, and leaves its stacks at once. A hunt that is running does not see a website refill until its next connect: the bait stays in the hunt's inventory and the feeder keeps its old fields there until then. The paid refill takes the account's own bait too: the original's Refill is enabled only with bait in the inventory, and its recorded refill moved the stock held into the barrel. The service is paid with `pack` (one use of an owned refill pack, item type 38), or `gold` (em$) or `silver` (gm$) at the price of the refill service (item 791), which leaves no item; the new balance comes as update 15 with the next heartbeat. The reply is `{price, success: true, metadata}` with `start_attr_ts`, `bait_attractiveness`, `feeder_status`, `bait_amount`, `start_bait_ts` and `bait_amount_org`; `price` is 0 for a pack (the original's reply for a pack is not known). `{"success": false}` for an invalid request, a feeder that is not the account's, no bait or no room, no pack, an unknown service or too little currency.
- **Shots near a feeder**: a hit on an animal a feeder is baiting is reported by the game as event 23 on the feeder with `bear_baited` in its `metadata` (any value; the game has checked the range). The server sets the feeder's `shot_ts` to its time then, so the feeder is in `Penalty` for 30 minutes, and a later hit restarts them; `bear_baited` and the rest of that event's metadata are not saved. On an item that is not a placed feeder of the account the event changes nothing. Open: whether a host can name another player's feeder (it is ignored for now).
- **Refills before the rule**: until the rule existed, event 23 only merged `bait_amount` and left the old timers, so such a feeder stayed inactive and its amount fell to 0. At every start, for each feeder with refill events newer than its `start_bait_ts`, the rule is applied again to those events in order, at their own times (so the warm-up counts from the first add, the amount and `start_bait_ts` from the last). A feeder the rule handled has `start_bait_ts` at its last refill and is left alone, so the step is idempotent and needs no schema number.

### Game management

Settings, Game Management of the website and launcher (#935) manages the player's own lodges and placed feeders on `web_host`, with the session cookie (401 `signed_out` without one); a state-changing request (`PUT`, `POST`) also needs the website's same-origin `Origin` or `X-Requested-With` (else 400 `invalid`). Integers are written without a fraction.

- **`GET /account/lodges`**: `{lodges: [{id, name, locked, style_id, lodge_type, trophies, weapons}]}`: `id` a string, `lodge_type` the display name of the lodge's type in the facts file (null when it has none), `trophies` and `weapons` the animal slots with a plain `trophy_id` and the weapon slots with a `weapon_id` in the lodge.
- **`PUT /account/lodges/<id>/settings`**: JSON `{name, locked}` (the lodge's style stays) as [`PUT /game/user/lodges/<id>/settings`](#lodges-and-trophy-visibility) takes it (same validation, same rows): `200` `{ok: true}`, `400` for a body or name that is not valid, `404` for a lodge the account does not own.
- **`GET /account/feeders`**: `{feeders: [{id, item_id, name, reserve, reserve_name, x, y, bait_amount, bait_max, status, empty_at, bait_owned}], packs, refill_price: {em, gm}}`: the account's placed feeders (placed items whose catalogue row has `bait_item`), by reserve then id. `id` is the item guid as a string, `name` the catalogue's, `reserve_name` the application catalogue's, `x` and `y` metres, `bait_amount` and `status` (`active`, `inactive`, `penalty`) as [Bait feeders](#bait-feeders) refreshes them, `bait_max` 50, `empty_at` Unix seconds (`start_bait_ts` + 8,640 × `bait_amount_org`; null for a feeder never filled), `bait_owned` the units of the bait it takes the account holds, `packs` the uses left in its refill packs, `refill_price` the refill service's em$ and gm$ price (0 when it is not for sale).
- **`POST /account/feeders/<id>/refill`**: JSON `{currency: pack|em|gm, max_amount}` runs `Inventory/refillBarrel` for the feeder (`em` is its `gold`, `gm` its `silver`; same rule, charge and bait use): `200` `{success, price, bait_amount, empty_at}` with the feeder as it is after (`success` false, nothing changed, when there is no bait, room, pack or money), `400` for a currency or a `max_amount` outside 0 to 50 that is not valid, `404` for a feeder the account does not have.

## Lodges and trophy visibility

A lodge is the rows it has (`lodges`, one row of `/game/user/lodges` and one of `/game/user/lodge` per lodge id, as read): the ones a restore stored for the account, and one for each unit of a lodge item the account buys in the store (a catalogue item of the facts file's `trophy_lodge.lodges`, bought in the launcher, on the website or in the game, or added with `admin item`). A new lodge is named for its layout (`Rustic Lodge`), unlocked, style 0, empty, with `lodge_engine_id`, `lodge_type_define` and `lodge_type_id` (the item id) of its layout and the account's next lodge id (a whole number above every lodge id of the account). At start, and after each restore (a restore brings the lodge items of the inventory, and the lodge rows only when the save had them), a community server adds the lodges an account lacks, idempotently: one for each unit of a lodge item it owns beyond the lodges of that type it has (a purchase made before the store added lodges, or a restore's inventory). **A new account starts with no lodge**: the start kit ([Starting data](#starting-data)) holds none, and the original's free start did not either; the selection page then says the player owns none. On a community server the player edits them, with a Bearer token, on API v2 and at the same path under `/v1` on the game's web host (the game sends the JSON body as `text/plain`; the body is read whatever its content type, and the web host's JSON-to-form rewrite ([The current build](#the-current-build)) leaves a PUT alone):

| Call | Effect |
|---|---|
| `PUT /game/user/lodges/<id>`, `{name, animal_slots: [{slot_id, trophy_id, pose_id, rotation, base_texture, is_skull}], weapon_slots: [{slot_id, weapon_id, rotation}]}` | Sets `name` (at most 100 characters) and the two slot lists (at most 200 slots each; the largest lodge has 163) on the lodge's stored rows (a key the body lacks stays). A slot needs a numeric `slot_id` and keeps only the keys shown, each a number, true, false or null (`base_texture` also a text of at most 100 characters, the texture's name). A slot that holds the same trophy or weapon as the stored slot of its `slot_id` keeps the keys the body lacks (`trophy_details`): the game sends a save whenever the player leaves the lodge. The save also sets, from the slot's type (the lodge's `lodge_engine_id` → the catalogue's `lodge/<engine>` slot of that `slot_id` → its `type`), `is_full_body` on every animal slot (true for the types 4, 6, 7, 8, 9: the round displays and platforms; false for the wall plaques and skull mounts; the catalogue carries no flag, so this table is the rule) and `rotation: 0` on a mounted slot of a type that `can_rotate` (`slot/animal`) and has none; a slot the layout does not list, and a lodge whose layout the catalogue lacks or whose `lodge_engine_id` is no whole number, are left as they are; a stored `is_full_body` that is no true or false is rewritten. The same rule is applied once to the stored lodges at start and after each restore (the lodge backfill), writing only rows that change, so a second start changes nothing |
| `POST /v1/game/user/lodges/<id>` (API host), form `name`, `style_id` | What the game sends when the player leaves a lodge or opens the editor. It stores nothing: the game sends the `name` and `style_id` it read when it loaded the lodge, which would undo a rename or a style that the editor or the selection page saved since. It answers 200 for the account's own lodge, 404 (the web API's error code) for another or an unknown one |
| `PUT /game/user/lodges/<id>/settings`, `{name, locked, style_id}` | Sets the keys the body has (`name` a string of at most 100 characters, `locked` true or false, `style_id` a number or null) |
| `PUT /game/user/trophies/<id>`, `{hidden}` | Sets `hidden` (true or false) of the trophy: on the restored rows of `/game/user/trophies` and of `Trophy/list`, and, for a harvest made here (a kill id), in the `hidden` column of `harvests` (schema 9) |

Each answers 200 with no body (the game does not read it). The lodge pictures (`GET /content/lodge/lodges/<name>.png` on the game's web host, `<name>` of lower-case letters, digits and `_`) are read from the install's archives, else from the static host's copy; any other name is 404. The selection page's hide button asks the in-game locale script for `hun_web_gui_hide`; a locale script without it is served with it added (`Hide`), unchanged if it already has it. A body that is not as shown is 400 (`application/vnd.goa.error`, `bad_request`) and changes nothing; a lodge or trophy the account does not have is 404 (so one player cannot edit another's); a request without a valid token is the expired-token reply ([Accounts](#accounts)). On the API host (`/v1`) a 400 or 404 is `{"errorCode": 400}` or `{"errorCode": 404}` with status 200, the web API's shape.

The lodge reads of any account (`GET /game/user/<id>/lodges[/<lodge>]`, [Public reads](#public-reads)) send the stored rows. A slot with a `trophy_id` and no `trophy_details` gets the trophy's row of `/game/user/trophies` as `trophy_details` (a hidden trophy stays in the lodge it was mounted in; `null` when the account has no trophy with that id). `locked` is sent as stored: the game hides a locked lodge from other players. A hidden trophy is left out of `Trophy/list` of another player and of the profile page's trophies; the player's own lists keep it. The trophy rows of `/game/user/trophies` (and so `trophy_details`) say where each trophy is mounted now, from the stored lodges: `lodge_id`, `slot_id` and `pose_id` of the slot that holds it (a harvest made here too), or `null` for a trophy whose row named a lodge the account has and that no slot holds any more; a row of a lodge the account has not stays as restored.

**Big lodge notice (#974).** The game keeps a lodge's trophies in its memory after the player leaves it (they are loaded when the lodge is chosen and stay until another lodge is chosen), and a lodge of about 100 trophies adds roughly 180 MB of textures to a 32-bit process that a hunt already fills to 2.5 or 2.9 GB of its 4 GB, so the game can close during the next hunt. An account that owns a lodge with 75 mounted trophies or more (`State.BigLodge`; a mounted trophy is an animal slot with a `trophy_id`, counted once per id over the lodge's two rows) gets one inbox message from the notice sender (`NoticeBigLodge`): "Your trophy lodge <name> holds <n> trophies. The game keeps a lodge's trophies in memory after you leave it, and with about 100 or more it can close during the next hunt. To avoid it: keep a lodge under about 60 trophies (move some to another lodge), or restart the game after visiting it before you hunt." With several such lodges the message names the largest (the lowest lodge id on a tie). It is sent at start to the owners that already have such a lodge (`BackfillLodges`, with the rest of the lodge pass), after a restore (the same call) and when a lodge save (`PUT /game/user/lodges/<id>`) first brings a lodge to 75, and once per account, ever: the message is its own marker (a later start, restore or save finds a message of the notice sender that starts with "Your trophy lodge " and sends none; a restore keeps messages), so no table was added. The opening words "Your trophy lodge " are therefore a contract: rewording them would send the message to every qualifying owner again (the words after them may change), and an operator's `admin message` that starts with them would count as the marker. The lodge's name is cut at 60 characters with an ellipsis. Only a plain (number or text) `trophy_id` counts as a mounted trophy, and the notice never fails the save or the start: whatever it throws is logged (`lodges: no big lodge notice (...)`) and the surrounding work goes on. The server limits nothing: a lodge is still sent and stored whole, because the game saves all the slots it received when the player leaves his own lodge, and a shortened reply would delete the hidden trophies at the first exit. Tests: `BigLodgeNoticeTests` (75 and not 74, mounted slots and not empty ones, once across starts, restores and saves, the largest named, a save that reaches 75, the lodge sent whole). The accounts it reaches at the first start are counted by the lodges of 75 or more `animal_slots` with a `trophy_id`: `SELECT l.account_id, CAST(l.id AS INTEGER) AS lodge, COUNT(DISTINCT j.value) AS mounted FROM lodges l, json_each(l.node, '$.animal_slots') s, json_each(s.value, '$.trophy_id') j WHERE l.source IN ('/game/user/lodge', '/game/user/lodges') AND j.type <> 'null' GROUP BY 1, 2 HAVING mounted >= 75` on `state.db` (the lodge's two rows count once; the number of different `account_id` is the number of accounts, minus those that were sent it already).

**The page reads.** The game's Trophy Lodge Selection page and the lodge editor make these reads, all answered on API v2 and, for the current build, at the same path under `/v1` on the game's web host:

| Read | Answered by |
|---|---|
| `GET /game/user/lodges`, with the player's token (the selection page's own list) | The lodges of the token's account, the rows of `GET /game/user/<id>/lodges` for it; `[]` for an account with none. An unknown or missing token is the expired-token reply ([Accounts](#accounts)). `OwnLodges` in the API document |
| `GET /gameengine/trophylodge/lodge/<lodge_engine_id>` (the selection page reads `image_url` of each of the player's lodges; a missing layout breaks the page) | The facts file's `trophy_lodge.catalogue` page `lodge/<engine>`: the layout's picture, styles, animal and weapon slots, for layouts 1, 3, 5 and 6; no token. Any other layout is the host's miss reply. The picture itself is `/content/lodge/lodges/<image_url>` (`/static/img/lodge/lodges/<image_url>` in the editor's own script, rewritten as above): a website file, answered like every `/content` and `/static` file (the archives, the recordings, the mirror; [How a request is answered](#how-a-request-is-answered)) |
| `GET /gameengine/trophylodge/animal`, `skull`, `weapon`, `slot/animal`, `slot/weapon` (the editor's icons and slot types) | The facts file's page of that name; no token |
| `POST /v1/Me/friends` (the friends tab) | The friends, as before ([Friends](#friends)); the page then reads each friend's lodges |
| `GET /game/user/<id>/lodges` (a friend's or a gameplay player's lodges, the editor's lodge list) | The stored rows of the account `<id>`, for the player himself or a player he may see ([Public reads](#public-reads)); a locked lodge is sent and the page hides it |
| `GET /game/user/<owner>/lodges/<id>` (entering a lodge, the editor's slots) | The stored lodge with its trophies, or the replay's 404 (`null` under `/v1`) |
| `GET /game/user/trophies` (the editor's trophy list), with the token | The account's trophies, each with where it is mounted: the restored ones (always, all of them), the animals mounted in the restored lodges that have no row (a public restore has no own list; each is in the lodge's `trophy_details`; saving a lodge or hiding one stores its row, and a new kill id is above it), and the harvests made here that were taxidermized, or whose grace window is open ([Taxidermy](#taxidermy)); any other harvest is no trophy. Newest first: restored rows (and mounted animals) by `timestamp`, harvests by their harvest time, in one order; rows without a readable date last |
| `PUT /game/user/lodges/<id>`, `PUT /game/user/lodges/<id>/settings`, `PUT /game/user/trophies/<id>` (the editor's save with its style choice, the selection page's lock and rename) | The edits of the table above |

## Taxidermy

A harvest made here can be taxidermized, or its skull mounted, once, in the game and from the player's own hunt page. The harvest then is a trophy: it is in `/game/user/trophies` (the lodge editor's list; a skull mount with `is_skull` true, for the editor's skull slots) and its harvest page has `taxidermized` true. A harvest that is not taxidermized is not in that list, except in the grace window below. The trophies a restore brought are not harvests made here: they are all listed, as always.

**A non-typical rack** (whitetail, mule deer and blacktail; `extended_species` 100, 101 and 106) is a trophy of its typical species, as the original sent it: its harvest page and its row of `/game/user/trophies` (and so a lodge slot's `trophy_details`) have the typical `species` (the variant mod 100) and its `species_define`, and the skull check reads that define. The game has no model for the variant and would leave the trophy out of the lodge editor. The harvest is stored with its variant and mapped when it is read, so the harvests stored before this are listed too; the restored rows are sent as they are. The variant stays in the kill's `extended_species`, which scoring, the hunterscore, missions and challenges read, in the stored harvest the leaderboards and the hunt's kills read, and in the score page's `killData.variant`, whose `species` is the typical one.

**The grace window.** Harvests made before the taxidermy went live were all in the lodge editor. Their window ends 72 hours after the deploy, not after the harvest: `taxidermy_grace` (schema 28, one row `start`, `grace_end`) is written once, at the first start with this code (`start` now, `grace_end` now + 72 hours; a database made new has `start` 0, so it has no such harvest). A harvest whose `timestamp` is before `start` has `end` = `grace_end` (the offer's `taxidermy.end` and the check of the game's path alike), same price and rules; until `grace_end` it is also listed in the editor, after it only if it was taxidermized. No date is in the code.

A harvest that is already mounted in a lodge slot is not taken out of its lodge by any of this: the slot's `trophy_details` ([Lodges](#lodges-and-trophy-visibility)) are read from every harvest of the account, taxidermized or not, so the lodge keeps showing the animal after the window ended or an operator's undo. Only the editor's list of what can be mounted is narrowed.

**The rules** (`State.Taxidermize`, one transaction): only the account's own harvest made on this server (a harvest a restore brought is never offered: 404, like an unknown kill or another player's); once, either product (409 `already`); not for a kill the ammunition rule disqualifies (403 `disqualified`, as the original's harvest window offered no mount for one; the player's page offers none for it while its window is open); within 72 hours of the harvest (`timestamp` of its page; the grace end for an older one; 410 `too_late`); a skull mount only for a species of the lodge catalogue's `skull` page (400 `no_skull`); the price in the currency the player picks (402 `not_enough` when the balance is lower; nothing changes). A kill taxidermized or mounted in a lodge before the rule was applied to stored kills keeps its mount (it was paid for); `admin ethics` flags it `kept`. The price is the facts file's `taxidermy` (10 em$ or 2,000 gm$, the same for both products; 0 is free). It is paid with `State.AddCurrency`: a wallet row `In-game payment.` for the store item 744 (taxidermy, `Taxidermy (immediate)`) or 1111 (skull mount), and the new balance reaches the game with its next heartbeat (update 15). The harvest page keeps what was paid (`taxidermy: {currency, price, ts}`) for the operator's undo.

**In the game** the harvest window sends `POST /v1/harvest/<kill id>/taxidermize` on the web host of the current build (JWT as `Authorization: Bearer`; API v2's `POST /game/user/harvest/<kill id>/taxidermize` for the old build). The body is `{"currency": "gold"|"silver"}` (gold is em$, silver gm$), with `"is_skull": true` for the skull mount; the handler also reads the form the current build's JSON becomes on its way here. The answer is 200 with no body (the game logs only a status outside 200 to 399), else the status above with `{"error": ...}`.

**On the player's own page.** `POST /account/harvests/<kill>/taxidermize` on `web_host` (the `hr_session` cookie and the header `X-Requested-With`, like the other account writes), body `{"product": "taxidermy"|"skull", "currency": "ems"|"gms"}`: 200 `{done, ems, gms}` with the balances after; 402 `{error: not_enough, currency}`; 409 `{error: already}`; 410 `{error: too_late}`; 404 for an unknown, restored or another player's kill; 400 for anything else, a skull for a species without one included. `GET /hunters/<handle>/hunts/<id>` shows the offer: when the `hr_session` cookie it carries is the handle's own, each kill made here has `taxidermy: {end, ems, gms, skull, done}` (`end` ISO UTC: the harvest plus 72 hours, kept after it passed so the page can hide the button; `skull` whether the species has a skull to mount; `done` `taxidermy`, `skull` or null). A restored kill, and the page of anyone else, has no `taxidermy`.

**The operator** lists the taxidermized harvests and undoes one ([Admin API](#admin-api)). An undo makes the harvest a plain one again (it leaves the editor's list, and can be bought again within its 72 hours) and gives the price back unless asked not to; a lodge slot that held it is not changed.

## Loadouts

An account's loadouts are its rows of `loadouts` (schema 12): `id` (unique across the accounts: it is the key of the save), `name`, `color` and `entries`, the list of items. Nothing deletes one. A restore reads the saved reply of `/game/user/loadout` (the extras file `game_user_loadout.json`) into rows, in order, under new ids (the saved ids are the game's own; an entry that is not an object is left out; a name that is no text, a color that is no whole number or items that are no list are read as `""`, 0 and `[]`); the migration to schema 12 did the same for the accounts restored before it. A purchase adds one default loadout (`<handle>'s Loadout #<n>`, `n` counting the account's loadouts, `color` 0, no items) for each unit of item 811, the loadout slot the store sells, that is delivered: bought alone or in one of its bundles, by `Inventory/buy` or the website's store, or granted by the admin.

On a community server, with the player's Bearer token, on API v2 and at the same path under `/v1` on the game's web host. The in-game page's list call carries the token by itself; its saves carry it only because the served script is rewritten ([The current build](#the-current-build), "The loadout saves send the player's token"): as the original page sends them, they have no token and get the expired-token reply.

| Call | Answer |
|---|---|
| `GET /game/user/loadout` | `[{name, color, items: [{slot_id, item_id, item_guid}], id}]`, ascending by `id`, `[]` for none: `application/vnd.game_data.user.loadout+json; type=collection` (`application/json` under `/v1`). `item_guid` is the dog's (slot 29) and null for every other item |
| `PUT /game/user/loadout/<id>`, `{name, color, items: [{slot_id, item_id, item_guid}]}` | Replaces all three of the account's loadout and answers 200 with it in the `GET` shape; the inventory is not touched. The body is read as JSON whatever its content type (the page sends `json`). `name` is 2 to 50 characters, `color` 0 to 11, `items` at most 200 entries, each with a whole `slot_id` of at least 1 but not 23 (the placed items' slot), a whole `item_id` of at least 1 and an `item_guid` that is absent, null or a whole number; any other body is 400 and changes nothing. A loadout the account has not (another player's, an unknown id) is 404, a request without a valid token the expired-token reply ([Accounts](#accounts)); on `/v1` a 404 is `{"errorCode": 404}` with status 200 |

The page's CORS preflight for either call is the replay's, with the headers it asks for allowed.

A non-zero `loadout_id` of `Launch/start` or `POST /v1/session/start_single_player` that is one of the account's loadouts is put on in the launch's transaction, before the connect, as the in-game page does when a loadout is clicked: every owned item goes to slot 0 except a placed one (slot 23, which no loadout lists); then each entry in order takes the owned row of its `item_guid` (a dog), else the first row of its `item_id` still in slot 0, and gives it the entry's `slot_id`. An entry with no such row, or with a slot below 1 or 23 (a restored loadout may have one), is skipped and the others still apply. While the connect template has `inventory.active_services.UNLIMITED_AMMO` set, an ammunition entry (catalogue category 3) with no free row clones a row the account owns of that item (a full stack), so one ammunition fills several slots. The slots stay after the hunt: they are the equipment last saved. A `loadout_id` of 0, of another account's loadout or of none changes nothing. A tutorial (`start_tutorial`) puts none on.

An ammunition entry of a loadout is skipped when the loadout puts a weapon in the slot 6 below its slot (8 to 10) that it does not fit, so no launch equips a non-fitting pair ([Equipped ammunition must fit its weapon](#equipped-ammunition-must-fit-its-weapon)).

## The store catalogue

`GET /catalog/store` on `web_host`, community server only (a local server answers the miss reply), for a signed-in player (the `hr_session` cookie; 401 `{"status": "signed_out", "message": "Sign in first."}` without a session): the website's server calls it over `SERVER_URL` with the player's cookie ([docs/website.md](website.md#signed-in-players-only)) and the launcher on the public domain, where the reverse proxy routes `/catalog/` here ([docs/deploy.md](deploy.md#the-community-server)). It is the same for everyone. The answer is `{categories: [{key, name, children: [{key, name}]}], items: [{id, name, variant, description, category, ems, was, gms, gwas, sale, buyable, unlimited, fuel, limit}], bundles: [{id, name, description, price, was, sale, buyable, items: [{id, name}]}]}`, made from the application catalogue the server loaded ([Starting data](#starting-data)):

- **Items**: those that are not hidden. `category` is the key of the item's sub-category. `ems` is today's em$ price: the catalogue's, or the price of the [sale](#store-sales) that lowers it now, the rule `Inventory/buy` charges by; `was` is the em$ price before the sale (null when the em$ price is not on sale: always null without a `store_sales` sale). `gms` is today's gm$ price rounded to a whole number (the catalogue's `silver_price`, or the sale's price when a sale lists gm$ in its `currencies`), and `gwas` the gm$ price before the sale (null when it is not on sale); it is 0 for an item with no gm$ price. `sale` is null or `{name, percent, until}` of the sale that lowers a price of the item now (the larger percentage when two do; `until` an ISO time, the end of the sale's current period). `buyable` is false for an item that is `notbuyable`, is a service (`Inventory/buy` does not sell services either), or has neither an em$ nor a gm$ price of at least 1 (the starter items cost 0); an item sold for one currency only has 0 as the other price. The catalogue's inventory weight is not in this list; the item's page has it as `capacity` (below).
- **Categories**: a category's key is a sub-category's, and a top-level one is keyed by its default (first) sub-category, the way the store links to it, so its page lists only that sub-category's items; `all` is the first. One with no item left (the store's own link to its bundles) is left out. An item whose sub-category no category lists is only in "all".
- **Bundles**: `price` is the bundle's price rounded to a whole number, or its [sale](#store-sales) price while a sale lists it (`was` is the list price then, null otherwise; `sale` as for an item, null otherwise), `gms` its gm$ price (17 × the em$ list price, or the sale's price while a sale that lists gm$ names it; `gwas` the gm$ price before the sale, null otherwise; see [The gm$ rate](#the-store-catalogue)), the items named from the catalogue, a hidden item too (an unknown id keeps an empty name). `buyable` is false for a bundle whose price is below 1 or that lists an item the catalogue does not have, or a license while `all_licenses` is on ([Licenses](#licenses)): [buying](#buying-from-the-website) refuses it.
- **The gm$ rate**: the catalogue prices its items at **1 em$ = 17 gm$** (`State.GmPerEm`): 958 of the 962 items sold in both currencies have `silver_price` = 17 × `price`, in every category (checked 2026-11-27 against the recorded catalogue); only the taxidermy services (10 em$ = 2,000 gm$, 12 em$ = 2,200 gm$) differ, and they keep their own prices. So everything the store sells can be bought with gm$: an item's gm$ list price is the catalogue's `silver_price` when it is at least 1, else 17 × its em$ price (at least 1); an item with neither price stays unsold, and hidden items, unlimited ammunition and fuel and the taxidermy services keep their rules. A bundle's gm$ price is 17 × its em$ list price, then the sale's percentage (a sale naming `gm`) and the share of the items the player owns ([Bundles](#bundles)), rounded up to a whole gm$ and at least 1. The same derived price is what the website, the launcher and `Inventory/buy` (`SILVER`) charge, what `gm` sales lower and what a random sale's `min_price_gm` and `max_price_gm` filter on, and what the game's `Application/application` reply carries as `silver_price` for an item the catalogue sold only in em$ (so the game's store can offer it in gm$; a bundle is not in that reply, so the game's own store sells bundles in em$ only, while the website and the launcher sell them in gm$ too).
- **Unlimited ammunition**: while `unlimited_ammo` is on, an ammunition item (category 3) has `unlimited` true and `buyable` false, a bundle that lists one is not buyable, and a bundle made only of ammunition is left out of `bundles` ([Unlimited ammunition](#unlimited-ammunition)); `unlimited` is false for every other item, and always when the setting is off.
- **Unlimited fuel**: while `unlimited_fuel` is on, the three fuel items (953, 954 and 955) have `fuel` true and `buyable` false, and a bundle that lists one is left out of `bundles` ([Unlimited fuel](#unlimited-fuel)); `fuel` is false for every other item, and always when the setting is off.
- **Limit**: `limit` is the most copies of the item a player owns ([Ownership limits](#ownership-limits)), 0 for no limit. The list is the same for everyone: what a bundle costs a player who owns some of its items is `GET /account/bundles` ([Bundles](#bundles)).

Only a recorded `Application/application` has categories and bundles: a catalogue built from the install alone has the items and empty lists for the others, and one with no item answers 404 `{"error": "no catalogue"}`. A recording is found by the host names of the settings (`api_host` and the others): with names that differ from the recording's, the server does not see it and falls back to the install. The reply is some 0.7 MB for a catalogue of about a thousand items, so the website keeps it for 5 minutes. It is built from the catalogue and the operator's sales, not replayed (the recorded `Application/discounts` is not read).

### An item's page

`GET /catalog/item/<id>` on `web_host`, community server only, for a signed-in player like the store (401 `signed_out` without a session), for an item the store lists (a hidden or unknown one answers 404 `{"error": "no item"}`). It holds what the item's page shows beyond the list, which would make the list ten times larger if it held it for every item; the website's server and the launcher ask for it when a page opens. The answer is `{capacity, contents, fits, ethical}`:

- `capacity` is the catalogue's inventory weight divided by 1000, the number the store shows as the capacity cost (it has no unit); 0 for an item without one. `contents` is `{count, name}` for an item the catalogue gives units (a pack of cartridges, a refill of uses), else null.
- `fits` is `[{title, key, items: [{id, name, variant}]}]`: the items of the catalogue's `fit` lists, one entry per group, under the title of the category of that number and its `key` (the store's key of that category, as `GET /catalog/store` names it; the clients translate the title by it) (a weapon lists its sights and its ammunition, an ammunition and a sight the weapons). A hidden item, which has no page, is left out; a catalogue built from the install alone has no `fit` lists.
- `ethical` is `{ammo: [{id, name, variant}], species: [{id, name, permitted: [bool]}]}` (`id` is the species' id, for the client to name it in the player's language) for a weapon (over the ammunition that fits it) and for an ammunition (over itself), else null: one row per species of the catalogue that has permitted ammunition (the dogs have none, so no row) and one flag per ammunition. The flag is the rule [kills are judged by](#scoring-and-paying-a-harvest) for a normal animal: the ammunition is in the species' `ethical` list (a non-typical variant uses its species' list), so a silver round shows as permitted only on the species whose list holds it (the werewolf and the wendigo): the page knows no animal, so it shows silver as not permitted on the species with a possessed version, where a possessed animal permits silver only. The page and a hit's verdict read the same code, so they cannot differ.

## Weapon families

Every weapon item of the catalogue (category 1: the store's rifles, shotguns, handguns, bows, muzzleloaders and event weapons) is in exactly one **weapon family**, one weapon type: its finishes and its engraved, event and creator variants. `State.Weapons` (`WeaponFamilies`, `WeaponFamilies.cs`) is the one place that decides it. The shooting skill follows it ([One shooting skill per weapon type](#one-shooting-skill-per-weapon-type)), and so will the changes that read a family (the missions that name a weapon): nothing else groups weapons.

Three relations put two weapon items in one family, and the families are their transitive closure:

- **name**: the same name (compared as the mission reader compares names: case, apostrophes and the `12GA` / `12 GA` spelling do not matter), whatever the finish (`variationname`).
- **chain**: the item definitions' `parent` chain (`id`, `define`, `parent` of the install's definitions) leads to the same root: the variants of one weapon under another name (a weapon and its engraved variant). The root need not be a catalogue weapon: two weapons that derive from an abstract base share one family. Without the install's definitions this relation adds nothing and the other two still apply.
- **shares**: a description names the weapon whose skill it shares: "shares skill level and rank with the X", "shares the same skill of the X (Finish)" or "All variations of the X share the same weapon skill". `X` must be the whole name of a weapon item (same comparison as **name**); `(Finish)` narrows it to the item of that finish; a name that matches no item (a plural, a quoted short name) adds nothing. There is no word-subset guess: a `Test Bow` description must not pull in the `Test Heavy Bow`. The item's `define` is not read: a variant's `define` may carry a suffix that names no base (its base can be another, numbered define), so a suffix rule would need guesses, while the sentences name the weapon.

A weapon with none of them is its own family. The **representative** is the lowest id among the members whose definition has no `parent`, or among every member when none qualifies (no definitions, or every member derives from a base that is no weapon). `Of(itemId)` gives the family (`Representative` and `Members`, the representative first then ascending) of a weapon item and null for anything else; `All` lists the families ascending by representative.

`admin weapon-families` prints the table of every weapon item of the catalogue the server loaded ([Starting data](#starting-data)), ascending by id, as a Markdown table: id, name, finish, the family (its representative's id) and why the item is in it (`name`, `chain`, `shares`, joined by `+` when several relate it to its family, or `alone`), then a count line. It writes nothing and one catalogue prints the same bytes every time; read it after a catalogue or install change to see the new weapons' families and every weapon left alone. The repository does not commit that table: it lists the real catalogue's names, which are the operator's data ([Starting data](#starting-data)); `WeaponFamiliesTests` pins the generator's output on an invented catalogue instead.

### One shooting skill per weapon type

A weapon type has one shooting skill: its finishes and variants (a [weapon family](#weapon-families)) share one level and one XP, earned by any of them and shown the same whichever one is equipped. What stays per variant is everything the game keeps by item: the achievements and the statistics counters they read (`weapon.<define>` paths of `Statistics/total`: a kill with a finish counts for that finish only), the hunt's weapon rows, the ranks' weapon items and the mission objectives that name a weapon.

- **Storage**: the `skills` table keeps one `weapon` row per account and weapon type, its `key` the family's representative item id.
