The answer is 200 `{status: "bought", ems}` (`gms` for `SILVER`) with the new balance of the wallet paid, 409 `refused` for something not for sale, a balance of that currency too low or an own limit reached (nothing changes), 401 `signed_out`, or 400 `invalid` (not from our page, no number id or count, or a wallet other than `GOLD` and `SILVER`). `Inventory/buy` refuses with `{"success": false}` for a balance too low, an own limit reached, or an item that is `notbuyable`, a service or without a price in the wallet (a gm$ price below 1 is no price; unlike the website's call it also sells a hidden item, and an em$ price of 0), and answers `{price, success: true}` with the total charged.

## Selling back

A player sells an unused item back to the store for part of its price, so a beginner's mistake is not lost for good (#928). `POST /account/sell` on `web_host`, community server only, with the session cookie and the website's `X-Requested-With` (like `POST /account/buy`); the form is `guid`, the copy's guid from `GET /account/inventory`. The website's inventory page (`/inventory`) and the launcher's offer a **Sell** button on the copies that can be sold, with the refund and a confirmation; the server decides, and refuses every other copy.

- **The refund** is 40 % of the price paid, rounded down (a refund of 0 is not offered: `too_cheap`), in the currency paid: em$ for an em$ purchase, gm$ for a gm$ purchase. A copy bought on sale refunds 40 % of the discounted price it cost, so buying on sale and selling back never profits.
- **Proof of the price.** A copy is sold for what it cost only when `purchases` ties this exact copy (its `guid`, and the same item id) to a purchase: a store purchase of one item (`POST /account/buy` or `Inventory/buy`, one or more copies) records the currency and the price of each copy. A bundle's copies have no price of their own, a grant, a reward and the start kit are no purchase: they have no proof and are refused (`no_proof`). A copy bought before schema 46 has none either, unless a restore brought it (below).
- **A restored copy** (a backup restore or an archive claim) is treated as bought with gm$: it is sold for 40 % of the item's catalogue gm$ price (`silver_price`, rounded down), in gm$; an item whose catalogue row has no `silver_price` (its gm$ price is only [derived](#the-store-catalogue)) is not sold back, as before. A restore marks each copy it brings in `purchases`. The one start at schema 46 marked the copies already held by an account that had restored a backup or claimed an archive, because neither the restore audit (a text report) nor the rows list which copies a restore brought; it skips the copies of an item the account's wallet history shows it also bought here (which copy is which cannot be told: those are not sellable). So on such an account an item the operator granted before schema 46 (`admin item`), and a purchase made before the wallet history existed, count as restored, and are sold for 40 % of the gm$ price when the store sells the item for gm$ (a grant of an item the store does not sell stays refused, `not_sold`). A restored copy of a start item (the facts file's `start`) or of an item the store does not sell for gm$ (not in the catalogue, not buyable, hidden or no gm$ price) is refused (`start_kit`, `not_sold`).
- **Refused**, with the reason in `status` of the 409 answer and nothing changed: `unknown` (no such copy of this account), `hunting` (a hunt is open and alive: the game holds the inventory), `equipped` (any slot but the lodge's, slot 0), `placed` (placed in the world, a copy a launch made, or a weapon on a rack of one of the account's lodges: a rack slot's `weapon_id` that equals the copy's guid or its item id keeps it, as the server cannot tell which the game writes), `ammunition`, `consumable` (an item with units), `license` (a license or an item with an end), `lodge` (a lodge item), `service` (the Inventory Loadout slot, item 811: its delivery made a loadout the player may have filled), `start_kit`, `not_sold`, `no_proof`, `no_wallet` (the account has no wallet row of the refund's currency, so the refund cannot be paid), `too_cheap`. No time limit.
- **The sale** is one transaction: the copy and its `purchases` row are removed (no undo), the refund is paid with the wallet row `Sold: <item name>` (the item's id in `item_ids`), and the game gets the new balance (update 15) with its next heartbeat and the inventory at its next connect. The answer is 200 `{status: "sold", currency: "em"|"gm", refund, balance}`, 409 as above, 401 `signed_out`, or 400 `invalid` (not from our page, or no number `guid`).
- **Ownership limits** ([Ownership limits](#ownership-limits)): a sold copy no longer counts, so a limited item (one of each) can be bought again.
- **The inventory read** tells the pages which copies sell: `GET /account/inventory` gives each row its `guid` and `sell`, `{currency, refund}` when the copy sells now (never during a hunt), else null.

## Sign-in and tokens

With `offline_mode` false (a community server) players sign in with Steam; the endpoints that act for a player need a token that resolves (recorded replies, archives, the mirror, the launcher's calls and the start page are public). With it true none of the following is on (the `/auth/*` endpoints, the start page, the launcher's calls and the replies that carry tokens are the replay's, as before). Steam's OpenID 2.0 needs no key. No Steam name, e-mail or address is read or kept: only the 17-digit Steam id, the handle the player picks and the game data.

**The sign-in, in steps** (always on `web_host`: a start on `signup_host` or `game_web_host` is first sent there; the origin is `https://<web_host>`):

Every page the player's browser sees is the website's ([docs/website.md](website.md#sign-in)); the server answers its sign-in routes with redirects to `/signin/<step>` (`handle`, `confirm`, `done`, `problem?reason=<key>`: a fixed path on `web_host`, never an address a request names) and with no HTML but the launcher's start page. A sign-in started on another host (`signup_host`, `game_web_host`) first redirects to the same path and query on `web_host`, so the browser always finishes on our site. A refused request (a login that ran out, a form without its token or from another origin, Steam not confirming, a banned account, an invalid app link) is a 302 to `/signin/problem?reason=<key>`, no longer a 4xx page; nothing changes in those cases.

1. The browser opens `/auth/steam/login?next=<local path>`. The server keeps a login in memory (15 minutes), sets the cookie `hr_login` (`Path=/`, so the website's pages can read it too) and sends the browser to Steam with `return_to=https://<host>/auth/steam/return?login=<id>`.
2. Steam asks for the password and sends the browser back. The server needs the `hr_login` cookie to match `login` (another browser or a replayed URL fails), then asks Steam to confirm (`check_authentication`) and accepts only an assertion that Steam confirms, whose `return_to` is exactly its own, whose `op_endpoint` is Steam's, whose `claimed_id` is `https://steamcommunity.com/openid/id/<17 digits>` and equals `identity`, whose signature covers `op_endpoint`, `claimed_id`, `identity`, `return_to`, `response_nonce` and `assoc_handle`, with no repeated `openid.*` field, and whose `response_nonce` is at most 5 minutes old (or ahead) and not seen before (kept in memory).
3. A known Steam id gets a session: the cookie `hr_session` (HttpOnly, Secure, SameSite=Lax, 30 days; its SHA-256 is in `web_sessions`, a table of its own, so a cookie is never a game token and a game token never a cookie) and a redirect to `next` (a local path; anything else becomes `/`).
4. A new Steam id is sent to `/signin/handle` (`GET /auth/handle` redirects there too), where the player picks a handle; the page reads `GET /auth/pending` (see below) and posts the form to `POST /auth/handle`; a refused form keeps what was typed (the name cut to 30 characters, the old name to 50) and a reason key (`invalid`, `taken`, `unknown`, `old-taken`) in the login and redirects back to the page, which shows both. (Steam gives none we keep). When the server has an archive ([Archive claims](#archive-claims)) the page also offers "I played before": the player types the old hunter name and may leave the new name empty to take the old one when it is free (else "Choose a name: the old one is taken."). The old name is looked up before any account exists, and it costs no daily lookup (each try is one POST of one sign-in); an unknown name shows the page again, "We did not keep a profile under this name.", and creates nothing. Otherwise the account is created as below, then the claim runs at once with the account's confirmation (`Claims.Claim`, the same rules: restored, or kept for the operator from hunterscore 10,000) and the player's inbox tells which. Without an archive the page only asks for the name. The account is created with the next user id of the server's range (below) and the server's starting data, then step 3. Two tabs of one sign-in make one account.

**`GET /auth/pending`** (the website's step pages read it; needs `X-Requested-With` and the browser's `hr_login` cookie) answers 200 `{step: handle|confirm|app|banned, csrf, ...}` (`handle`: `offer`, `handle`, `old`, `restore`, `error`; `confirm`: `handle`, `code`; `app`: `handle`; `banned`: `csrf` empty and `reason`, the operator's ban reason or `""`, see [bans](#archive-claims)) or 404 `{status: expired}`; `csrf` is the token the step's form posts back. It changes nothing; a cross-site page cannot read it (no CORS, a `SameSite=Lax` cookie, a JSON type with `nosniff`).

**The launcher's start page** is `GET /` on `signup_host` (the launcher loads it with `#/launcher/start`; the hash never reaches the server). The old launcher's browser (Chromium 25, TLS 1.1) cannot be expected to load Steam's pages, so the page signs in through the player's own browser, in the style of a device code: the page asks `POST /auth/link` for a code and opens `https://<web_host>/auth/steam/login?link=<code>` with `ewlauncher.openURL` (the page is restyled with the website's colours and type in CSS the old browser reads: no grid, variables or flex gap, ES5; the in-window sign-in link is gone, since Steam's pages do not load there). `POST /auth/link` answers `{code, userCode, url, expires}`: the code lives 600 s, and `url` always points the browser to `https://<web_host>/auth/steam/login?link=<code>`. The player's browser runs steps 1 to 4, then `/signin/confirm` shows the code `ABCD-EFGH` and "Sign in the app that shows this code?" (the first 8 characters of the code); the player confirms, and the launcher, polling `GET /auth/link/<code>` every 2 s, gets `ok` with the `hr_session` cookie once and reloads. The confirmation matters: without it, anyone could send a victim a link made from their own code. Signed in, the page shows "Play": `POST /auth/launch` returns `{"url": <launch URL>}`, and the page calls `ewlauncher.startGame(url)`. The page is plain HTML and ES5, English only.

### The loopback sign-in

The launcher and the backup tool sign in as native apps do (RFC 8252 loopback redirect, RFC 7636 PKCE), on `web_host`, `signup_host` or `game_web_host`; the answer can only reach the PC that started the sign-in, so the player compares nothing and a sign-in link sent to someone else gives the sender nothing. The code link above stays for the start page.

1. The app makes a random `verifier` (43 to 128 URL-safe characters), its `challenge` (SHA-256 of the verifier, base64url without padding: always 43 characters), a random `state` (16 to 128 URL-safe characters), and listens once on `127.0.0.1` on a free port.
2. `GET /auth/app/start?challenge=<challenge>&port=<port>&state=<state>` in the player's browser. The server refuses (a 302 to `/signin/problem?reason=app`, no login started) a challenge that is not 43 base64url characters of 32 bytes, a `port` that is not a whole number from 1024 to 65535 and a `state` of the wrong length or characters. Else it starts a login as at `/auth/steam/login` (Steam, the name at the first sign-in), bound to the challenge, the port and the state.
3. The player confirms on `/signin/confirm` ("Sign in the app on this PC?"), which posts `/auth/confirm` (the same CSRF token and `Origin` rules as every form). The server then makes a one-time code (32 random bytes, hex), keeps it with the account and the challenge for 3 minutes (`Lifetimes.AppCode`; at most 10 000 live, the oldest goes first), and redirects the browser to `http://127.0.0.1:<port>/?code=<code>&state=<state>`. The host is a literal and the port a validated number: the redirect never goes to another host. A second confirmation of the same login makes no second code.
4. The app answers the browser a short page "Signed in, you can close this tab" only for a request whose `state` is its own (any other request gets a refusal and the app keeps waiting), then `POST /auth/app/exchange` with the form `code` and `verifier` and the header `X-Requested-With` (or a same-origin `Origin`). A request that is not from our own page gets 400 `{"error": "not from a page of this server"}`. A verifier that is not 43 to 128 URL-safe characters is refused next, and the code stays. Else the server removes the code, so every try with a good verifier spends it, then checks the lifetime and that SHA-256(`verifier`) equals the stored challenge in constant time. A good exchange answers 200 `{"status":"ok"}` with the same `hr_session` cookie as the code link (the app keeps it, the launcher in `session.dat`); anything else answers 400 `{"status":"invalid"}`, whatever the reason.

The server logs the path only, never the query or the form.

**The launch URL** is `<scheme>://<game_web_host>/<session>/lang/<code>/access_token/<access JWT>/refresh_token/<refresh JWT>` ([The current build](#the-current-build)), three fresh tokens of the signed-in account and the game's language (`en_US` unless the launcher asked for another, below); the scheme (`ProtocolLiterals.LaunchScheme`, the one literal that names the game) carries the name the client's environment check looks for anywhere in the URL. `game_web_host` must not contain `dev` or `beta` (the same check).

**`POST /auth/launch`** needs the session cookie and a page of this server (a same-origin `Origin`, or `X-Requested-With`), and answers `{"url": <launch URL>}`. Without a form it is only the URL, as the start page sends it. With a form it first opens the hunt, as `POST /v1/session/start_single_player` does, through the same step (`State.NoteLaunch`: it closes any open hunt, opens a new one, sets the mission state and puts the loadout on): `reserve` and one of `lodge` (a start of [The launch map](#the-launch-map)) or `tent` (the guid of one of the player's placed tents in that reserve, [A hunt at a tent](#a-hunt-at-a-tent)) are required, `time` (minutes after midnight, 0 to 1439; the hunt's connect carries it as `expedition.start_time`; schema 16, `expeditions.start_time`; as `start_single_player` and `Launch/start` do with their `time`; absent or out of range: the recorded time) and `loadout` (a loadout id of [Loadouts](#loadouts), 0 or absent for the equipment last saved) are optional, each a whole number; anything else is 400 `{"error": ...}` and opens nothing. The launcher sends it when the player picks a reserve and a start. A form with `tutorial` (a tutorial id of the map, 0 is the first) opens that tutorial's session as `start_tutorial` does, with no hunt, and answers a launch URL whose session is that tutorial's; an unknown id is 400. 401 when signed out.

**The game's language and units.** A form of `POST /auth/launch` (a hunt, a multiplayer start or a tutorial, or one with `lang` alone) may carry `lang`, the language the game starts in: the launch URL's `lang` segment, read once by the game at its start. It is one of the 22 languages the game ships (`GameTokens.GameLanguages`: `en_US`, `de_DE`, `es_ES`, `et_EE`, `fr_FR`, `hr_HR`, `hu_HU`, `nl_NL`, `pl_PL`, `pt_BR`, `pt_PT`, `ru_RU`, `sv_SE`, `da_DK`, `cs_CZ`, `nb_NO`, `it_IT`, `fi_FI`, `tr_TR`, `sl_SI`, `ka_KA`, `ro_RO`); missing or unknown, it is `en_US` (the old start page sends none). The language is the launcher's choice (`launcher.json`); the server keeps none. The units are the account's: `POST /account/settings` with the form `use_imperial=1` or `use_imperial=0` stores `accounts.use_imperial` (schema 37, 0 by default, added at every start when the column is missing, whatever the version), 400 for any other value, 401 when signed out. The game's connect carries it as `account.use_imperial` (1 or 0; the game converts distances × 1.09361 and weights × 2.20462 when it is non-zero), `GET /auth/me` as `imperial`, so the pages show the same units.

**Multiplayer through `POST /auth/launch`**: the form's `mp` is `host` or `join` (any other value is a 400). `mp=host` takes the fields of a hunt (`reserve`, `lodge` or `tent`, `time`, `loadout`) and the session's own (`name`, `description`, `language`, `public_game`, `free_players`, `hs_min`, `hs_max`, `password`, `max_players`); `mp=join` takes `network_id`, `lodge` or `tent`, `loadout`, `password` and `host_tent`, and no `reserve` (the session's is used) and, unless it sends a `time`, the session's time. With `host_tent=1` the `tent` is a tent of the session's host, not the joiner's own ([The host's setup](#multiplayer)). Both answer `{"url"}` as above, the URL's session being the one the game connects with. A refused join is a 400 `{"error": "password" | "full" | "not_allowed" | "own_game" | "host_not_ready" | "host_network" | "bad_start" | "gone"}` ([Multiplayer](#multiplayer)) and opens nothing.

**Tokens** (all in `tokens`, hashed; every token resolves everywhere: the lifetimes limit a leak, they are not different powers):

| Token | Handed out by | Lifetime |
|---|---|---|
| session (`X-Hun-Session`) | `Launch/start`, `Launch/start_mp`, `Launch/join`, the start page | 7 days |
| access (`oauth_access_token`) | `Launch/start`, `Launch/start_mp`, `Launch/join`, the start page, `Oauth/token` | 1 day |
| refresh (starts with `r`) | `Launch/start`, `Launch/start_mp`, `Launch/join` | 30 days; the only ones `Oauth/token` renews; `Oauth/token` (`oauth_refresh_token`) returns a new access token and the same refresh token |
| JWT (`Bearer`) | `User/jwt`, on the game host and the API host, for the `oauth_access_token` of its JSON or form body | 30 minutes; three dot-separated parts whose payload has `sub`, `iat` and `exp` (the client reads `exp`); the third part is random, not a signature |
| access JWT (`Bearer`, `oauth_access_token`) | `/auth/jwt/refresh`, the launch URL ([The current build](#the-current-build)) | 900 s; HS256-signed, kept in `tokens` |
| refresh JWT | the same, the cookie | 30 days (7 days when the photo uploader asks with its placeholder and the session); renews only through `/auth/jwt/refresh`; not rotated (the game may keep presenting the one of its launch URL), so it ends with its 30 days, a ban or a delete |
| web session cookie | the sign-in | 30 days |

Expired rows are deleted at startup. `Launch/start`, `User/jwt` and `Oauth/token` answer a request without a valid token as every other state endpoint does (the expired-token reply).

**The launcher's own calls** (`EmoteLauncher/token`, `initialise`, `compatibleHardware`, `ping`, under the game host) are answered without recordings: `err` 0, placeholder `access_token` and `refresh_token` (the launcher only needs them to be something other than `"0"`), no files to download, `compatible` 1. The Steam session ticket that `token` receives is not checked: checking it takes a Steam Web API key (Valve's documentation asks for the game's publisher key, and one page also allows an ordinary key on the public host, rate limited), and the server runs without any key, so any client could send any ticket. Signing in is the browser's Steam sign-in above.

**User ids**: an account of the server's own gets the next id from 2,000,000,000 (`Store.FirstServerId`) to 2,147,483,647 (int32, the game's width); ids that come from a backup are below that, so they never collide. The allocation and the insert are one step under a lock (one server process, [ADR 0001](adr/0001-sqlite-for-profiles.md)). A restore that brings a user id and handle replaces the id and handle of an account that has not played (no row in `expeditions` and `events`, only its starting rows); an account that has played keeps its id and takes only the handle ([ADR 0009](adr/0009-restore-a-zip-onto-an-account-that-has-played.md)). The rest of the account (Steam id, tokens, sessions) stays.

**`web_host` must be the website's public domain** (the website sends the host the browser used, and the server picks its sign-in by it); the game's own web host is `game_web_host`.

**Handles**: 3 to 30 characters, ASCII letters, digits, `-` and `_`, starting with a letter or a digit; unique whatever the case (`Handles.Problem`, the column is NOCASE).

**Endpoints** (the OpenAPI document `game` lists them): `GET /auth/steam/login`, `GET /auth/steam/return`, `GET /auth/pending`, `GET|POST /auth/handle`, `GET|POST /auth/confirm` (GET redirects to the website's page), `POST /auth/link`, `GET /auth/link/<code>`, `GET /auth/app/start`, `POST /auth/app/exchange` ([the loopback sign-in](#the-loopback-sign-in)), `GET /auth/me` (`{userId, handle, hunterscore, rank, avatar, ems, gms, imperial, steamLicence, discordLinked, discordVeteranAt}`, the wallet in whole numbers and the avatar as in [Avatars](#avatars), `imperial` whether the player chose imperial units, `steamLicence` as in [Steam licence](#steam-licence), the last two as in [Discord Linked Roles](#discord-linked-roles), or 401), `POST /auth/signout`, `POST /auth/launch`, on `web_host`, `signup_host` and `game_web_host`; `GET /` on `signup_host` and `GET /launcher` on `game_web_host` (the start page); `POST /auth/jwt/refresh` and `POST /auth/emotelauncher/token` on `game_web_host` only. A state-changing request must come from a page of the same origin: its `Origin` is the origin, or it has no `Origin` and carries `X-Requested-With`. The website reaches them through the proxy; the website's own use is in [website.md](website.md#sign-in).

A restart forgets the logins in progress (the player starts the sign-in again); sessions, accounts and tokens are in the database.

## Steam licence

The game is no longer sold on Steam, so only an account that already holds its licence can still download it. The launcher's "Install with Steam" button ([launcher.md](launcher.md#install-with-steam)) shows next to it whether the player's Steam account has that licence. `GET /auth/me` carries it as `steamLicence`: `owned`, `not_owned` or `unknown` (always present: `unknown` when the check is off or an account has no Steam id).

With `steam_web_api_key` and `steam_app_id` set ([Run it](#run-it)) the server asks Steam's Web API once for the signed-in account's Steam id (`SteamLicence`): `GET https://api.steampowered.com/IPlayerService/GetOwnedGames/v1/?key=…&steamid=…&include_played_free_games=1&appids_filter[0]=<app id>&format=json`. A reply listing the game is `owned`; a reply with `game_count` 0 (a public library without the game) is `not_owned`; an empty `response` (the player's game details are private), an HTTP error, a timeout (5 seconds), a reply that is not the expected JSON and an account without a Steam id are `unknown`. The key is only ever in the request's address: it is never logged and never in an error text.

**The wait is capped at 2 seconds** (`SteamLicence.DefaultWait`): the website and the launcher give up on `/auth/me` after 5 seconds and take the player as signed out, so a slow Steam makes that one reply `unknown`; the call goes on and its answer is in the cache for the next read. Every failure of the call, whatever the exception, is `unknown` and never leaves a failed call in the cache. A non-200 answer of Steam is logged once with its status code only (a wrong key shows there; the key never does).

**At most one Steam call per account per day.** The answer, `unknown` included, is kept in memory for 24 hours with its time (`SteamLicence`): later `/auth/me` calls of the account (the website and the launcher read it all the time) read it, and requests that arrive together make one call. A restart forgets it, so an account is asked again once after a deploy. Nothing but this yes/no is kept, and nothing of it is written to the database.

Tests: `SteamLicenceTests` (Steam's reply faked: the three results and the odd replies, the day's cache for every kind of answer, one call for simultaneous requests, no key and no call), `SignInFlowTests` (`/auth/me` says the licence of the signed-in account's Steam id, and `unknown` without the key), `SettingsTests`.

## Referrals

A player recruits friends with a **referral code**. The new player gets a welcome reward at once; the recruiter is rewarded step by step as his recruits really play. Online servers only; the code, the entries and the payouts are in the tables `referral_codes`, `referrals` and `referral_payouts` (schema 52).

- **The code.** Every account has one: 8 characters of `A-Z` and `2-9` without the look-alikes (no `I`, `O`, `0`, `1`), unique and stable, made the first time `GET /account/referral` asks for it. It is read without case, spaces and dashes. The website's `/join/<code>` link carries it.
- **Entering a code**, once per account and never changed (`POST /account/referral/code`): at sign-up or within `enter_hours` (72) after the account was made; an account made **before the feature first started** (the time is written once, `referral_release`) has `existing_days` (31) from that time instead, or the 72 hours if they end later. Refused: `unknown` (no such code), `own`, `loop` (the code's owner is a recruit of his: no A to B and B to A), `already` (he entered one; an entry the operator dropped counts, and so does one made by an account of the same Steam id that was deleted since: `referral_steam` outlives the account, so signing up again gives no second entry), `expired` (the window closed), `banned` (the code's owner is banned). On success the entry records his hunterscore and his playtime here at that moment, and the `recruit_reward`, when set, is paid to him at once.
- **Qualification.** A recruit qualifies when he has played at least `qualify_hours` (2) on this server (the hunts since the entry, as [server playtime](#server-playtime-and-hunting-statistics) counts them) **and** gained at least `qualify_score` (10) hunterscore since the entry. What a restore or an archive claim brings does not count: the score the entry counts from moves by what the backup added, in the restore's own transaction (the zip, the operator's approve that moves the account to the backup's user id, a claim). It is checked whenever the recruit's hunterscore is updated on the serving server (the close of a hunt, a heartbeat with hunt events, a restore), when he links Discord and whenever the recruiter reads `GET /account/referral`; the start pass and the admin commands (`admin referral list` among them) settle nothing and pay nothing, only the serving server does, after the settings are loaded. A recruit whose account or Steam id is banned never counts: his unpaid entry is dropped (marked `revoked`; entries already counted stay, there is no clawback). A banned recruiter is paid nothing, by a qualification or by `admin referral pay-qualified`.
- **Weekly cap.** A qualified recruit is **counted** (`paid_ts`) for the recruiter while fewer than `weekly_cap` (5) of his recruits were counted in the last 7 days; over the cap he stays qualified and uncounted and is counted at the next check below the cap.
- **Tiers.** When the counted recruits reach a tier's `recruits` (default tiers: 1, 3, 5, 10, 20), that tier's `reward` is paid to the recruiter, once. The **Discord milestone** is the same for the recruits who are counted (qualified and inside the weekly cap) **and** linked Discord ([Discord Linked Roles](#discord-linked-roles)): `discord_milestones` (default one, at 1 recruit).
- **Rewards are settings, empty by default.** A reward is `{"gm": 0, "em": 0, "items": [{"id": 384, "count": 1}]}`: gm$, em$ and items (ids of the catalogue, checked when the settings are read; a reward of nothing is none). Absent or `null`: the milestone is reached, the pages say the reward is to be announced, and nothing is paid. A reward set later pays **only the milestones reached after it was set**: the ones reached before stay unpaid (the milestone is on record with `paid` 0), and so does a milestone listed later that the recruiter had already passed, until the operator runs `admin referral pay-qualified --apply`. Everything is paid as a wallet row **"Referral reward"** (the items listed on the row, or on a row of 0 gm$ for items only) and in the inventory; a running game gets the balance and the items with its next heartbeat. An item the account may not own another copy of ([Ownership limits](#ownership-limits)) is left out and logged.
- **Settings** (`referral` of `settings.json`, listed in the table at the top, read again within 5 seconds of every change of the file; a wrong block is refused whole and logged, what is in force stays): `qualify_hours` (a number, 2), `qualify_score` (a whole number, 10), `weekly_cap` (a whole number from 1, 5), `enter_hours` (72), `existing_days` (31), `recruit_reward` (the welcome reward), `tiers` and `discord_milestones` (lists of `{"recruits": 1 or more, "reward": ...}`, no number twice). An unknown key is refused (a typo would pay nothing).

**Endpoints** (session cookie; 401 `signed_out` without; the website's host):

- `GET /account/referral` answers `{code, link, canEnter, enterUntil, enteredCode, recruiter, qualification: {hours, score}, recruits: [{handle, hours, score, qualified, qualifiedAt, discordLinked, paid}], qualifiedCount, countedCount, tiers: [{recruits, reward, reached}], discord: {linkedCount, milestones: [{recruits, reward, reached}]}, recruitReward}`. `enterUntil` is unix seconds, null when `canEnter` is false; `enteredCode` and `recruiter` (his handle) are null until he entered one; `hours` (a number with at most 2 decimals) and `score` (a whole number) are what the recruit made since his entry. `qualifiedCount` is the qualified recruits and `countedCount` those counted under the weekly cap: the tiers and their progress bars follow `countedCount`. `recruits` are sorted: not qualified first, the closest to qualifying first (`min(hours / qualify_hours, 1) + min(score / qualify_score, 1)`, largest first), then the qualified, the latest first. `tiers[].reached` is by the counted recruits. A reward is null or `{gm, em, items: [{id, name, count}]}`.
- `POST /account/referral/code` with the JSON body `{"code": "ABCD2345"}` answers 200 `{status: "ok", recruiter, reward}` (`reward`: what was paid to him, null when none is set) or 409 `{status}` with the reason above.

**Admin.** `dotnet run --project server/Server -- admin referral list [--account <id or handle>]` lists the recruiters, their recruits (hours and score of the entry, state `waiting`, `qualified (over the weekly cap)`, `paid` or `dropped`) and the tiers reached (with whether a reward was paid); `admin referral revoke <recruit>` drops the unpaid entry of a recruit (it stays on record, so he cannot enter another code; a counted recruit is not dropped); `admin referral pay-qualified [--apply]` pays the tier and Discord rewards that are set now to the recruiters who reached them before (once each; without `--apply` it only says what it would pay).

## Discord Linked Roles

A player links a Discord account to their game account once and gets 5000 gm$ and 250 em$ the first time ever. The server gives the linked players the Discord server's roles itself, with a second, minimal bot (**the roles**, below): "Verified hunter" (linked) and "Veteran" (HunterScore of `discord_veteran_at`, 20000, or more). The role connection stays: Discord reads the player's hunterscore as role data and shows the profile box. Online only, and only with `discord_client_id` and `discord_client_secret` set ([Run it](#run-it)); without them `/discord/*` is not served. The Discord application is the project's own: its id and client secret are the operator's settings and never in the repository, and the server never uses that application's bot token (the application's role-connection metadata is registered once, outside the server: one record, `key: "hunterscore"`, `type: 2`, integer greater than or equal). The server pushes only `metadata: {hunterscore: <int>}`. The operator's steps in the Developer Portal and on the Discord server are in [deploy.md](deploy.md#discord-linked-roles). `GET /auth/me` carries `discordLinked` (true while the account has a link; null when Linked Roles are off, so a server without the settings offers no link) and `discordVeteranAt` (`discord_veteran_at`; null when Linked Roles are off): the website and the launcher offer the link (a card, a pill, a menu item) only until then.

**The flow** is Discord's Linked Roles flow, all on `web_host` (the proxy sends `/discord/*` to the server):

1. The player opens `GET /discord/link` from the website's or the launcher's entry points. It is also the application's **Linked Roles Verification URL**, which Discord's Linked Roles page offers because the Discord server's role "Linked" requires the connection: the one Linked Role, which the player claims once so the profile box shows ([deploy.md](deploy.md#discord-linked-roles)); "Verified hunter" and "Veteran" are normal roles (**the roles**, below). Without the `hr_session` cookie the browser goes through `/auth/steam/login?next=/discord/link` and comes back here; with it, to `https://discord.com/oauth2/authorize` with `response_type=code`, `scope=identify role_connections.write`, `prompt=consent`, `redirect_uri=https://<web_host>/discord/callback` and a `state`.
2. The `state` is `<end>.<HMAC>`: the end time (Unix seconds, 10 minutes ahead) and an HMAC-SHA256 of the session cookie's value and that time, keyed from the server's secret key file. It is bound to the session, so the state of another browser, an ended, a changed or a missing one is refused before Discord is asked anything; the server keeps nothing in memory for it.
3. `GET /discord/callback?code=&state=` needs the session cookie (without it: the sign-in of step 1). It checks the state, trades the code at `POST /api/v10/oauth2/token` (the client id and secret; the reply must grant both scopes), reads `GET /users/@me` for the Discord id (a snowflake: digits only), links the pair and pays the reward in one transaction, then pushes the role data with the player's own access token: `PUT /users/@me/applications/<application id>/role-connection` with `{platform_name: "Hunter Resurgence", platform_username: "<handle>", metadata: {hunterscore}}`. It always ends in a redirect to the website's `/signin/discord?result=<key>` ([website.md](website.md#discord-linked-roles)): `linked` (the reward was paid now), `rewarded` (linked; paid before), `taken` (this Discord account is linked to another hunter), `other` (this hunter is linked to another Discord account) or `error` (the state, the code or Discord's answer was no good, or Discord could not be reached; logged). A push that fails at that moment is logged and the sweep (below) tries again; the link and the reward stand.

**The rules.** One Discord account per hunter and one hunter per Discord account: `discord_links` has `account_id` as its key and `discord_id` unique. A Discord account linked to another hunter, or a hunter linked to another Discord account, is refused and nothing is written. The same pair again is fine: new tokens, a new push, no second reward. There is no unlink: Delete my data removes the link and revokes Discord's access (below).

**The reward** is 5000 gm$ and 250 em$ (`LinkedRoles.RewardGm` and `RewardEm`), paid once ever, per hunter and per Discord id. It goes through `State.AddCurrency` like every other payment: a `wallet_log` row per currency with the reason "Reward for linking Discord", `base` = the amount and no bonus event, the wallet updates a running game reads, and a message from the notice sender in the inbox ([Messages](#messages)). The payment is one row of the ledger `discord_rewards` (`hash` its key, `account_id` unique), inserted in the same transaction as the link: when the hash or the account is already there nothing is paid. So two requests at once, an unlink and a link again, a second hunter with that Discord account, the same hunter with another Discord account and an account made again after a delete all find it paid.

**The tokens at rest.** `discord_links` keeps the user's access token and refresh token sealed with AES-256-GCM, each key derived (HMAC-SHA256 of its purpose) from the server's secret key file `<db>.jwtkey`, which `backup` does not copy: a copy of the database holds nothing readable. A sign-in cookie or a game token is stored as a hash because it is only compared; a refresh token has to be read back, so it is sealed instead. The Discord id is the associated data, so a sealed token is good for its own row only. When the access token has ended (with 60 seconds to spare) the next push first refreshes it with `grant_type=refresh_token` and stores both new tokens (Discord rotates the refresh token, so one push runs at a time on the server). A 400 `invalid_grant` (the player removed the application from Discord) clears both tokens, is logged, and keeps the row, so the rules and the reward stand until the player links again; any other failure (the network, a 5xx, a wrong client secret) is logged and tried again. A 401 of the push itself makes the next one refresh. A refresh or a clear writes only while the row still holds the refresh token it read, so a player who links again meanwhile keeps the new tokens.

**The hunterscore stays current** by a sweep: every 10 minutes (`LinkedRoles.SweepEvery`) the server pushes every linked account that is not banned and has a refresh token whose hunterscore is not the one of its last successful push (`pushed_score`; a new link has none), or whose handle is not the one it last pushed (`pushed_handle`). So the profile box on Discord shows the player's current handle and hunterscore: every push sends `platform_username` as the handle only (`LinkedRoles.Username`, `Mikado`; Discord already shows the metadata's "12345 HunterScore" under it) and the hunterscore in `metadata`, the first link, a link again and the sweep alike. Discord allows 100 characters there: a longer handle is cut (a handle has at most 30 characters, so this only guards the limit). A player who passes `discord_veteran_at` later gets the "Veteran" role without linking again. A sweep was chosen over a push from the hunterscore code, which runs at every heartbeat with hunt events and would call Discord every few seconds for a player on a hunt. A failing push is logged and tried again at the next sweep, and never touches a hunt or another player.

**Delete my data** first takes the account's roles away on the Discord server (**the roles**, below), then takes the account off Discord (`LinkedRoles.UnlinkAsync`), holding the lock the pushes take, so no push runs meanwhile and a sweep that listed the account before finds no link after: `DELETE /users/@me/applications/<application id>/role-connection` with the player's access token (Discord's documented removal of the role connection: the profile box and the role data), refreshed first with the refresh token when it has ended (the new tokens are not stored: the row goes right after; a refused refresh skips the DELETE), then `POST /api/v10/oauth2/token/revoke` with `token` (the newest refresh token, else the access token), `token_type_hint` and the client id and secret, which revokes Discord's access: every token of the authorization, whichever one is sent. It is best effort: a refusal, an unreachable Discord or no token left is logged as `discord unlink of account <id>: <why>` (`discord roles of account <id>: <why>` for the roles) and never stops or fails the delete. Then it removes `discord_links` (the Discord id and both tokens) with the account. `discord_rewards` keeps one row per Discord id ever paid: `hash` is an HMAC-SHA256 of the id keyed from the same key file, not a plain hash (a Discord id is a guessable number, so a plain hash could be turned back into it, or matched against a list); `account_id` is cleared when the account is deleted (`ON DELETE SET NULL`); no handle, no time. It is a third record that outlives the account ([What a delete keeps](#upload-a-backup-zip)), and the privacy page and the delete dialog say so. It is the minimum that stops an account made again from collecting the reward again, so it is never purged. A restore that gives the account another user id moves both rows with it (`Restorer.Adopt`). Losing the key file makes the ledger match nothing.

**The roles.** Discord's Linked Roles are managed roles that only the member can claim, and a bot cannot give them; so "Verified hunter" and "Veteran" are normal roles of the Discord server, and the server gives them itself with the **role bot** (`LinkedRoles.RoleBot`): a second Discord application whose bot has only the Manage Roles permission and no intents. Its token (`discord_role_bot_token`) lives only in the server's settings and goes only in `Authorization: Bot <token>`, never in a log line; the project's application keeps its own bot token outside the server. Without all four settings ([Run it](#run-it)) the server never calls the Discord server. It uses three of Discord's documented calls on the guild `discord_guild_id`: `GET /guilds/<guild>/members/<Discord id>` (the member's roles), `PUT` and `DELETE /guilds/<guild>/members/<Discord id>/roles/<role>`. It reads the member first and changes only what differs from what the link says: "Verified hunter" while linked, "Veteran" while linked with a HunterScore of `discord_veteran_at` or more; it never touches any other role. It runs right after a link (after the push), at every sweep for every linked account that is not banned (a full pass: one GET per linked account, then a `PUT` or `DELETE` only where a role differs; so a "Verified hunter" removed by hand comes back, "Veteran" follows the HunterScore both ways, and a player who joins the Discord server after linking gets the roles at the next sweep), and on Delete my data (both roles taken away, before the unlink, under the same lock; best effort). A member not in the Discord server (404) is skipped quietly and looked at again at the next sweep. A 429 (Discord's rate limit) is sent again after its `retry_after` seconds, at most 3 times in all and never after more than a minute. The server logs `discord roles of account <id>: <why>`: a role given or taken away, or a refusal; a refusal (a 403 when the bot's own role sits below the role, for example) is logged once per sweep, not once per account. Every call runs under the lock of the pushes, so it never runs during a Delete my data.

## Import a backup zip

```
dotnet run --project server/Server -c Release -- [--settings <file>] import backup <zip> [--account <id>]
```

Restores a [backup zip](backup.md) into `state.db` in `data_dir` and prints the report: what the reader found (restored, unsupported, missing, warnings), the rows stored per table, and what was dropped and why. It runs on its own, with no server running, and needs the recordings like `admin` does. Any zip is accepted, and it replaces the account's profile (items, wallet, skills, profile, statistics, achievements, trophies, lodges, loadouts, missions, challenges, saved replies); hunt history stays, no merge (only the friend edges of its lists are added to the ones the account has, [Friends](#friends)), and the server hunts still add to the restored statistics ([The statistics](#the-statistics)). When the account exists, the old database is copied first to `state.db.bak-<UTC time>`; the zip is kept as `restores/<account id>/<SHA-256>.zip`; importing the same zip twice gives the same rows (and a second audit row). The account is the zip's user id (created from the zip's handle when it does not exist), `--account <id>` names an existing one, and a local server (`offline_mode` true) restores into the local account, the one the game connects as. Exit code 1 for a refused zip, an unreadable file, a missing `--account` target or a handle another account has. The model, the dropped fields and what the server then answers from the rows are in [docs/restore.md](restore.md#storing-the-model).

```
dotnet run --project server/Server -c Release -- [--settings <file>] import pictures <zip> --account <id>
```

Restores only the pictures of a backup zip onto an existing account (refused when it does not exist), for an account restored before the server stored pictures that has played since: its items, wallet, skills and the rest stay as they are. It only adds: no picture of the account is removed, and a picture it already has (the same file hash) is not added twice, so a second run adds nothing. The gallery links the zip lacks a file for are read from the pictures an upload kept with the zip, or fetched now from the `picture_link_hosts` ([Pictures from links](restore.md#pictures)). It sets its avatar from the zip's profile ([Avatars](#avatars)) and prints the `pictures stored:` line (with `pictures from links: <ok> of <n>`) and the avatar link. The server may be running; the zip is kept under `restores/<account>/` like a restore's.

```
dotnet run --project server/Server -c Release -- [--settings <file>] admin gallery-details [--account <id or handle>]
```

Sets the details of the restored gallery pictures of every account that has kept zips (`restores/<account id>/*.zip`), or of one: the species, the score and the kill id a saved gallery entry holds (`photos.saved`, [Gallery](#gallery)), for an account restored before the server read the entry's `animal` object. It reads the kept zips (newest first) and the pictures already on disk, finds each picture by its file hash and rewrites its row's details; no picture is fetched, added or removed, and nothing else of the account changes. It prints one line per account, `<id> <handle> <n> of <m> gallery pictures updated`, and can be run again. It also re-applies the profile background and ribbon: an account with no saved v2 profile (`game_user_<uid>_profile.json` in `extras`) gets the newest kept zip's, and the line ends `, profile background restored`; items, wallet and the rest are not touched.

**Wallet floor.** Every restore (this command, an approved upload, [an archive claim](#archive-claims)) leaves the account with at least 15,000 gm$, the starting wallet's amount: a lower restored amount, or the missing wallet of a public zip, is raised to it, and the report says so. A higher one is kept. The same holds for the starting kit: each `start.items` id the restored items lack is added like a new account gets it (`start items added: <n>` in the report). The em$ are the zip's (0 for a zip without a wallet), except that a zip never takes the start em$ the account has not spent: an account that no zip restored before keeps up to 1,000 em$ (`Restorer.StartEm`) of its own balance when the zip holds less (`wallet: em raised from <old> to <n>`). Spent em$ are not given back, and a second zip replaces the first one's em$.

## Upload a backup zip

On a community server (`offline_mode` false) a signed-in player uploads their own backup from the website ([docs/website.md](website.md#restore-and-your-data)) or from the backup tool ([docs/backup.md](backup.md)). The website's server calls these endpoints over `SERVER_URL`, with the player's session cookie (`hr_session`), on `web_host` under `/account/`; the backup tool and the launcher call them on the public domain, where the reverse proxy routes `/account/*` to the server (with `/auth/`, `/hunters`, `/catalog/`, `/photos/` and the minigames' `/leaderboards/`, [docs/deploy.md](deploy.md#the-community-server)). Every route under `/account/` answers 401 without the session cookie, and every write also needs `X-Requested-With` or a same-origin `Origin`, else 400. An upload is never restored by itself: an operator approves it (below). With `offline_mode` true they do not exist (the replay answers), and `import backup` above is the local way.

| Endpoint | |
|---|---|
| `GET /account/restore` | `{restoredUserId, review: [{ts, note, format}], room, decisions: [{ts, kind, userId, handle, handleKept, reason}]}`: the backup user id this account restored (once), or null; the zips kept for the operator, each with the time it was kept, its note (`waiting for the operator`, or the reason of the operator's latest refused approve) and its `format`: `ours` when our backup tool's `manifest.json` is in it (the first reader, `Importer.FormatOf`), else `other` (another tool's reader); `room`, whether the account can store one more zip (under its file and byte limits, refused zips counting too; false: a new upload is `quota`); and what the operator decided about the others, oldest first. `kind` is `restored` (`userId`, the account's `handle` now, `handleKept`: the account kept its own name because the backup's was not allowed or belongs to another player) or `rejected` (`userId`, `reason`: the operator's words, possibly empty). 401 when signed out |
| `POST /account/restore/start` | Starts an upload in parts: `{status: started, id, partBytes}`. Needs the cookie and `X-Requested-With` or a same-origin `Origin` (a form of another site cannot set either) |
| `POST /account/restore/<id>/<part>` | Part `<part>` (0, 1, ...) of the zip as the request body, at most 90 MiB, same checks: the server appends it to the upload's file. `{status: received}`. Parts come in order (another is `409` `invalid`, the upload stays open); the last part may be sent again and replaces itself, so a part that failed on the way is sent again |
| `POST /account/restore/<id>/finish` | Same checks: reads the joined zip and answers `{status, message}` (below); the upload ends whatever the answer |
| `POST /account/restore` | A zip of one part in one request: the request body, at most 90 MiB. Answers like a finish |
| `POST /account/delete` | Form `confirm=delete`, same checks: deletes the account and all its data (below), clears the cookie |
| `POST /account/report` | The launcher's problem report: the zip as the body, header `X-Report-Trigger`, same checks as buy (cookie, `X-Requested-With`). `{reference}` ([Problem reports](#problem-reports)) |
| `POST /account/buy` | Form `id`, `count`, `wallet`, `bundle=1`, same checks: buys with em$ or gm$ ([Buying from the website](#buying-from-the-website)) |
| `POST /account/sell` | Form `guid`, same checks: sells an unused item back for 40 % of its price in the currency paid ([Selling back](#selling-back)); 409 with the reason in `status` |
| `GET /account/loadouts` | The player's saved loadouts as `GET /game/user/loadout` answers them (`[{name, color, items, id}]`, ascending by id) for the launcher's start picker; `id` is the `loadout` of `POST /auth/launch` ([Loadouts](#loadouts)). 401 when signed out |
| `GET /account/placed` | The player's own placed tents, callers, blinds, stands, tripods, towers and climbing ropes: `{items: [{id, reserve, kind, name, x, y}]}`, `x` and `y` percent of the reserve's map picture; `id` is the `tent` of `POST /auth/launch` for a tent ([The launch map](#the-launch-map)). 401 when signed out |
| `GET /account/inventory` | The player's own items: `{items: [{guid, id, name, variant, category, categoryName, units, slot, equipped, expires, sell}]}`, one row per owned copy, oldest first. `guid` names the copy (`POST /account/sell`); `sell` is null, or `{currency, refund}` when the copy can be sold back now ([Selling back](#selling-back)). Name, variant and the store's sub-category (key and title) are the catalogue's (`""` for an item it lacks); `units` is null for an item that is not counted; `equipped` is a slot above 0; `expires` is the end of a license or timed item in unix seconds (the item's metadata key `expires`, 0 for an unlimited one), null for an item with none; the store shows it. Nothing on the server writes this key yet (a purchase stores empty metadata), so a license shows its end only when the item's metadata carries it. With `all_licenses` on, each license item is one row with `expires` 0 ([Licenses](#licenses)). Item id 0 is an item like any other. 401 when signed out |
| `GET /account/events` | `{play, event}`: whether the player plays seasonal events (on by default) and the event that runs now as `{name, define, reserves}`, or null ([Seasonal events](#seasonal-events)). 401 when signed out |
| `GET /account/events/panel` | The running event's panel for a player who plays events: `{name, title, start, end, community, contributors, missions, items}`, else `{"event": null}` ([Seasonal events](#seasonal-events)). 401 when signed out |
| `POST /account/discord` | Same checks as buy, no form: `{first}`, true the first time for the account and false after (the column `accounts.discord_invited`, schema 20, 0 for every account before); the launcher and the website show their one-time Discord dialog when it is true. 400 from no page of this server, 401 when signed out |
| `POST /account/restore-offer` | Same checks as `/account/discord`: `{first}`, true the first time for the account and false after (the column `accounts.restore_offered`, schema 23, 0 for every account before), and false when the account has a zip restore (`restore_audit` row whose `zip_sha256` is not `archive:<n>` and whose mode is not `merge`) or an upload waiting (`uploads`); an archive claim (waiting, restored or rejected) does not stop it, the player may still have a backup zip with more than the archive held; the launcher and the website show their one-time "Do you have a backup of your profile?" dialog when it is true. 400 from no page of this server, 401 when signed out |
| `POST /account/events` | Form `play=1` or `play=0`, same checks as buy: stores the setting and answers like the `GET`; 400 for any other value |
| `POST /account/settings` | Form `use_imperial=1` or `use_imperial=0`, same checks as buy: stores the account's units, answers `{use_imperial}`; 400 for any other value ([the game's language and units](#sign-in-and-tokens)) |
| `GET /account/friends` | `{friends, requests, pending}`, each a list of `{handle, online, avatar}` sorted by handle: the player's friends, the players who asked it, the players it asked ([Friends](#friends)); `online` is only true for friends; `avatar` as in [Avatars](#avatars). 401 when signed out |
| `GET /account/friends/search?text=<text>` | `{hunters: [{handle, online, avatar}]}`: up to 10 players whose handle contains the text (any case, at least 3 characters once the spaces around it are left out; a shorter text finds nothing and is not counted), the exact handle first, then by handle, never the player itself or a banned account; `online` only true for the player's friends. 429 `rate_limited` past 30 searches an hour of the account ([Read limits](#read-limits)); 401 when signed out |
| `POST /account/friends/add`, `POST /account/friends/remove` | Form `handle` (any case), same checks as buy: `add` accepts the other's open request, else asks them; `remove` declines, cancels or ends a friendship, as `Friend/add` and `Friend/remove`. `{status: ok}`; `404` `invalid` for the player's own handle, a banned one or one with no account here |
| `GET /account/bans` | `{bans: [{handle, avatar, ts}]}`: the players the account banned from its multiplayer games ([The host's bans](#the-hosts-bans)), the latest first (`ts` Unix seconds; `avatar` as in [Avatars](#avatars), `null` for none); a banned account that was deleted is not listed. 401 when signed out |
| `POST /account/bans/unban` | Form `handle`, same checks as buy: lifts the account's own ban of that player. `{status: ok}`; `404` `invalid` for a handle the account has not banned (another account's ban is never touched), 401 when signed out |
| `GET /account/messages` | `{conversations: [{handle, preview, ts, unread, avatar}]}`: the server's notice conversation first, then the restored ones as saved ([Messages](#messages)); `avatar` is the partner's ([Avatars](#avatars)), `null` for the notices. 401 when signed out |
| `GET /account/messages/thread?handle=<handle>` | `{messages: [{mine, content, ts}]}`, newest first, of the conversation with that handle (any case); marks nothing read. `404` `invalid` for a handle that is none of the player's conversations; 401 when signed out |
| `GET /account/missions` | The player's own missions page: `{missions: [{id, title, desc, state, objectives, objectives_done, objective_weapons, needs, reward, reserve, species, weapon}], groups: [{id, title, desc, favourite, missions}], completed, auto_mission_activate, activate_only_favourite, hide_completed_favourite}` ([Missions](#missions)). `missions` has every definition: `state` is `active`, `available`, `completed` or `locked`, `desc` has no markup, `objectives` the objective titles and `objectives_done` one flag per objective, true when the account has it (all of them for a completed mission), `objective_weapons` one list per objective of the weapons it accepts (one name per weapon, empty when it names none: [Weapons of a mission](#weapons-of-a-mission)), `needs` the titles of the missions it depends on that are not completed yet, for every state (a mission restored as active or completed may still list some), `#<id>` for a dependency with no definition; the page shows it for `locked` ones only, as the reason, and `reserve`, `species` and `weapon` are the first name each of the definition's lists names (`""` for none). 401 when signed out; `404` `invalid` when the catalogue has no missions |
| `POST /account/missions/<call>` | `<call>` is `activate`, `activate_all_available`, `update_automatic_activation`, `update_activate_only_favourite`, `update_hide_completed_favourite` or `update_favourite`, with the form its `POST /v1/Mission/<call>` takes (without the token) and the same effect: `{status: ok}`; `409` `refused` for a mission that is not available or a group id that is no number; `404` `invalid` for another call or a catalogue without missions. 401 when signed out; 400 when not from the website's own page |
| `GET /account/missions/daily` | The player's own daily missions page ([Daily missions](#daily-missions)): `{today, ends_in, frozen, broken, days: [{number, stars, gms}], missions: [{title, desc, objectives, reserve, rewards, species, gender, giver, done}]}`: the 7 days of the streak's block (`stars` the difficulty id, `gms` the reward) and the mission of each, in that order, empty (`title` `""`) for a day without one; `species` the species define of the mission (null when it names none), `gender` 0 male or 1 female (the page turns them into the species picture) and `giver` the alias of the giver in lower case (null for a day without a mission); `ends_in` the seconds until the UTC day ends, `broken` true when the frozen streak was broken (not for a new one). 401 when signed out; `404` `invalid` when the server has no daily missions |
| `POST /account/missions/daily/activate` | No form: the very handler of `Daily_Mission/activate_user_progress`. `{status: ok}`; 401 when signed out; 400 when not from the website's own page; `404` `invalid` when the server has no daily missions |
| `POST /account/gallery/delete` | Form `id`, same checks as buy: deletes the photo of the player's own gallery (its row and files, [Gallery](#gallery)). `{status: deleted}`; `404` `invalid` for an id that is none of the player's photos |
| `GET /account/gallery/zip` | The player's own gallery as a zip of its pictures at full size (`application/zip`, each named as its link ends). 401 when signed out |

**Limits.** Each request fits a proxy's 100 MB request limit: a body over 90 MiB is `413` (`too_large`) and is not read. A zip is at most 1 GiB: a part that takes it over is `413` and ends the upload. One upload at a time per account and four on the whole server, from the start to the finish (`429`, status `busy`); an upload that gets no part for 30 minutes is deleted and frees both (a later request for it is `404` `invalid`: start again). A new start, or a delete of the account, drops the account's upload that waits for its next part, so an interrupted upload can start over at once. The parts are joined in a file of `data_dir/uploads-tmp`, deleted when the upload ends (a file a stopped server left there is deleted 30 minutes after its last write); the finish reads the zip from that file, so memory stays bounded whatever the zip's size. The reader's caps apply (`Limits`: 1 GiB of content, 100,000 entries, 100:1 compression, JSON depth 64), so a zip bomb is `400` (`invalid`) before anything is parsed. Stored zips are limited (settings `upload_account_files`, `upload_account_bytes`, `upload_total_bytes`, `upload_min_free_bytes`; restored zips do not count): an account may store at most 3 zips for the operator (waiting plus refused, below) and 1 GB of them; the server at most 5 GB, and none when the data disk has less than 10 GB free. A start over the account's limits is `429` `quota` ("You have 3 backups waiting for the operator. You can upload again once the operator has reviewed them." or "Your backups waiting for the operator already use 1 GB."), over the server's `429` `paused` ("Uploads are paused for now, try again later."). The same checks run on every part (the received bytes may not pass what the account has left, nor the server's room or the disk's floor: the upload ends) and when the zip is kept; a quota keeps no file and sends no alert. The operator frees room with `review reject`; a player cannot remove a stored zip (only "Delete my data" deletes the account with its zips).

**Rules.** Every zip that the reader accepts is kept for an operator and never restored automatically. Whether the zip's Steam id is the one the player signed in with is shown to the operator as information, not as proof: anyone holding the zip can edit the field.

| The zip | Result | `status` |
|---|---|---|
| Not a zip, or refused by the reader (caps, unsafe names, not a backup zip, a claim key that does not match) | `400`, nothing kept | `invalid` |
| A user id that was restored before (by this account or any other) | Refused, nothing kept: "a backup can be restored once" | `already` |
| A user id another account holds | Refused, nothing kept | `taken` |
| Any other zip (ours, or another tool's: [restore.md](restore.md#backups-of-other-tools); the Steam id in our manifest is the only claim of ownership, so another tool's zip is never a Steam match) | Kept as `restores/<account id>/<SHA-256>.zip`, a row in `uploads`; nothing is restored. The gallery pictures the zip lists by link and does not hold are fetched first from the `picture_link_hosts` and kept beside it as `<SHA-256>.pictures.zip` ([Pictures from links](restore.md#pictures)). The player is told that an operator will check it | `review` |

When a zip is kept, the server also sends one message to the `review_webhook` of the settings file, if set: a `Backup waiting for review` alert (see [Discord alerts](#discord-alerts)). The refusals above (`already`, `taken`) are `409`. A user id outside 1 to 1,999,999,999 is kept like any other zip and refused when the operator approves it (below).

The reader takes a `manifest.json` at the root of the zip or one folder down (a folder zipped instead of its content). The reason of a zip that is readable but holds no backup (the reader's "not a backup zip") is what the player can act on, found from the entry names alone: `Your backup is inside another folder or zip. Choose the zip your backup tool made, as it is: not a folder, not a zip you made yourself.` (a `.zip` entry, or a `manifest.json`, `index.json` or `ARCHIVE_MANIFEST.json` deeper than one folder), else `This zip holds a program, not a backup: upload the file the program made (it ends in _PRIVATE.zip)` (a `.py`, `.exe`, `.bat` or `.dll` file), else `This zip holds only pictures, not a backup.` and the same sentence to choose the zip (only `.jpg`, `.jpeg`, `.png`, `.gif`, `.webp` or `.bmp` files); any other reason keeps `This file cannot be read as a backup: <reader's reason>. Nothing was changed.` and the same sentence.

A zip that arrived whole but cannot be read as a backup (the two `invalid` rows above about the content, not a missing or short part, which the player just sends again) is also recorded: one `upload_results` row `kind = refused` with the reason, shown on the restore page as "Your upload of <date> could not be read" (the `decisions` of `GET /account/restore`, no inbox message), one `Upload refused` alert to the `review_webhook` (see [Discord alerts](#discord-alerts); its `kept` line is `r<account id>-<first 8 hex of the zip's SHA-256>`), and the file moved to `data_dir/refused/<account id>/<SHA-256>.zip` (its reason beside it as `<SHA-256>.reason`) instead of being deleted. It counts in the account's stored zips; the same bytes again are kept once; when a quota leaves no room it is not kept and no message is sent. It stays until the operator rejects it (`review reject r<account id>-<8 hex>`): nothing deletes it by time. "Delete my data" and the account's deletion remove that folder too.

Every outcome of an upload except a received part writes one console line, `upload of account <id>: <status>: <message>`: a zip refused by a quota keeps nothing, so this line is its only record (the reader's reason for `invalid`, which of the `busy`, `quota` and `paused` cases). On the VPS the console goes to the host journal ([deploy.md](deploy.md#logs)).

A zip refused as no backup also writes one console line for the operator, who sees the file's name only once it is kept: `refused zip of account <id>: <size> bytes, <n> entries, first: <up to 5 entry names>` (names only, a control character shown as `?`, a name cut at 80 characters; `no entries read` for a file that is no zip or that the reader's caps refuse to open).

**Review.** The operator works the kept zips with the server's command line (no web page):

```
dotnet run --project server/Server -c Release -- [--settings <file>] review list | approve <id> [--keep-handle] [--account <account id>] | reject <id> [--account <account id>] [reason]
```

Upload ids are never reused (`uploads.id` is AUTOINCREMENT, from 100 at least on a database migrated from before). `--account <account id>` (the account in the alert) makes `approve` and `reject` refuse, changing nothing, when the waiting zip belongs to another account; both print `approved|rejected <id>, account <account id>`.

`list` prints, per kept zip: its id, the account, the zip's user id and handle, the time, the note, and whether the zip's Steam id equals the account's verified one (information only); then each refused zip as `r<account id>-<8 hex>`, `refused`, `account <id>`, `handle <handle>`, the time, `<n> bytes`, the reason (tab separated). `reject r<account id>-<8 hex>` deletes that refused file. `approve <id>` runs the restore with the rules below and prints the restore report or the reason it refused; with `--keep-handle` the account keeps its own name instead of taking the zip's (the player's notice says so). `reject <id> [reason]` deletes the kept zip and its `uploads` row and keeps the reason (the rest of the line) for the player. Like `import backup` it runs on its own and needs the recordings.

**A public save on an archive claim.** A save of a public profile (mode `public`, [Backups of other tools](restore.md#backups-of-other-tools)) whose user id is the one of the account's archive claim (a `restore_audit` row `archive:<uid>`) is not refused as already restored: approving it only adds what the claim lacked and the account has not (`Restorer.Merge`): only the saved replies of hunts and their details, gallery lists and competitions (`extras`: `Expedition_list`, `Public_user_expedition`, `Gallery_list*`, `Page_content_*`), the lodges it lacks (`lodges` by source and id; a saved lodge of a type the account already holds replaces that lodge in place, keeping its id, only when nothing is placed in it, such as the empty lodge the server gave for a lodge item, else the account's lodge stays), and the gallery pictures not yet in its gallery; the save's profiles and every other reply are not added. The merge's audit row has mode `merge`, which the start em$ rule does not count as a restore of the wallet. Everything the account has stays: items, wallet, skills, statistics, achievements, trophies, profile, name, friends, the rows restored from the claim and what it played since. The audit row, the `restored` result and a notice are written as for any approve, with no `restored_ids` row.

**What the player is told.** A refused approve stores its reason as the zip's `note`; an approve that restores, and a reject, add a row to `upload_results` (the `decisions` above) and every one of these three leaves a message from the server's notice sender in the account's inbox, the thread `Chat/conversations` lists: "Your backup was not restored: <reason>" (once per reason: the same refusal again adds nothing), "The backup was restored: …" with the user id and the name, or "Your backup was rejected by the operator", with the reason when there is one. The website's restore page shows the same. The messages are English, like the server's other notices.

| `approve` | Result |
|---|---|
| The user id was not restored before, is not held by another account, is from 1 to 1,999,999,999 | Full restore, once per game user id: the profile rows are replaced (uploading is the player's consent; hunts, kills and photos made here stay) and the account takes the zip's handle when it is a plain name of at most 32 characters nobody else has (otherwise it keeps its own). An account that has not played (no row in `expeditions` and `events`) also takes the zip's user id: the Steam id, tokens and sessions follow it, and so do the account's photos: `photos/<old id>/` is merged into `photos/<new id>/` (a name clash keeps the account's own photo). An account that has played **keeps its own user id** ([ADR 0009](adr/0009-restore-a-zip-onto-an-account-that-has-played.md)): nothing moves, and no id of the zip can clash with the ids handed out here; the notice and the `decisions` entry carry the account's own id. One audit row, one row in `restored_ids` (the zip's user id, for either kind of account); the row in `uploads` goes, the zip stays. The whole database is **not** copied (`Store.Backup` is the local rule): the kept zip is the way back |
| A user id restored before, held by another account, or outside that range | Refused with the reason, which becomes the zip's note; the zip stays kept until the operator rejects it |

A user id is restored once, enforced by the primary key of `restored_ids`, so two uploads at the same moment cannot both win. If a restore turns out wrong, the operator deletes the account's rows (or the account) and imports the kept zip again with `import backup <zip> --account <id>`; deleting the account frees its user id.

**Delete my data.** `POST /account/delete` deletes the `accounts` row, and with it, by the foreign keys, every per-player row (the crash reports too), the tokens, the cookie sessions, the review rows, the decisions and the restored user id; then the account's `restores/<id>/`, `photos/<id>/` and `reports/<id>/` folders (the pictures a restore unpacked are in the second, [Pictures](restore.md#pictures); the last holds the problem reports). A trophy shot the player took of another member's kill in a multiplayer hunt is that member's photo, in his folder ([Gallery](#gallery)): it stays with his kill, and only its `taker` is cleared. A restore that changed the account's user id moved the folders and the crash reports to the new id (a report's reference and file names change with it), so none is left under the old one. The Steam id and handle go with the row (the Steam id and the time of the delete are kept apart, below), so signing in again makes a new account. It refuses (`429`) while an upload or a report of the account is running. The replies are `{status, message}`; the cookie is cleared.

**What a delete keeps.** Three small records outlive the account. The time of the delete with the Steam id (`steam_deletions`: Steam id and time, nothing else), for 24 hours and then removed by the daily purge (or at server start), so that a new account of the same Steam id cannot make a claim at once ([Archive claims](#archive-claims)); the operator's ban record (`banned_steam`: Steam id, time, reason and, once lifted, the lift time), so that deleting the account does not undo a ban; and, for an account that linked Discord, one row of the reward ledger (`discord_rewards`: a keyed hash of the Discord id and nothing else, no account, handle or time; [Discord Linked Roles](#discord-linked-roles)), so that an account made again, or another one, is not paid the reward for linking twice. The ban record is not removed when the ban is lifted: it stays so that a later claim from that Steam id shows "banned before". The Discord id and its tokens (`discord_links`) go with the account. No handle, profile, game data, claim row, archived user id or claim state is kept. The privacy page of the website (`Data.Delete.Text`, `Data.Keep.Steam`, `Data.Keep.Discord`) and the delete dialog (`Restore.Delete.Text`, `Restore.Delete.Discord`) say the same.

## Problem reports

When the game fails on a player's PC the launcher builds one zip (its contents: [launcher.md](launcher.md)) and sends it from the signed-in player. `POST /account/report` on `web_host`, signed in with the `hr_session` cookie, header `X-Requested-With: launcher` (or any value: a form of another site cannot set it), header `X-Report-Trigger: <trigger>`, the body the zip (`Content-Type: application/zip`).

| Trigger | Sent when |
|---|---|
| `manual` | The player pressed "Report a problem" in the launcher's Settings |
| `short` | The game ran less than 60 s |
| `exitcode` | The game's exit code was not 0 |
| `nowindow` | No visible game window was ever seen |

| Reply | When |
|---|---|
| `200` `{"reference":"p<account>-<sha8>"}` | Kept. `sha8` is the first 8 hex digits of the zip's SHA-256, so the same zip sent twice is one report |
| `401` | Not signed in |
| `400` | A trigger that is none of the four, a request from no page of this server, or a body that is no readable zip (the zip is opened, not unpacked) |
| `413` | Over 6 MiB (`Content-Length` over it is refused unread; a chunked body stops at the limit) |
| `429` | A report of the account is being received (one at a time, from the start of the request to the end), or the account sent 5 in the last 24 hours |
| `507` | The data disk would have less than `upload_min_free_bytes` free after it (the setting of [Upload a backup zip](#upload-a-backup-zip), counting the declared size) |

A report is `reports/<account id>/<reference>.zip` with `<reference>.json` beside it (`{ts, trigger, size}`, `ts` in epoch seconds); the 24-hour limit counts those notes. It is only read by the operator: never through `/account/restore/*`, never restored. It is deleted 30 days after `ts` (a zip without a note, by its file time), at server start and once a day, and with the account (`POST /account/delete`). When a new report is kept the server posts one `Problem report` alert to the `review_webhook` (see [Discord alerts](#discord-alerts)).

**Review.** `GET /admin/reports` lists the reports (`{reference, account, handle, ts, trigger, size}`, newest first) and `GET /admin/reports/<reference>` downloads the zip ([Admin API](#admin-api)). On the command line:

```
dotnet run --project server/Server -c Release -- [--settings <file>] review reports | reports <reference> <file> | diagnostics <account>
```

`review reports` prints one tab-separated line per report: the reference, `account <id>`, `handle <handle>`, the time, the trigger, `<n> bytes`; with a reference and a file it copies that zip to the file (which must not exist). `review diagnostics <account>` (and `GET /admin/diagnostics?account=`) shows what the server knows of the account in the last 30 days, to read beside a report:

- the kept [crash reports](#crash-reports): time, kind, module, the top frames and the message;
- the hunts that ended without the game's disconnect call: closed hunts whose end is their last heartbeat (the server closed them after 30 s of silence, or the player's next start replaced them; at most 100). A hunt that got no heartbeat and ended by a disconnect is not in the list: the server cannot tell it from a replaced one;
- the closed hunts that lasted under 60 s (at most 100);
- how many times the game sent event 30, "the connection to the host failed" ([Multiplayer](#multiplayer)).

## Crash reports

The game sends its error reports to `crash_host` as envelopes: a header line, then per item a header line (`type`, and `length` in bytes) and its payload. With player state the server reads them instead of appending them whole to `telemetry.jsonl` (it held every report's install paths and device hash and was never pruned). The game gets 204 whatever happens.

- **Kept:** `event` items whose `level` is `fatal` or `error` (no level counts as error), and `session` items whose `status` is `abnormal` (a run that ended without a crash report or a clean exit). Everything else is dropped: warnings, info, other sessions, dumps and other attachments, transactions. An envelope is read to its first cut or garbled item, at most 8 items.
- **Whose:** the `user.id` of the event, or the `did` of a session, when it is the user id of an account of this server. A report with none, or of an account that does not exist, is dropped.
- **What is stored** (`crash_reports`, schema 27, one row per account and event id; the same id again changes nothing): `ts` (the report's own time, never later than its arrival; a report older than 30 days is dropped), `kind` (`fatal`, `error`, `abnormal`), `module`, `frames`, `message` and `release`. `frames` are the top 8 frames of the exception's stack, the crashing one first, each `module!function` or, when the report names no function, `module+offset` (hex), the module found by address in the report's own list of loaded images, shown by its file name. `message` is the exception's type and value (else the event's message), at most 300 characters. Every Windows path in a stored text or image name (a drive such as `C:\`, a share such as `\\pc\c$\`, or a device or extended form: `\\.\`, `\\?\`, `\\?\UNC\`) is reduced to its file name when that has a known extension (`dll`, `exe`, `log`, `ini`, `dat`, `sav`, `pak`, ...) and does not sit directly in a `Users` or `home` folder; any other path (a folder, or a name with another dot such as a profile folder) becomes `<path>` together with the rest of its line, so a user name never stays. Nothing else of a report is stored: the device hash, user name, address, machine name, other contexts and breadcrumbs are dropped.
- **Kept** the newest 200 per account and kind (a flood of harmless errors never pushes out a fatal report or an abnormal session) and 30 days; a purge at start and once a day deletes the older ones. The rows go with the account (`POST /account/delete`).

`telemetry.jsonl` keeps the telemetry host's requests (the death types and organ data the heartbeat does not carry), each body cut at 64 KB.
