At start the file is rewritten once when it holds crash host lines (an older server appended them whole) or is over 32 MiB: the crash host lines go through the rules above first (those of the last 30 days; the rest is dropped) and the newest 32 MiB of the other lines stay. A server without player state keeps the crash host's requests in the file like the telemetry host's.

## Archive claims

Players can no longer make a backup, so a signed-in player may claim the public profile the operator's archive kept for them ([ADR 0008](adr/0008-archive-claims.md)). The archive is one SQLite file, `archive.db` (the setting `archive_db`, default `<data_dir>/archive.db`), made on the operator's PC and copied into the data folder. The server opens it read only, and no route serves it. No file: claims are off (lookups answer `found: false`, the website hides the claim box). The file is looked for at every call, so copying it in needs no restart.

```
meta(key, value)                                  -- 'format' = '1'
player(uid, handle, hostname, hunterscore)        -- hostname = the handle in lower case, unique: the lookup key
reply(uid, path, endpoint, params, status, body)  -- the replies a public-mode backup zip of that player would hold, file by file
```

A claim reads the player's `reply` rows with the same reader as a public zip (`Importer.FromReplies`, [restore.md](restore.md)) and writes them with the same restore (`Restorer.Apply`).

**Rules.**

| | |
|---|---|
| Lookup | The name must match a handle exactly, in any case. 5 lookups per account per UTC day, counted in the table `claim_lookups` (schema 18), so a restart keeps them. A claim of a name the archive does not have costs one too |
| One claim per account, ever | A row of the account in `claims` (any state, also a rejected one) refuses another claim |
| Hunterscore below 10,000, and nobody else claimed the player | Restored at once |
| Hunterscore 10,000 or more, or another account already has a `claims` row for that archived player | Kept as a `waiting` row for the operator; nothing is restored. The player gets a notice in the inbox and the `review_webhook` gets a `Claim waiting for review` alert (see [Discord alerts](#discord-alerts)) |
| The account has played (a hunt was opened or an event came in) | `409` `confirm` until the player confirms the overwrite (`confirm=overwrite`) |
| The Steam id's last account was deleted less than 24 hours ago | `409` `refused` with the time the claim is possible from; no lookup is counted |

**After a delete, and bans.** Deleting an account does not make its Steam id new at once. `POST /account/delete` keeps the Steam id and the time of the delete (the table `steam_deletions`, schema 32; nothing else of the account) for 24 hours (`Claims.CooldownHours`; older rows are deleted at server start and in the daily purge, so a row can outlive the 24 hours by up to a day, but it is not read after them). A claim from an account of that Steam id less than 24 hours after the delete is refused with `409` `refused` before it costs a lookup, and its message says from when a claim is possible (UTC); "I played before" at the first sign-in is refused the same way, and the new account's inbox says so. The operator's `claim` command is not held back. After 24 hours the account makes its one claim as any account does.

A Steam id the operator banned (`admin ban`, [the admin API](#admin-api)) is a row of `banned_steam` (Steam id, time, reason, lift time; schema 32), not tied to an account row, so `POST /account/delete` does not lift it. The Steam sign-in of a banned Steam id, with or without an account, ends on the sign-in problem page `reason=banned` ("This Steam account is banned and cannot sign in. To talk about it, come to our Discord.") and makes no account. **The reason is the player's to read:** the sign-in in progress keeps it, and `GET /auth/pending` answers that browser `{step: "banned", csrf: "", reason}` (`""` without a reason); the page shows it under the text ("Reason: …", as plain text) with the Discord invite (`link_discord`). The URL never carries it, so a link someone else makes shows no reason. Write it for the player. `admin ban <user id | handle | Steam id> [reason]` also sets `banned` on the account the Steam id has now (an account without a Steam id is refused); `admin unban <Steam id>` clears both and keeps the row with `lifted_ts`, so the `Claim waiting for review` and `Claim restored` alerts (`earlier ban: <date>, lifted <date>`) and the `review list` line of a claim from that Steam id (`steam id banned before: <date>, lifted <date>`) say it was banned before.

**Attempts of a banned Steam id (schema 43).** Every sign-in that ends on the banned page (Steam's return, a banned account's session, the name form: the three places the sign-in checks the ban) writes one server log line, `banned sign-in <UTC time> steam <Steam id> account <id or none> route <web | launcher link | app> attempt <n>` (no reason, no cookie or token), counts on the `banned_steam` row (`attempts`, `last_attempt`, added at start when missing, guarded by the columns) and sends the `🚫 Banned Steam id tried to sign in` alert to the review channel. `app` is the loopback sign-in of the launcher and of the backup tool alike (they use the same start route). The alert is rate-limited by the row: one for the first attempt, then one only when the previous attempt is an hour old, so a player who retries gets one alert per burst while the log line and the count see every try; the same page reload (`GET /auth/pending`) is no attempt. An account flagged `banned` by hand without a `banned_steam` row has no count: each of its attempts logs and alerts. `review list` shows each Steam id banned now that tried to sign in (`banned`, Steam id, account, attempts, time of the last); lifting the ban drops it from the list, and a later ban of the same Steam id keeps counting on the same row.

**What a claim does.** It replaces the account's items, skills, achievements, trophies, lodges, loadouts, missions and challenges, and the saved replies kept as extras, with the archived ones (the restore of [Upload a backup zip](#upload-a-backup-zip), no merge). The statistics are the archive's, and the hunts made here count on top of them ([ADR 0003](adr/0003-statistics-are-a-base-plus-server-hunts.md)). Hunts, kills and photos made here stay. The account's wallet stays too (an archived profile has none), with gm$ raised to at least 15,000 like after every restore. **The account's user id never changes**: no `restored_ids` row is written, so an own-mode zip of the same player can still be approved later, and a claim never changes the user id, whether or not the account has played ([ADR 0008](adr/0008-archive-claims.md)). No zip is kept; the audit row's `zip_sha256` reads `archive:<archived user id>`. The handle is the archived one when the player asked for it, it is a plain name of at most 32 characters and no other account has it (any case); otherwise the account keeps its own, and the notice says so. The archived friends are connected to the accounts that claimed their profiles, whichever claim came first ([Friends](#friends)). The player gets a notice in the inbox, like an approved upload.

**A claim over a restored backup replaces nothing.** When the account has a restore of its own backup (a `restore_audit` row of mode `my-account`), an approved claim never replaces what that backup restored: not the items, wallet, skills, statistics, achievements, trophies, lodges, loadouts, missions, challenges, profile, handle, avatar, gallery pictures or other picture files, nor a saved reply kept as an extra. It only fills what the backup left empty: a table in which the account has no row (items, skills, statistics, achievements, trophies, lodges, loadouts, profile, wallet), a saved reply under a path the account lacks, a gallery picture the account does not have yet, a picture file whose name is free. The archived name is not taken (the notice says the backup was kept), the claim becomes `restored` like any other, and an audit row `archive:<archived user id>` is written; the report of that row says `profile kept, only what it had no row for was added`. The check is made when the claim is decided, so a claim that waited for the operator is judged by the account's rows at approval, which is why `review list` and the alert show `backup restored` on it. This holds for the operator's `claim` command too.

| Endpoint (`web_host`, session cookie, as under [Upload a backup zip](#upload-a-backup-zip)) | |
|---|---|
| `GET /account/archive?name=<name>` | One lookup: `{found: false}`, or `{found: true, handle, hunterscore, items, trophies, lodges, skills, statistics, achievements, nameFree}`: the counts are what a claim would restore, the three flags say whether the archive has that part, `nameFree` whether the archived handle could be taken. `429` `{status: limit}` over the limit; 401 when signed out |
| `GET /account/archive` | `{enabled, claimed}`: `claimed` is null, or the account's claim `{state: waiting\|restored\|rejected, handle, ts}`. 401 when signed out |
| `POST /account/archive/claim` | Form `name`, `old_name=1` (take the archived handle) or `0`, `confirm=overwrite` (optional), same checks as `POST /account/restore`. `{status, message}`: `restored`, `review` (200), `refused` (409: the account has its claim already, or the profile cannot be restored), `unknown` (404), `limit` (429), `confirm` (409) |

**The operator.**

```
dotnet run --project server/Server -c Release -- [--settings <file>] review list | approve c<id> [--keep-handle] | reject c<id> [reason]
dotnet run --project server/Server -c Release -- [--settings <file>] claim <account> <archived handle or user id> [--keep-handle]
```

`review list` prints a waiting claim as `c<id>`, `claim`, `account <id>`, `archived <uid>`, `handle <handle>`, `hunterscore <n>`, the time and the note (why it waits), tab separated, after the kept zips. `review approve c<id>` runs the restore above (the player's choice of name stands unless `--keep-handle` keeps the account's own) and the row becomes `restored`. `review reject c<id> [reason]` marks the row `rejected` and keeps the reason; the player gets it in the inbox, and the claim stays the account's one. `claim` restores an archived player onto an account (its user id or handle) at once: no lookup limit, no review, no confirmation, and it replaces the account's earlier claim row. All of them run on their own and need the recordings, like `import backup`. Deleting an account deletes its `claims` and `claim_lookups` rows, so the archived player can be claimed again.

When the account has a restore of its own backup (`restore_audit` mode `my-account`) `review list` adds the column `backup restored` before the note: the approve then replaces nothing of that backup and only adds what it lacks ([above](#archive-claims)).

Claim ids are never reused (`claims.id` is AUTOINCREMENT, from 100 at least on a database migrated from before), so a `c<id>` in an old alert never names another player's claim after an account is deleted.

## News

The posts of the website's feed (`/` and `/feed`) and of the launcher's feed are rows of the server (schema 36), so the operator publishes one in a minute, without a code change, a pull request, a deploy or a launcher release. Online only.

**The tables.** `news` (`id`, never reused; `published` and `until` in Unix seconds, `until` null for no end; `link`; `picture`, the extension of the stored file or `slot:<name>` for a picture slot of the website and the launcher, null for none; `pinned`; `hidden`; `updated`, the version of the picture's link) and `news_texts` (`news_id`, `lang`, `title`, `body`, `link_text`): one row per language, **English always**. The migration step (`Store.CreateNews`) is guarded by its own checks only, not by the version: it creates the tables when they are missing and inserts the project's first three posts (the launcher 0.31 game check, the backup tool, the project in development, ids 3, 2 and 1) with all the translations they had in the 19 languages when the table has no row. Their pictures are the slots they showed before, `competition-hero-170039`, `competition-hero-170031` and `competition-hero-170026` (a `picture` of `slot:<name>`), and their links are `link:source`, `link:backup` and `link:source`.

**What a post is.** A title (200 characters at most) and a body (20,000) per language; a body is Markdown, written as plain text, and the clients draw it as elements, never as HTML: paragraphs (a blank line between them), `**bold**`, `*italic*` or `_italic_`, `[text](https://address)` and lists (`- item`, `1. item`); anything else, a tag or a script too, is shown as the characters it is, and only an http or https address is a link ([website.md](website.md#the-news) says where the reader is). A **link** is an https address or `link:<key>`, a link of the project's own that the website and the launcher resolve in their `settings.json` (`link:source` is its `link_source`; without it the text is plain); it needs its **link text** (100 characters), which a translation may give again (else the English one is shown). `{game}`, `{GAME}` and `{game_full}` in a title or a body are the game's name, as in the interface texts. A pinned post is listed above the newer ones.

**Schedule.** `--at` is when a post starts to show (default: now) and `--until` when it stops (the first second it no longer shows); a post for an event can be written before it starts. A hidden post never shows. Times are ISO (`2026-12-24T18:00:00Z`, no zone means UTC); `until` must be after `at`.

**`GET /news?lang=`** (the website's host, public, no cookie; the reverse proxy routes `/news` here, [deploy.md](deploy.md#the-community-server)): `{posts: [{id, published, pinned, title, body, link, linkText, picture}]}` of the posts that show now, pinned first, then newest first (then newest id), each in the language `lang` names (`fr`, `pt-BR`; an unknown or missing code is English) with English for what that language lacks, so a post with English only shows in English to every reader and a French translation added later shows to French readers at the next request. `picture` is the path `/news/<id>/picture?v=<updated>` or null (the clients then draw the default slide picture, the slot `news-default` of [images.md](images.md)). Sent with `Cache-Control: public, max-age=60`, so a post shows on the website and in the launcher within a minute of the command; the server itself reads the table on each request (a few rows). `GET /news/<id>/picture` is the file with its type, `nosniff` and `Content-Security-Policy: sandbox`, cached for a year (the `v` changes with the picture); 404 for a post without a picture and for one that does not show now (a scheduled post's picture is not public).

**Pictures.** `--picture file` stores a JPEG, PNG or WebP file of at most 5 MB in `<data_dir>/news/<id>.<jpg|png|webp>` (the data folder is the one writable folder of the container; the pictures of the gallery are there too), by its first bytes, **as given: there is no resizing**, because the server has no image library. The carousel draws it cropped to fill its 805×380 slide, so give it that shape (the thumbnail is the same picture at 175×95). A post may instead name a picture slot (`admin news edit <id> --picture-slot competition-hero-170039`: the operator's file for that slot, [images.md](images.md); `GET /news` gives it as `picture: "slot:<name>"`). A post with neither shows the default slide picture. A file the command reads must be reachable by the process: in the container that is `/srv` (the operator's folder), or the standard input for a body (`--body-file -`).

**Commands** (`state.db` in the data folder; a running server shows the change within a minute; the file options also take `-` for the standard input where it says so):

```
admin news add --title "…" --body-file post.md [--link URL --link-text "…"] [--picture file.jpg | --picture-slot name] [--at ISO] [--until ISO] [--pin] [--discord]
admin news list
admin news edit <id> [--title "…"] [--body-file post.md] [--link URL|none] [--link-text "…"|none] [--picture file|none | --picture-slot name] [--at ISO] [--until ISO|none] [--pin | --unpin] [--show]
admin news hide <id>
admin news lang <id> <code> --title "…" --body-file post.md [--link-text "…"]
```

`add` prints the new id and whether the post is shown or scheduled; `list` one line per post (id, `shown`, `scheduled`, `ended` or `hidden`, `pinned`, from, until, picture, languages, English title); `edit` changes the English text and the settings (`none` takes a link, a link text, an end or the picture away); `hide` takes a post off (`edit --show` brings it back); `lang` adds or replaces a translation, any time later (title and body both; the code is `fr`, `pt-BR`, …). A refusal (bad time, a link that is not https, a picture that is not JPEG, PNG or WebP or over 5 MB, an unknown id) prints why, exits 1 and changes nothing. On the VPS: `cd ~/web && docker compose exec -T server dotnet server.dll admin news add --title "…" --body-file - --pin < post.md`.

**Discord.** `--discord` also posts the English title, body, link and picture to the announcements channel as one embed, through the webhook of the setting `news_webhook` (an `https` URL, like `review_webhook`; off by default: without `--discord` nothing is sent, and `--discord` without the setting is refused before anything is written). The post is saved first: when the webhook fails, or the post starts later (`--at` in the future: it would announce what nobody can read yet), the command says so and exits 1. The embed's picture is `https://<web_host>/news/<id>/picture`; a post whose picture is a slot (`--picture-slot`) has none in the embed, and a `link:<key>` link is in it only when the server's settings have `link_<key>` (the `link_discord` they already hold): `link:source` and `link:backup` are the website's and the launcher's settings, so use an https link for a post you announce.

**Admin API** (localhost only, like the other routes; the same as the commands, so a future admin panel only adds a form): `GET /admin/news` (every post with its texts), `POST /admin/news?discord=` (body `{title, body, link, linkText, at, until, pinned}`: the English text; 400 for a refusal, 502 when the post is saved but the Discord post failed), `PATCH /admin/news/<id>` (the same fields, and `hidden`; an empty `link`, `linkText` or `until` takes it away), `PUT /admin/news/<id>/lang/<code>` (`{title, body, linkText}`), `PUT /admin/news/<id>/picture` (the file as the body, up to 5 MB: the one admin body larger than a form), `PUT /admin/news/<id>/picture-slot/<name>` and `DELETE /admin/news/<id>/picture`.

## Discord alerts

Every alert to `review_webhook` and `signup_webhook` is plain text (no embeds) in one layout, built by `Alerts.Text`: a bold title with one emoji for the kind, then one `label: value` line per fact, then the exact command to run in a code span when there is one. A handle is in a code span (a `_` in it stays a `_`) and only plain name characters; the Steam profile is a `[profile](<link>)` link (the angle brackets keep Discord from adding a preview), or `none`.

| Alert | Channel | Lines after the title |
|---|---|---|
| `📦 Backup waiting for review` | review | `id`, `handle`, `account`, `backup user id`, `steam`, `run: review list` |
| `📜 Claim waiting for review` | review | `claim` (`c<id>`), `handle`, `account`, `archived id`, `hunterscore`, `steam`, `backup restored` (only when the account has a restore of its own backup), `earlier ban` (only when the Steam id was banned before), `run: review list` |
| `⛔ Upload refused` | review | `handle`, `account`, `steam`, `reason`, `kept` (`r<account id>-<8 hex>`) |
| `🩺 Problem report` | review | `handle`, `account`, `reference`, `trigger` (`manual`, `short`, `exitcode`, `nowindow`), `size` |
| `🚨 Read limit hit` | review | `handle`, `account`, `limits` (the three caps of [Read limits](#read-limits)); at most one an hour per account |
| `🚫 Banned Steam id tried to sign in` | review | `steam id`, `steam`, `account` (when there is one), `route` (`web`, `launcher link`, `app`), `attempts` (when the Steam id has a ban row), `time`; one for the first attempt and one after an hour without one |
| `🆕 New account` | signup | `handle`, `account`, `steam`, `archived` (the claimed handle and its hunterscore, when the sign-up came with a claim), `outcome` (`fresh start`, `claim restored`, `claim waiting for review` or `claim refused (<kind>)`) |
| `✅ Claim restored` | signup | `handle`, `account`, `archived handle`, `hunterscore`, `steam`, `earlier ban` (only when the Steam id was banned before) |

Example:

```text
📜 **Claim waiting for review**
claim: c12
handle: `exampleuser`
account: 2000000123
archived id: 1234567
hunterscore: 10450
steam: [profile](<https://steamcommunity.com/profiles/99999999999999993>)
run: `review list`
```

## Behind a reverse proxy

A proxy in front of the server speaks plain HTTP to it (a listener without `cert`). The game routes by host name, which a proxy hides, so a local forwarder in front of the proxy puts the name the client asked for in a request header. The server reads `host_header` (default `X-Game-Host`) and routes by its value, only when the peer address is in `trusted_proxies`; from any other peer the header is ignored. A value that is not a host name (optionally with a port) is ignored too.

## Admin API

`/admin/*`, Swagger UI (`/swagger`) and the OpenAPI documents are for a person at the server's machine. A request is served only when all of these hold: its `Host` is `localhost` or `127.0.0.1`; it came in on a listener marked `"admin": true`; its peer address (not a header) is a loopback address; and it carries no header that says a proxy relayed it (`X-Forwarded-For`, `X-Forwarded-Host`, `X-Forwarded-Proto`, `Forwarded`, `Via`, `X-Real-IP`, the `host_header`). Any other request for those names gets 403. No listener is an admin listener unless marked, and a proxy on the same machine is a loopback peer too, so keep the listener a proxy connects to unmarked and give the admin API its own loopback listener (for example `127.0.0.1:8081`, reached from the VPS through an SSH tunnel). Admin bodies are limited to 64 KiB.

The routes (Swagger UI describes each; `account` is a user id or handle, left out: the local account, except `/admin/rescore`, where left out means every account). Except `status` and `misses`, they answer 503 when the player state is off (no connect to seed it from) (`reports` also when the server is offline, not a community server), and the ones that take `account` answer 404 for an account that does not exist:

| Route | Does |
|---|---|
| `GET /admin/status` | What the server serves from: the player state database, the recordings loaded, the recorded keys, the install's archive entries and the lines of the miss log |
| `GET /admin/wallet?account=`, `GET /admin/inventory?account=` | The account's balance per currency (`em`, `gm`); its owned items |
| `GET /admin/items/<item id>` | The item's definition as heartbeat update 10 sends it; 404 for an item with none |
| `POST /admin/grants/items?account=` | Body `{itemId, count}` (count 1 to 1,000 full stacks, default 1) in slot 0; not idempotent; 409 when the account would own more of the item than its limit ([Ownership limits](#ownership-limits)) |
| `POST /admin/grants/currency?account=` | Body `{currency: "em" or "gm", amount}` (negative takes; 409 when the balance would drop below 0); not idempotent |
| `POST /admin/rescore` | Below |
| `POST`, `GET` and `DELETE /admin/spawn` | Add a group of animals to the next hunt (the fields of `admin spawn`: `reserve`, `animal`, `males`, `females`, `x`, `z`, `radius`; and `minWeight`, `maxWeight`, `wounded`, `dormant`), list the entries and where the last connect put them, remove one entry (`?id=`) or all |
| `POST /admin/spawn/wake?account=` | Body `{populationId, group}` (from the list, `group` 1 or more): the running hunt wakes a dormant group with its next heartbeat |
| `GET /admin/misses?limit=` | The newest requests nothing could answer, keys only (1 to 1,000, default 50) |
| `GET /admin/reports`, `GET /admin/reports/<reference>` | The problem reports ([Problem reports](#problem-reports)), newest first; the zip of one by its reference (404 for a reference that is none) |
| `GET /admin/diagnostics?account=` | The account's last 30 days: crash reports, hunts that ended without a disconnect, hunts under 60 s, the event 30 count ([Problem reports](#problem-reports)) |
| `GET /admin/hunting` | Every player's server playtime and hunting figures with their handles, most playtime first (`{since, events, players: [{account, handle, huntSecondsHere, reserves, species, otherGm}]}`; `events` as in `GET /stats/hunting`; a reserve row has `gm`, `baseGm` (the gm$ without any bonus) and the paid and `comparable` rates, a species row `harvests`, `gm` and `baseGm`), without the 5-player floor; kept an hour ([Server playtime and hunting statistics](#server-playtime-and-hunting-statistics)) |
| `GET /admin/taxidermies?account=` | The account's taxidermized harvests, newest first: `{kill, species, harvested, done, currency, paid, ts}` ([Taxidermy](#taxidermy)); `currency`, `paid` and `ts` are null for one marked before the price existed |
| `POST /admin/taxidermies/<kill>/undo?account=&refund=` | Takes a taxidermy back: the harvest is a plain one again, and `refund` (default true) gives the price back with a wallet row (`{kill, currency, refunded}`); 404 for a harvest that is not taxidermized |
| `POST /admin/season/start` | Ends the current leaderboard season one second before now and starts the next number now: `{no, start, end}` of the new one; 409 when the current season started this second or later ([Leaderboards and seasons](#leaderboards-and-seasons)) |
| `POST /admin/ban?who=&reason=` | Bans a Steam id for good ([Archive claims](#archive-claims)): `who` is an account (user id or handle) or a Steam id (17 digits; it may have no account yet), `reason` at most 200 characters; `banned` is set on the Steam id's current account. `{steam, account}`; 404 for an unknown account, 409 for an account without a Steam id |
| `GET`, `POST` and `PATCH /admin/news`, `PUT /admin/news/<id>/lang/<code>`, `PUT` and `DELETE /admin/news/<id>/picture` | The news posts of the feed: [News](#news) |
| `POST /admin/unban?steam=` | Lifts the ban of a Steam id (17 digits) and of its current account; the ban stays on record with its lift time. `{steam, account}`; 400 for a value that is not 17 digits, 404 for a Steam id that is not banned |
| `POST /admin/accounts/<account>/message`, `POST /admin/message` | Body `{text}`: one inbox message from the server's notice sender to that account (user id or handle in the path; 404 for one that does not exist) or to every account ([Messages](#messages)). Plain text, 1 to 2,000 characters, kept as written (400 otherwise). `{sent}`: how many accounts got it. Not idempotent; no confirmation here |

`POST /admin/rescore?account=&kill=&dry_run=`: scores the stored harvests again with the rules of today (`Scoring`, the ammunition check, the stored kill events: the same step the heartbeat runs), for the account named (user id or handle) or, without `account`, every account. A harvest whose score or score type differs gets its stored harvest page updated (one that now scores nothing gets 0, so the boards stop listing it; the kept boards are dropped after a real run); when its css rounds to more than 0 and the account has no `wallet_log` row `CSS Reward (Animal Id: <kill id>)`, that gm$ is paid through `State.AddCurrency` with that summary (and sent as update 15). A reward already paid is never taken back. A disqualified harvest, or one with no score before and now (no antlers, a species without rules), is left as it is. Running it again pays nothing more. One transaction per account. `kill` (needs `account`, answered 400 otherwise, kill ids being per account) rescores that harvest alone (an unknown kill lists nothing), so an operator can apply just the harvests a dry run listed for a known cause. `dry_run` defaults to `true` and writes nothing; the reply is `[{account, rescored, paid, gms, harvests}]` with the same counts a real run would write; `harvests` lists each rescored harvest as `{kill, species, oldScore, newScore, gmsPaid}` (the gm$ its kill was paid before, for the record).

The same step from the command line, for a server whose admin API is not reachable (the community server's listener has no `admin`): `admin rescore [--account <id or handle>] [--kill <id>] [--apply]`, e.g. `cd ~/web && docker compose exec -T server dotnet server.dll admin rescore --account <id> --kill <kill id>` (the image's entry point is `dotnet server.dll`, the compose service is `server`). It runs `State.Rescore` on `data_dir` with the same rules, a dry run unless `--apply` is given, and `--kill` needs `--account`. It prints one JSON line per harvest whose score changes, `{"account","kill","species","oldScore","newScore","gmsPaid"}`, then, right after its harvests, one line for each account that changed or would be paid, with `rescored`, `paid`, `gms` and `applied`, then a last line saying dry run or applied. A running server drops its kept boards only when its own route runs: after `--apply` they show the change within 15 minutes (24 hours for an ended season's boards), or at once with `docker compose restart server`. Take a [backup](deploy.md#backups) first.

The command also reports, over every stored harvest it walks (not only the rescored ones, and with the `--account` and `--kill` filters applied), the harvests whose new score lies outside their species' `min_score` to `max_score` (the `variants` row `Scoring` uses; a species without a row is skipped): one JSON line per species and gender, after the account lines and before the last line, `{"species","gender","harvests","outside","min","max","lowest","highest"}`, e.g. `{"species":4,"gender":1,"harvests":12,"outside":1,"min":35,"max":60,"lowest":34.9653,"highest":51.2}` (`harvests` counts the scored harvests of that species and gender, `lowest` and `highest` their new scores). Only rows with `outside` above 0 are printed. It is a report, no failure: the exit code and what `--apply` writes do not change, and a female of a scored species can sit a little under the minimum.

### The ammunition rule on stored kills

`admin ethics [--account <id or handle>] [--apply]` (command line only, `State.JudgeKills`) judges every stored kill (every account, or one) again by today's rule, as its kill page judges it, and makes the stored kill what a new one would be. A dry run unless `--apply`. Its start-up takes the short write transactions every admin command takes (the schema check and each account's hunterscore); the judging then only reads, with no lock. `--apply` judges each kill that changes again inside its own short transaction, reading its mount and its payment again there, and writes it there, so a live heartbeat waits for one kill at most: it may run while players hunt (a kill harvested meanwhile is judged by the same rule at its harvest). The gm$ paid and the lodge mounts are read once per account for the listing. It prints one JSON line per kill that changes or is reported, in account and kill order, `{"account","kill","species","change","flag","reason","ammo","basis","coat","score","board","boardRows","top10","taxidermied","lodge","slot","photo","gms","ifNormal","ifPossessed"}`, then a total line `{"changes","flags","reasons","lostScore","boards","boardRows","top10","unscored","gmsKept","gmsPaid","applied"}` (`boards`, `boardRows` and `top10` count the disqualified kills holding a leaderboard row, their rows and their top-10 places) and the dry-run or applied line:

- `change` `disqualify`: the rule judges the kill unethical and it has a score. Its stored page gets the score and score type of a disqualified harvest (0 and `""`), so it leaves the boards and the personal bests and is not counted as ethical; `board` is true when it holds a leaderboard row now (`boardRows` of them over every board and season, `top10` of them in a top 10; another kill of the same hunter may take its place); `reason` is `silver_on_normal` (a normal animal hit with silver), `not_silver_on_possessed` (a possessed animal hit with another ammunition) or `not_permitted` (an ammunition outside its list), `ammo` the item id of its first hit outside the list, `basis` how its kind was decided (`coat`, `species` or `hunt`), `score` the score it loses. Nothing else is taken back: `gms` (the gm$ its kill was paid) stays, no wallet row is written, removed or changed, and its trophy shot (`photo`), taxidermy and lodge mount stay.
- `change` `unscored`: a kill harvested before the schema-41 step (`schema_marks`) that the rule judges unethical, that has no score to lose (a species or a sex that scores nothing), and whose hits the rule it was judged by when it was made permitted: in a hunt whose first connect recorded the event (since `server-1.47.0`, not set by the step), the event's silver for a species its list names; otherwise its species' list and an event's silver. A kill made after the deploy was judged by this rule when it was made and is never listed. Nothing is written for it (a `kept` flag when it keeps a mount); it is listed so every run, dry or applied, holds every kill the change turned unethical, and so it is listed again on every run while a second `--apply` writes nothing. A kill without a score that would score was disqualified (or refused) at its harvest already and is not listed.
- `change` `requalify`: the rule judges the kill ethical and it has no score (a coyote or blacktail deer killed with its normal ammunition in an event hunt, disqualified by `server-1.47.0`, for one): it is scored and paid as `admin rescore` pays a missed harvest (`score` the new score, `gms` what is paid now; nothing when a `CSS Reward` row of the kill exists).
- `flag` `kept`: an unethical kill that keeps a taxidermy (`taxidermied`) or a lodge mount (`lodge`, the `lodges.n`, and `slot`, the slot's `slot_id`), which a disqualified kill could not get now: one it disqualifies (`change` `disqualify`), or one already without a score (`change` `flag`), a kill disqualified at its harvest included (one taxidermied before the taxidermy refused disqualified kills). When the mount moves, the flag is written again with the new lodge and slot (`change` `flag`).
- `flag` `undecided` (`change` `flag`, `reason` and `basis` `undecided`): a kill of a hunt the schema-41 step left undecided (`expeditions.event_undecided`) whose coat proves nothing: it keeps the verdict it was made under (its species' list and the event's silver), on every path, score and pay included; it is flagged when the rule could judge it otherwise (`ifNormal`, `ifPossessed`: the verdict as a normal and as a possessed animal, `ethical` or `unethical`): a hare in coat 73 or a European rabbit hit with silver, with normal ammunition, or with both. `score` is the score it keeps. A kill both kinds judge as it was judged gets no flag.
- `change` `unflag`: a flag whose cause is gone (the mount taken down, the kill judged ethical) is removed.

Not flagged: a disqualified kill that keeps nothing, old or new. A new kill is never flagged at its harvest. The operator compares the dry run's `disqualify` and `unscored` lines with a reference list before `--apply`; the output depends only on the database, so two dry runs print the same lines. A running server shows the new boards within 15 minutes, or at its restart.

`admin ethics list [--account <id or handle>] [--kind kept|undecided] [--reason <reason>] [--species <id>] [--since <ISO date>]` prints the flags, one JSON line each (`{"account","kill","kind","species","coat","reason","ammo","basis","ifNormal","ifPossessed","taxidermied","lodge","slot","lostScore","flagged"}`, `flagged` the time it was flagged) and a total line `{"total","kinds","reasons"}`; it writes nothing. The table can be read directly too, e.g. `sqlite3 <data_dir>/state.db "SELECT account_id, kill_id, reason, lodge, slot, lost_score FROM unethical_kills WHERE kind = 'kept' ORDER BY account_id, kill_id"`. No player sees a flag: the pages show a disqualified kill as such already.

## Tests

`dotnet test --project server/Server.Tests -c Release`: xUnit v3, no network and nothing outside a temporary folder. Fixtures are built in code. CI runs them on `ubuntu-latest` only, in the `server-ubuntu` job ([CONTRIBUTING.md](../CONTRIBUTING.md#ci-and-updates)). `EndToEndTests` plays the whole journey over HTTP: a made-up backup is uploaded and approved, the account launches, connects, harvests an animal and disconnects, and the new kill is read in the hunterscore, the statistics, the trophies and the hunt history by the player and by a second account (the second account reads the trophies on the website and, in the game, in the lodge the harvest is mounted in).

The test classes run in parallel (xUnit's default: one test collection per class); each test has its own temporary folder, database files and server on an ephemeral port. What the server keeps per process is pinned or kept apart: `Scoring`'s config (`StaticHost`, `OwnLogo`, `Variants`, set by `Scoring.Load` at start) is pinned once for the run in `Fixtures.PinScoring`, which the parallel tests only read; a test that changes it, or sets an environment variable, the current directory or `Console.Out`, is in the `ProcessState` collection (`[Collection("ProcessState")]`), which xUnit runs alone after all the parallel classes. A test that touches process-wide state goes there.
